# Eroticization Operator

**Why does a person feel ashamed of something?**

Three national surveys (GSS 1972–2024 · NSFG · Open Psychometrics), the ethnographic coding of 186 societies
(SCCS), and a 68-category sexual-preference matrix.
**100 rounds · 635 scripts · 661 ledger entries · three units: person · decade · society.**
What follows is the question being chased down — **and every answer that failed is written where it failed.**

---

## 1. The easy answer: because it is rare

The first dataset gives `corr(rarity, shame) = 0.758` (Entry 528). Case closed, apparently.

**But a correlation does not tell you which side is upstream.** Could rarity and shame both be shadows of a
**third thing — what the society around them does with it?** Answering that needs a bigger unit than the
person: the **society**.

## 2. So: 186 societies. And the law broke.

The Standard Cross-Cultural Sample codes both **attitude** and **frequency** for three sexual practices.
Rank-correlating condemnation against rarity: **only one of five cells holds** (premarital +0.80 / +0.63;
extramarital and homosexuality fall inside their own resolution — Entry 528).

> **So "rarer means more condemned" is not a law about *practices*. It is a fact about one practice.**

## 3. Is it a fact about *societies*, then? — Also no.

If harshness were a property of a **society**, a society strict about one sexual practice should be strict
about others. The strongest available pair (premarital × extramarital, n = 90) gives **+0.086**; the median
of the full eight-cell grid is **+0.125** — and the right comparison is not zero but **how correlated any two
variables coded by the same team already are: 0.187 ± 0.152** (Entry 529 · 530).

> **Indistinguishable. In this data there is no such thing as a "strict society".**

## 4. Wait — maybe I was asking the wrong unit

All three steps above **varied the practice**. What if the practice is **held fixed** and only **who bears it**
changes? One coding team recorded corporal punishment for four targets (early/late childhood, boys/girls;
139 societies carry all four):

- **Change who is hit: median of six pairs +0.845.** Barely moves.
- **Change which method** (hitting / lecturing / teaching / permissiveness / affection): **+0.229.** Three
  quarters gone.
- **Cross to wife-beating: upper bound only +0.440.** (Entry 640 · 641 · 642)

> **Harshness is not a property of a society, nor of a broad class like "violence" — it is a property of one
> specific practice.**

⚠ The second line could have been false: perhaps the coder simply gave one society similar marks down every
child-rearing column. **I deliberately gave that halo world a chance to win — it lost** (changing the method
gives +0.229 against +0.845 for changing the target — Entry 642).

## 5. Does the same shape hold in people?

It does, and more sharply. **Of five moral domains, only purity has all six item pairs above 0.30**
(weakest link **0.335**); each of the other four contains a pair far lower (fairness is
**0.062** — Entry 653).
⚠ **But "only purity" was manufactured by that chosen 0.30, and it is withdrawn**: against a null built from
**all 593,770 random six-item blocks** of the same questionnaire, the 95th percentile is **0.0299** —
**all five domains clear it**, purity **11.23×** > authority 5.68× > ingroup 4.99× > harm 4.65× > fairness 2.08×
(Entry 716).

> **All five moral domains cohere more than a random six. Purity is the tightest — twice the next —
> not the only one.** (Entry 653 · 716)
> ⚠ At the null's **99th** percentile of 0.2019, **only purity does clear it.**
> **Both cuts are reported: the verdict depends on where the cut falls, so what is load-bearing is the
> ordering, not either binary.**
> ⚠ And the null's **maximum is +0.4501**, above purity's own 0.3354 — a random six-item block **can** be
> tighter than it, **so this is a distributional statement, never any single comparison** `[#716「五个域全都比随机凑的六题更连成一片,「只有纯洁」是那把尺子造的」]`.
> ⚠ **And whether it is one structure for men and for women was measured separately.** The twenty facets' **ordering** agrees across sexes at ρ = **+0.952**;
> the within-sex random split-half ceiling is **+0.975** (ten splits, [+0.965, +0.980]) and the facet-label-shuffled null is **0.453**.
> **By the letter, 0.952 is below the ceiling ⇒ the structure differs. And the placebo written before the run says that criterion's reference class was wrong:**
> **splitting by age instead of sex gives only +0.958, also below that ceiling** — any **non-exchangeable** split loses this much,
> while the two halves of a split-half are **exchangeable**. **I compared a between-group quantity against a within-group ceiling.**
> ⇒ What stands: **the ordering is one structure (sexes 0.952 against age 0.958, not separable); and in 7 of the 20 facets the internal coherence differs by sex** —
> tighter in women for *I am very aware of my sexual feelings and needs*, *I'm motivated to be sexually active*, *I notice how others perceive it*;
> tighter in men for *if I had a sexual problem I would be in control of whether it improved*, *mostly determined by chance*, *I am depressed about it*, *I avoid risky sexual behaviour*.
> ⚠ **This is a difference in coherence, not in level** — it says how tightly that family of items hangs together in him or her,
> **not** who scores higher on it. Those are entirely different sentences `[#727「组间的量不能拿组内的天花板去比,而安慰剂就是为这件事写的」]`.
> ⚠ **Those seven were then put through multiplicity, and the verdict is UNVERIFIED.** Under **BH over 20 (q = 0.05)** against a **sex-label permutation** null (B = 2000, resolution 0.0005): **seven still survive, and the set changed by one** — *I notice how others perceive and react to the sexual aspects of my life* drops out (p = 0.042) and *I have the ability to take care of any sexual needs I may have* comes in (p = 0.0075). ⚠ The one that dropped is exactly the facet `#720` found sitting on its own null at 1.02× — **the same facet fails twice in two unrelated tests.** ⚠⚠ **And the verdict on the whole section is UNVERIFIED, not confirmed**: the sex-label permutation null's 95th percentile is **0.0281** against a median \|Δ\| of **0.0411** across the seven — **the null is 68% of the effect.** **Per-facet BH passes and aggregate strength does not; both have to be said.** What stands is still the **shared ordering** (2.10×); **the seven coherence differences are a 1.46× lead, not a conclusion** `[#728「判词只比了个数没比集合,而集合换了一个面」]`.

**And "sex is one block" holds on two entirely different instruments** (GSS four items, weakest link 0.416;
NSFG three items, 0.346 — different wordings, populations, years). Conversely, **the seven "family morality"
items are not a domain**: they are **four items about whether the unmarried count as a family, plus three
that go their own way** — one of which is not a moral judgement at all but *marriage has not worked out for
most people I know* (Entry 650).

## 6. One last question, and it nearly destroyed all of the above

**How much of these answers is made by the way the question is asked?**

This survey moved to web administration in 2021, and in 2022 and 2024 **both modes ran in the same year** —
so it can be measured directly:

- face-to-face **0.7051** against web **0.5951** (2022) · **0.7917** against **0.6057** (2024)
- **the same contrast on the death penalty: +0.018 and −0.012 — essentially nothing.** (Entry 660)

**It looked specific to sexual items.**

⚠ **Then I took it apart myself.** Redone across 19 moral items, the first pass was clean: sexual median
+0.0735, non-sexual −0.0074. **But code 1 does not mean the same thing across items** — for *premarital sex
is always wrong* it is the **harsh** answer, for *allow the incurably ill to die* it is the **permissive**
one. **A signed median let the non-sexual group cancel itself.** On absolute values the non-sexual median is
**+0.0378**, not −0.0074, the gap falls to **+0.0357**, and **its interval contains zero** (Entry 661).

> **Non-sexual moral items move with the mode too — they simply move in different directions. "Specific to
> sexual items" was manufactured by sign cancellation.**

**What survives looks like a shape about direction rather than size** — and the next round killed that too.
Correctly oriented, the sexual items are not twelve of twelve but **10/12**; resampling the non-sexual
group to the same cell count gives a median agreement rate of 0.750, putting the sexual group at the
**76.6th percentile, empirical p = 0.234**; and randomly relabelling sexual/non-sexual produces a gap
(**0.106**) **larger than the real split's (0.083)** (Entry 662).

> **At this cell count the agreement rate cannot tell a real grouping from a random one.**
> **Those 11–19 points are real; whether they are specific to sexual items is not answerable here.**

⚠ And that "12/12" was itself computed on **unoriented** differences — **using the very defect the
paragraph above it had just diagnosed. I named the defect, then handed the next round a number that
still carried it.**

---

## What stands
**⟨…⟩** = **the effect divided by its own null's 95th percentile** (`⟨—⟩` where it cannot be computed: 8 entries whose round persisted no results, 10 with output but no extractable effect–null pair).
⚠⚠ **And every normalised number on this page is an under-estimate — measured in a synthetic world, not assumed.** With the true correlation, the noise and the marginal skew all known, on four categories (as in GSS), 27 cells, 200 replicates each: **raw −28.5% · ceiling-normalised only −22.2% · disattenuated only −16.6% · both composed −9.5%** — **all four under-estimate, the composition is closest, and the two improvements are close to additive, so they are not correcting the same thing and may be composed** (Entry 736). ⚠ **And even composed it still under-estimates by 9.5%, because four-category discretisation itself attenuates by 12–13% and none of the four estimators repairs that.** ⇒ **Every normalised number on this page should be read as a lower bound, not a point estimate** `[#736「四种校正全都低估,而页上的数一直是保守的」]`.
⚠ **But the *magnitudes* in that simulation do not transfer to this page — only the direction does**: the synthetic ceilings are **0.95–0.99**, while **GSS's six ceilings run 0.4770–0.8515 (median 0.7335)** — **in real data the ceiling is set by marginal skew, not by category count**, so normalisation does far more work here than the simulation suggested. The pre-registered mechanism check passed (mixed 4×5 ceiling 0.9544 < same-category 0.9914, normalisation contributing +0.036 against +0.007): **the mechanism is right, the regime is not** `[#737「合成世界的天花板是 0.95,GSS 的是 0.48–0.85,所以只有方向可搬」]`.
⚠⚠ **And there is something heavier than wobble: the denominator is a *same-pool* random block, and the pool is one I chose.** Same people, same GSS sexual four,
only the companion battery changes, and the ratio runs from **0.93×** (against the abortion four) to **4.56×** (against the police four) — **max/min = 4.90, with the numerator identical to the digit**.
⇒ **Every ⟨ratio⟩ is relative to its pool. Ratios are comparable within one instrument and one pool; across pools they are not, and across instruments still less** (Entry 731).
⚠ **That denominator is itself an estimate, so how much it wobbles has been measured**: of the **28 rows carrying ⟨…⟩, 25 never declare whether the denominator was enumerated or sampled**, and `#716` once caught one moving by a factor of 1.9. Measured, **only one kind bites**: at the same B = 4,000, a **permutation null's relative SD is 1.61%, while subsampling a structured block space is 10.73% — 6.7× worse**, and the combinatorial arm is **not reliably improved by drawing more** (the weakest-link distribution is heavily tied, so its 95th percentile hops between plateaus). ⇒ **every cell whose block space is enumerable has been switched to full enumeration** (MFQ k=6 · GSS k=4 · NSFG k=3/k7 · SCCS k=4); **for the remaining permutation-null rows the denominator wobbles at the ~2% level, which changes no ratio's reading** `[#719「分母自己的抖动量出来了,而咬人的只有一类」]`. ⚠ **Carrying that instrument discipline back to the object, the third instrument gives not a replication but an adjacent answer.** This page says a person has *a* sexual morality. What about a person's sexual **self-concept**? **MSSCQ** (100 items · **n = 12,789 complete cases**, 17,685 rows released · five-point *how like me*) ⚠ **And that n was wrong until now**: page and ledger both read 17,685, which is the `wc -l` **row count of the release**, while **4,896 rows leave at least one item unanswered**. **The scripts were always right** — the persisted artifact stored 12,789 — **what was wrong is that I re-quoted it from a different source**, and the two kinds of number look identical inside a sentence. Every statistic was computed on 12,789, so **the verdicts and the ratios do not move; what moves is the scope, and scope is half of `G1`** `[#726「我引的是文件行数,不是测量」]`。 asks for self-description — sexual anxiety, esteem, control, motivation. ⚠ **First, what it cannot do**: the four GSS items are **moral judgements about other people's behaviour**, MSSCQ is **self-description**, **not the same construct ⇒ this is not `#653`②'s third replication point, and *sex is one block* still has only the GSS leg.** ⚠ **And the honest answer to *who grouped the facets* is: nobody did, in this release.** The codebook lists 100 stems and not one facet name; the 20 × 5 cyclic structure is **something I read off the item texts** (item 3 *I am very aware of my sexual feelings and needs* against item 23 *I am very aware of my sexual motivations and desires*) — so it is not a premise but a thing to be tested, **and it passed**: median within-facet agreement **0.5645**, reproducing `#542`'s 0.579 within ±0.05. **The result: all twenty facets clear their own null, median +0.4809 = 2.62× it; while five items drawn at random from the same questionnaire sit at −0.0235, and the median cross-facet pair is only 0.0725.** ⇒ **A person's sexuality is not one thing. It is twenty separately solid things that do not imply one another** — whether he is anxious about sex, whether he feels in control of it, and how much he wants it are three things free to move apart. ⚠ **The single facet sitting on its null is the tenth** — *I notice how others perceive and react to the sexual aspects of my life* (1.02×), **not one thing, and the only facet that brings other people into it.** ⚠ Two notes belong beside this: ① **three facets are negative without sign alignment** (17, 7, 8) and all turn positive under optimal signs — `#718`'s lesson reproducing intact on a brand-new instrument, and my first pass again forgot the step I had built two rounds earlier; ② **the median cross-facet pair, 0.0725, does not match the 0.173 recorded in `#542`** — ② **the median cross-facet pair, 0.0725, does not match the 0.173 recorded in `#542`** — the next round closed that gap, and closing it turned up two heavier things `[#720「一个人的性不是一件事,是二十件互不蕴含的事」]`. ⚠ **The whole difference is whether absolute values are taken**: `#542` reported a median of `|ρ|` (raw), and the four-cell comparison gives **raw `|ρ|` = 0.5856 within / 0.1734 across**, matching its 0.5789 / 0.1726 cell for cell, with pair counts 200 / 4,750 identical to the word. **And 1,786 of the 4,750 cross-facet pairs (38%) are negative** ⇒ by the rule `#607` already established, **a median of absolute values over correlations whose signs are essentially random measures how big *nothing* is at this sample size** — so 0.1734 is an upper bound and **the signed 0.0725 is the readable one**. ⚠ **The second thing exposed while closing it is that my own positive control passed by luck**: I compared a *median of per-facet medians* (0.5645) against `#542`'s *pooled median over 200 pairs* (0.5789) — **two different statistics** that merely both landed inside ±0.05. Recomputed like for like it is **0.5856 against 0.5789, a gap of 0.0067** — **the conclusion is unchanged, and until now it held by luck.** This is the mirror of `#718`: there a failing positive control caught me comparing mismatched numbers; **here a passing one concealed the same thing.** ⚠ **The third must be inherited: `#542` was already downgraded to a VERIFICATION by `P14`'s `prior_art` line** — same-facet items are often near-synonymous rewordings (*I am very aware of my sexual feelings and needs* against *…motivations and desires* = 0.666), and **a result restating how the instrument was built is a verification, not a finding**. ⇒ **`#720`'s within-facet half inherits that downgrade verbatim, and the page row now says so. The cross-facet half does not inherit it** — an author designing twenty distinct facets does not guarantee people answer them independently; **had there been one overarching *sexual wellbeing* factor, all twenty would have correlated together at 0.6. They do not. That is about people, not about the questionnaire** `[#721「未决关掉了,而它带出我那个蒙对的正对照和一条早该继承的降级」]`. ⚠ **And this page's oldest education claim does not move under the new tools — for a structural reason, not by luck.** `#676` says education pushes both item groups toward tolerance, and pushes gender roles harder, yet only for the sexual group do the four judgements become bound to one another — marginal-matched Δ **+0.1604** against a null 95th percentile of **0.0372**, **4.31×**. Recomputed with `#718`'s optimal signs: **sexual four Δ greedy +0.1837 / optimal +0.1837; gender-role three +0.0221 / +0.0221 — identical to the digit.** The reason is not *I checked and found no difference* but **all six pairwise correlations are positive, so the greedy assignment already is the optimum and `#718`'s mechanism is structurally impossible here**. **The first is a noisy observation; the second is a sentence that can be cited.** ⚠ And writing this I again pushed detail into a table row and tripped the gate — **`#721` established that rows are summaries and detail belongs in the narrative, and two rounds later I broke it myself**; this paragraph is that detail moved back where it belongs `[#723「`#676` 在新工具下不动,而这一条是 CLOSURE,不许写成 FRONTIER」]`. ⚠ **And a hole this page has carried for a long time is now filled by the same tool.** `#617` says a society does not decide whether violence is right, it decides to whom; `#618` recorded that **the same question could not be measured for sex** — none of three search versions passed its own positive control, and the reason was that **the *to whom* lives between variables, not inside any one of them**. `#717` had just shown how to use exactly that structure. ⚠ **The family recovered is `barry1976traits`'s *ten traits × four targets*, and sexual restraint is one of the ten** — same team, same societies, same coding, with `Self-restraint` as the neighbour control: the same thing minus the sex. **All 91,380 same-pool same-k blocks enumerated as the null (q95 0.1537); all ten traits clear the null's median.** **And sexual restraint's weakest link is +0.6838, ranking 3rd of ten (competitiveness 0.7575 · obedience 0.6885 · sexual restraint 0.6838 · aggression 0.6437 … responsibility last at 0.2709) — near the most uniform end, not the exception.** The world I had bet on — that sex is the one trait graded by target — **is refuted.** ⚠ **And the cross-team arm only makes sense read alongside it.** Whyte 1978 (**a different team**) coded directly whether there is a double standard for premarital and extramarital sex: where there **is** one, the median boy–girl gap in sexual restraint is **0.750**; where there is **none**, it is **0.000**. **The two do not conflict; together they are the answer: a weakest link is a rank correlation — it sees ordering and is blind to level.** ⇒ **What a society varies about sex is the level, not the ordering: it is stricter with girls than with boys, and a strict society is strict with both. The double standard is an offset, not a seam.** ⚠ And that cross-team arm has an arm of only 22–28 societies, **below the floor of 30 written before the run**, so per that pre-registration it is **observation only and stays out of the verdict** — the ruling half of the sentence above rests on the ranking, not on the 0.750 `[#724「双重标准是一个偏移量,不是一条接缝」]`. ⚠⚠ **The society side was later asked a more basic question: is a society's strictness about sex merely a facet of how strict it is in general?** Correlating each of `barry1976traits`'s **ten inculcated traits** with another team's (`broude1976cross`) coding of **attitudes to female premarital sex**: **sexual restraint reaches +0.6558, rank 1**; the runner-up, **obedience, only +0.3030**, and all nine non-sexual traits fall between **−0.10 and +0.30**. The loose null (shuffling the adult item across societies) has a 95th percentile of **0.1779**, and the **MDE was computed before the run (n≈112 ⇒ 0.19)**. Swapping the composite from a mean to a weakest link leaves the ordering intact: sexual restraint +0.5695 against obedience +0.2715. ⇒ **A society's strictness about sex is not a facet of how strict it is in general.** ⚠ **And the sentence has to be read in two halves: the convergent half — two unrelated teams reading the same ethnographies give the same answer about how strict this society is on sex — is a verification, not a discovery (`#542` was downgraded by the `prior_art` rule for exactly this shape). The discovery is the discriminant half: obedience, industry and self-restraint, the *general* strictness, cap out at 0.30.** ⚠ And this round's **halo null did not act**: permuting the four target values within a society moves the composite (a four-target z-mean) by only **9.85% of its own SD** — **it is a no-op control, and its giving ≈ the observed value on every row is the tell. It must not be counted as a control that passed.** `[#749「一个社会对性的严厉,不是它一般有多严的一个侧面」]`. ⚠⚠ **The next round put that sentence to a stronger standard: not a ranking, but explicitly removing general strictness.** Partialling out each of the nine non-sexual traits in turn leaves **+0.5618 to +0.6809** between sexual restraint and `SCCS165` (each specification's own MDE being only 0.181–0.203); collapsing the nine into a principal component and removing it leaves **+0.5666 with n down to 59** (MDE 0.253); **removing all nine simultaneously leaves +0.5504** (MDE 0.276). The nine non-sexual traits' own bivariate ceiling is **+0.3030** ⇒ **the partialled sexual-restraint link is 1.85× it.** ⇒ **Strip out how strict a society is in general and +0.55 remains between teaching children sexual restraint and condemning adult female premarital sex — sex has its own line at the level of the society.** ⚠ Three things written before the run were honoured: ① the **collinearity pre-check**, corr(PC1, sexual restraint) = −0.3746, **not collinear, so partialling is meaningful** (above 0.8 it would have been undecidable); ② **the MDE chose the primary specification** — requiring all nine cuts n from 121 to 59 and lifts the MDE from 0.19 to 0.25, **so the primary partials out only the strongest single trait**; ③ **no halo null**, because `#749` measured it to be a no-op for this kind of composite. ⚠ And the convergent half remains a **verification** (`#749` labelled it); **what this round strengthens is the discriminant half** `[#750「把一般的严整个扣掉,性还剩 +0.55」]`. ⚠⚠ **Two same-direction rounds make a basin, so the next step was deliberately one whose positive outcome I would not welcome: does the same sentence hold at the level of the person?** The only non-sexual moral items GSS asks on the same wrongness scale as the four sexual ones are two — cheating on taxes and lying for benefits — **and hard rule ① measured that they were asked in 1991 and 1998 only, overlapping at n=734**. The result: **ρ(sexual strictness, how wrong cheating the state is) = +0.1317, disattenuated +0.1910**; **removing the latter entirely leaves the four sexual items 98.0% as coherent, and 97.2% even assuming the control is perfectly reliable**. ⚠ The thing most likely to fool me here: **at the society level I removed nine traits, at the person level only two items, and a thinner control makes "sex survives" easier to obtain** — the two items' reliability is 0.7031, and **partialling out an unreliable control necessarily under-corrects, in the direction of the conclusion I wanted**, so the verdict was placed on the "if it were perfectly reliable" side. ⚠⚠ And **the raw output was −0.1317**: the two batteries are coded in opposite directions (high = permissive for the sexual items, high = strict for the others), which only reading the value labels revealed — **the fourth instance of the sign family**, the previous three all caught by reading code. ⚠ **The claim's reach is narrower than it sounds**: what the person level removed is dishonesty toward the state, not general morality, **so what this supports is "sex and thinking it wrong to cheat the state are two lines", not "sex and general morality are two lines".** ⇒ **Both units give the same direction through completely different magnitudes (+0.6558 vs ≤+0.3030 at the society level; +0.13 against 97% retention at the person level) — exactly the shape `#737` established: direction transfers, magnitude does not.** `[#751「一个人对性的严,和他觉得逃税骗福利有多错,只共享 +0.13」]`. ⚠⚠ **And that sentence was narrowed inside the same round, by the gate rather than by me.** `readme_gate`'s `single_instrument` rule blocked a round standing on one instrument — the exact weakness I had just written into its own NEXT. On a second person-level instrument (MFQ, n=7267): **ρ(sex, general morality) = +0.3161, and partialling it out leaves only 59.5%** — **the two instruments disagree.** Do not average; find the assumption they differ on: **GSS's two items are tax and benefit fraud, which are harm-and-fairness content, while MFQ's composite also contains ingroup and authority.** Taken apart: **ρ(chastity, harm) = +0.0060 · (fairness) = −0.1685 · (ingroup) = +0.4194 · (authority) = +0.5001**; **partialling out harm+fairness leaves sex at 94.9%, agreeing with GSS's 97.2%; partialling out ingroup+authority leaves 60.6%.** ⇒ **How strict a person is about sex has almost nothing to do with whether they think harming people or being unfair is wrong, and a great deal to do with whether they think one should obey authority and be loyal to one's own.** ⚠⚠ **So the decomposition running under these three rounds was itself wrong**: "sexual morality versus general morality, one thing or two" has no answer, **because general morality is not one thing** — sex is independent of the harm-and-fairness family and is part of the authority-and-ingroup one. **Asking whether sex is its own line requires first naming who stands on the other side of it.** ⚠ And the two units agreed independently: at the society level the one trait approaching sexual restraint was **obedience, +0.3030**; at the person level the strongest of the four foundations is **authority, +0.5001** — **which I did not go looking for; the disagreement forced it out.** ⚠ **prior_art: that purity clusters with authority and ingroup as binding foundations is MFQ's own central published claim, so most of this arm is verification rather than discovery.** What is not prior work: the GSS arm itself, the fact that the instruments' disagreement was a dispute about a definition, and the correspondence across the two units.`[#752「性独立于伤害与公平,却就是权威那一族的一部分」]`. ⚠⚠ **And that sentence was narrowed one round later, by its own implication.** If sex belongs to an authority-and-ingroup **family** at the person level, then of the nine child-rearing traits `#750` partialled out at the society level **only obedience belongs to that family** — which can simply be recomputed. ⚠ The strongest confound was real: **I had already seen `#750`'s nine-cell grid, so any family I drew myself was fitted to it**; the defences were both external — Barry/Child/Bacon's published 1957–59 dichotomy, sixty years older than my seeing the grid, and a factor split that **never looked at the outcome variable**. **The blind split rejected the premise outright**: eight of the nine load on PC1 with the same sign, only aggression opposing — an 8-against-1 split is not a family split. And the costs, taken apart: **obedience alone +0.0783, responsibility alone +0.0046, the whole compliance pair +0.0750 — adding responsibility makes the cost smaller.** ⇒ **At the society level there is no obedience family, only obedience; and of the +0.0898 that removing all nine cost in `#750`, +0.0783 is obedience by itself.** ⚠ What was narrowed is not the previous round's measurements, which stand, but **my reading of them**: what matches across the two units is **one trait against one foundation** (obedience +0.3030 ↔ authority +0.5001), **not one family against another** — the person level's ingroup +0.4194 has no counterpart that was ever measured at the society level. **I had written a single match up into a structure.**`[#753「社会层没有服从那一族,只有服从这一个」]`. ⚠ **The same knife has to cut the person-level family too, or I am simply favouring one unit.** Ask the cheapest question first: **ρ(authority, ingroup) = +0.7005** — they are already mostly one thing, so "both correlations are high" may be one thing rather than two. Cut them apart: **partialling out ingroup leaves chastity×authority at 63.6% (14.0× its own null), while partialling out authority leaves chastity×ingroup at 26.5% (still 4.9× its own null, so not zero).** ⚠ **Neither pre-registered threshold was met, and the verdict was honoured as W-3 rather than re-run with adjusted ones** — a threshold chosen after seeing the result is a narrative. **What could not be decided was the 75% I picked, not the world**: both partials sit far above their own nulls, so whether there is anything there is clear, and whether it qualifies as two things is something I failed to define. ⇒ Taking both arms together: **the society level is "obedience alone", the person level is "authority mostly, with a small ingroup remainder" — not contradictory, but not the same structure either.** ⚠ And the two families are near-orthogonal (ρ(authority, harm) = −0.0549 · ρ(ingroup, fairness) = −0.1407), which both units support.`[#754「人层那个族大半是一件事,而判不了的是我的阈值不是世界」]`. ⚠⚠ **Everything at the person level so far rests on a self-selected sample (MFQ / YourMorals.org), the limitation it had written into its own ledger — so change to a probability sample.** GSS's Kohn child-rearing values battery overlaps the four sexual items pairwise at **n≈16,000**, and offers a rough but matching analogue (`obey`≈authority · `helpoth`≈care · `workhard`≈achievement · `popular`≈ingroup). The result: **`obey` ranks first on all four sexual items**, agreeing across three statistics; on `premarsx` it is **10.1×** its own null, while **`helpoth` is only 0.6×, below the noise floor**. ⚠⚠ But first the instrument's arithmetic: the battery is **strictly ipsative** (each respondent's five items sum to a constant, std = 0.0000000000), so the five covariances with any external quantity sum to exactly zero — **which means "one positive, one negative" is forced by the constraint rather than evidence, and only the ranking is readable.** ⚠⚠ And **the positive control failed the first time because I pointed it at the wrong statistic**: the identity governs raw-value covariances (measured Σ = −1.96e-15, exactly zero), while I had computed Spearman, and ranking each item separately destroys ipsativity (the sum's std goes from 0 to 2620). **The control was sound and I aimed it wrongly — the same family I have been repeating, this time committed inside the control itself; and the script honoured its pre-registration and refused to print the rankings.** ⇒ **Three instruments now point at the same ordering through three different constructs**: at the society level SCCS makes obedience the only trait worth anything (+0.0783, responsibility worth +0.0046); at the person level MFQ gives authority +0.5001 against harm +0.0060; and GSS gives obedience first on 4/4 against helping others at 0.6×. **The care-and-harm end sits near zero on all three.** ⚠ And `thnkself` coming second everywhere is not an independent finding — its inter-item correlation with `obey` is −0.469, so it is largely the first item's mirror.`[#756「一万六千人的概率样本上,管性最强的预测项是要不要孩子服从」]`. ⚠ **If three instruments all say that policing sex and demanding obedience are two sides of one thing, the next question must be direction — and this round's task was not to answer it but to measure whether this data is entitled to ask.** **The person level is structurally unidentifiable**: GSS is a repeated cross-section, not a panel, and each person is asked once, so **there is no individual time ordering at all** — it would require **panel data**, and the cell is recorded as "N/A plus what it would require" per realstat §2, **never as "planned"**. **The decade level does have an identifiable quantity**: `obey` and `premarsx` share 22 years, and after detrending the contemporaneous cross-correlation is **+0.6426** (the highest), with an asymmetry (lag+1 − lag−1) of **−0.1295**. ⚠⚠ **But −0.1295 must not be read as "which one leads"** — its own null's 95th percentile is **0.6462**, so the observation is **0.20×** that, below the noise floor; and injecting lead strengths of 0.1→0.8 gives detection rates of only 0.044→0.551, so **80% power is unreachable anywhere on the grid**. ⚠ Worth stating precisely: the detrended residual sd is 2.53–3.11× the sampling se, so **there genuinely is signal beyond sampling noise in those residuals** — the problem is not that only noise remains, it is that **22 time points cannot resolve a lag asymmetry**. ⇒ **The cell is conceded: this data can say the two move together contemporaneously, and cannot say which comes first.** And that was learned by **computing the MDE first**, rather than by running a weak test and explaining it afterwards.`[#757「方向这个问题这套数据撑不起,而这是先算 MDE 才知道的」]`. ⚠⚠ **One strong rival explanation had still never been tested, and the arc could not proceed past it: a single left–right political axis might produce both the +0.5001 with authority and the −0.1685 with fairness.** If so, "sex is bound to authority" collapses into "sex is bound to conservatism" — something already known. **That is a positive outcome I would not welcome, which is exactly why it had to be tested.** On the GSS probability sample (n≈16,000), partialling out `polviews` entirely leaves **`obey`'s link to the four sexual items at a median 87.7%** (86–90%), with a specification curve of **87.9% (linear) / 87.4% (seven dummies) / 78.6% (plus year dummies)** — all three on the same side. Politics does explain something — **−0.0332, which is 594.5× its own spread, genuinely real** — **but it accounts for 12.1%.** ⇒ **Strip political left–right out entirely and seven-eighths of the link between wanting children to obey and policing other people's sex remains: sex is not bound to conservatism, it is bound to obedience.** ⚠⚠ And **the confound I wrote down before the run was refuted by my own measurement, and is withdrawn here**: I claimed MFQ's politics was range-restricted so that partialling it out would under-correct; measured, MFQ's `politics` has sd **1.555** against GSS's `polviews` sd **1.408** — **wider, not narrower**. The left skew is real (mean 2.87 against ≈4), but that is skew, not range. The verdict still went to GSS as pre-registered, with the reason changed from range to **its being a probability sample**, which was true before the run and after. ⚠ It also resolved the two-instrument disagreement left open above, in the opposite direction to what I expected: **MFQ's "the more you value chastity the less you value fairness", −0.2116, drops to −0.0290 (13.7%) once politics is removed**; GSS's `helpoth` sits near zero before and after. **⇒ With politics gone, sex and care-and-fairness are unrelated rather than in tension — that negative sign was almost entirely political sorting.** ⚠ The two instruments disagree about how much politics explains (GSS 12.1%, MFQ about 30%), and MFQ comes from a politics-interest platform; **the verdict was placed on the probability sample as pre-registered, not averaged.**`[#760「把政治左右整个偏掉,服从与管性还剩八成八」]`. ⚠ **So what is the other nine-tenths? The strongest candidate is religion** — and here too the positive outcome I would not welcome is clear: if religion explained it away, the finding compresses into "sex is bound to piety", which is again already known. On the same rows (n=9,580–10,467), partialling out each in turn: **politics leaves 90.6% · attendance 79.0% · fundamentalism 82.3% · strength of identification 86.2% · all three religion measures together 66.9% · religion plus politics 62.4%**. ⇒ **Of the link between policing other people's sex and wanting children to obey, about a tenth is politics and about a third is religion; strip out every religious and political measure GSS has and five-eighths remains.** ⇒ And within religion, **how often you attend (what you do) explains more than how strongly you identify (what you call yourself) — 21.0% against 13.8%.** ⚠ **The verdict is W-M: 66.9% falls inside the 50%–75% band I pre-registered, so it is undecidable, and I did not change the threshold.** ⚠⚠ **And what most deserves recording here is about me: this is the second consecutive landing inside my own middle band** (the previous was 63.6%). Neither time was the world being vague — **I am picking thresholds as round numbers**, when they should be derived from something measurable: the effect's own null, the MDE, or another known explanator's share (politics' 9.4% here is a ready-made ruler). ⚠ The three religion measures are correlated with one another (ρ(attendance, identification) = −0.5083), so **"all three together" is not three independent explanations**; and all are self-reports, while a partial correlation is not causal adjustment.`[#762「宗教解释三分之一,政治解释一成,而还剩六成二」]`. ⚠⚠ **And a second instrument in the same round reversed the first two shares — while leaving the third alone.** MFQ carries its own religious-attendance measure, so no exemption was available: on it (n=5,251) **religion explains 16.0%, politics 28.7%, and the residual after both is 62.6%** — **0.2 percentage points from GSS's 62.4%.** ⇒ **The two instruments reverse each other on whether religion or politics matters (GSS makes religion 3.5× politics, MFQ makes politics 1.8× religion) and yet land on almost the same number for what neither explains.** Per realstat §2.5 they are not averaged; part of the disagreement is attributable — **MFQ has one religion measure where GSS has three, so its religion share is structurally under-measured** — **but that cannot explain the other half of the reversal**: MFQ's politics share is 3.05× GSS's, politics is a single variable on both sides, and **MFQ comes from YourMorals.org, a politics-interest platform**. ⇒ **What can be said plainly is that the split between religion and politics moves with the population, while the five-eighths neither of them explains does not.** That is stronger than either split — two samples with different constructs and different sampling land on the same residual while reversing which factor dominates. ⚠ In passing, MFQ's chastity×fairness goes from −0.2040 to −0.0342 (16.8%) once politics is removed, matching the 13.7% measured on the same instrument above — **that negative sign is political sorting, and it now replicates across two different subsamples.**`[#763「两具仪器在宗教还是政治上完全相反,却在剩多少上差 0.2 个百分点」]`. ⚠⚠ **And both of those rounds returned "undecidable" — the second consecutive landing inside a band I had picked myself, which deserves its own repair because the world was not being vague.** The old form was "does retention clear 75% or fall under 50%", and that line was a round number I chose. The new form: **a verdict compares only measured quantities, each against its own null.** Backtested per `#623` against the two rounds that motivated it, computed from the persisted result files — **both go from undecidable to decidable, with more informative conclusions**: in `#754` authority is **14.0×** its own null and ingroup **4.9×**, so **both are real, one simply larger** (2.9× the other); in `#762` religion is **3.50×** politics, while **the residual is 1.89× religion and 6.61× politics**. ⇒ **The largest single "explanation" of the link between policing other people's sex and wanting children to obey is something we have not measured — it is 6.6× politics and 1.9× religion.** ⚠⚠ **One line has to be stated flatly: this is not relaxing a threshold after the fact.** Both old verdicts remain correct as answers to "does retention clear 75%"; **what is retired is the form of the question** — when a question needs me to pick a line before it can be answered, part of its answer is the line I picked, and that part is not about the world. So they are marked RETIRED-AS-BADLY-POSED, **not OVERTURNED**. ⚠ And the new form decides not by being more permissive: it replaces "does it clear my line" with "among these measured quantities, which is larger, and how far is each from its own null" — **which requires me to pick no number at all.**`[#764「两次落进自己拍的中间带,而问题不在阈值高低,在问法」]`. ⚠⚠ **And the residual just promoted to the lead was bitten twice in the next round, the second time through.** **The first was my own filter**: `.where(x>0)` was a blanket missing-code guard I added, while GSS's `.dta` already sets DK/NA to missing — so **`attend` code 0 = never (n=14,883, the largest single category) was deleted wholesale**, and that is precisely the reference group for a religion analysis. Fixing it raises n from 9,580 to 12,151, moving religion 33.1%→33.5% and the residual 62.4%→**61.0%** — **a real defect with a small consequence, and the claim stands under it.** ⚠ The exposure was measured and confined: 13 scripts repo-wide use that filter, and only that one touched a variable with a valid zero. **The second bit through: the controls' own reliability.** Religion α = **0.7287** (⚠ first written as 0.4804, measured on the pre-fix sample), politics Spearman-Brown = **0.5965** — **partialling out an unreliable control necessarily under-corrects, and what under-correction leaves behind gets recorded by me as "residual".** Dividing the control constructs' correlations by √reliability and recomputing the partial: **religion 33.3%→47.1%, residual 63.3%→49.0%, and residual/religion falls from 1.90× to 1.04×.** ⇒ **The previous row's "the residual is 1.89× religion" is retracted** — corrected, the two are the same size. ⚠ **And the honest form is an interval, not another point**: α under-estimates reliability for heterogeneous scales, so the correction overshoots, **and it overshoots in exactly the direction that shrinks the residual**, giving **religion [33.3%, 47.1%], residual [49.0%, 63.3%], residual/religion [1.04×, 1.90×]** — **the interval contains 1, so "much larger than religion" is unsupported, while "the residual is still the largest single component" holds at both ends, as does "the residual is 4.24–9.3× politics".** ⚠ An internal consistency check I had not designed for came out right here: the political composite (`polviews`+`partyid`) explains only 6.8% raw, **less than `polviews` alone at 11.5%** — averaging a good indicator with a worse one adds noise — **and the reliability correction returns it to 11.6%, almost exactly the single indicator's value.**`[#765「我的过滤器删掉了参照组,而真正咬人的是控制量的信度」]`. ⚠ **That interval's upper end rested on Cronbach's α, which is unbiased only when all items load equally — on a heterogeneous scale it under-estimates reliability, so my correction overshot.** Switching to McDonald's ω, which assumes no such thing (a three-item one-factor model is exactly identified, with closed-form loadings, λ² = [0.476, 0.717, 0.276], all inside (0,1)): **ω = 0.7352 > α = 0.7287**, tightening the interval to **religion [33.3%, 46.7%], residual [49.4%, 63.3%], residual/religion [1.06×, 1.90×]** — **still containing 1, so the retraction above stands.** ⚠ Politics has only two items, so **ω is structurally unidentifiable** there and only Spearman-Brown is available — recorded as N/A plus what it would require (a third political indicator), never as "planned". ⚠⚠ **And what this round actually found is about the ledger: the α = 0.4804 written above was copied from the run made before the filter was fixed, while the persisted artifact says 0.7287.** The shares are unaffected (the script used 0.7287 internally); **only the number I re-quoted from another source was wrong** — the same family as "I quoted the file's line count, not the measurement", for the second time. ⚠⚠ **And that mis-quote measured something more valuable in reverse**: the same religion scale gives **α = 0.4804 on the truncated sample and 0.7287 on the full one** — **the filter defect did not merely move the shares, it cut the scale's internal consistency too**, so "a real defect with a small consequence" above **holds for the shares and not for the reliability.** ⚠ And one accident is not a regularity, so it was re-tested on a second instrument: dropping each item's lowest category from MFQ's six-item purity scale lowers α in **6/6 cells with ω moving the same way ⇒ the direction replicates**; **but by only −0.015…−0.051, against GSS's −0.248, about five times any cell ⇒ direction transfers and magnitude does not, so "cut by a third" is GSS's number, not a general fact.**`[#767「区间只动了一点,而我发现自己把另一次运行的数抄进了账本」]`. ⚠ **The residual remains the largest gap, and hard rule ③ forbids a fourth candidate from the same matrix of social categories — so change the kind of variable: what the person themselves has done.** If "policing others is policing yourself" held, the ontology would move from authority to self-regulation and this arc would need a different lead. The answer came in two layers: **a person's last-year conduct (partners, frequency, infidelity) explains only 1.9%** — under a third of politics' lower bound. ⚠⚠ **But the first arm used the wrong instrument, which I found and repaired inside the round**: for a married fifty-year-old, "partners last year" is identically 1 and says nothing about their history, **while the folk theory is about a lifetime**. Switching to lifetime partners: **9.1%, 4.8× the last-year figure**, landing inside politics' interval; and it is not age (age alone explains 1.4%). ⚠⚠ **And the second arm carries a confound I did not write before the run and must state after it**: someone with many partners has almost certainly had premarital sex, so `lifetime` and `premarsx` overlap semantically — on the least entangled item (attitudes to homosexuality) the share is **5.8%**. ⇒ **Own lifetime sexual history explains [5.8%, 12.4%]; religion explains [33.3%, 46.7%].** **"Policing others is policing yourself" is a little bit true and nowhere near the main cause.** ⚠ And the round's real methodological claim — that two time windows under one variable name are two constructs — had to change instruments: NSFG carries both partner counts, **lifetime is stronger on 3 of 4 items with one reversal ⇒ the pre-registered verdict is "not a universal rule"**, and NSFG top-codes lifetime partners at 50 on a sample of women 15–44, **both of which compress the contrast.**`[#768「管别人的性,一点点是在管自己一生做过的事,几乎不是在管去年」]`. ⚠⚠ **And that 5.8% lower bound was picked by me after the fact, not measured** — I decided only after seeing the results that attitudes to homosexuality were least entangled with a respondent's own opposite-sex partner count. The measurable alternative: **entanglement = each item's bivariate |ρ| with lifetime partners**, regress the four shares on it, and read the intercept at zero entanglement. **The slope is +0.2982 — the share does rise with entanglement, so the worry was correct** (`premarsx` at entanglement 0.34 gives 12.2%; `homosex` at 0.21 gives 5.9%). **The intercept is 1.9% with a 95% interval of [−2.2%, 6.2%].** ⚠ **But the pre-registered branch fires honestly: that interval is 8.4pp wide against my hand-ordered 6.6pp — this replaced "what I picked" with "what cannot be resolved", and is not a tightening.** ⇒ **All that can be said is: once the semantic overlap is stripped out, own lifetime sexual history becomes indistinguishable from nothing, and this design cannot tell whether it is 0 or 6%.**`[#769「纠缠度不该由我来排,而测出来之后是测不动的」]`. ⚠⚠ **And across all five of those rounds I reported an "explained share" without once asking whether it could have come out otherwise.** With a single control the partial correlation has a closed form, so the share contains only three numbers — **and the closed form matches the numerical pipeline to 1.9e−16 on all four items, machine precision.** Then build a **bivariate Gaussian matching only those two marginal correlations** (utterly unlike the real religiosity composite above second order): **the share differs by at most 0.89pp while the skew differs by 0.38** ⇒ **the share eats only second moments and is blind to what the variable actually is.** ⇒ **The explained share is a derivation, not an independent measurement.** "Religion explains a third" is the same sentence as "religiosity correlates +0.27 with valuing obedience and +0.42–0.47 with sexual attitudes". ⚠⚠ **So the earlier claim that "the largest single explanation is something we have not measured" is retracted** — the "residual" is merely the partial correlation on another scale, **not a quantity about the world**; **what remains sayable is: no measured covariate's correlations are large enough to flatten the link between wanting children to obey and policing other people's sex.** That is much weaker, and it is true. ⚠ The correlations themselves remain measurements; and the gate demonstrated that this check can fail — **a deliberately denominator-less formula differs by 0.0305**.`[#770「解释份额是推导不是测量,而我用它写了五轮」]`. ⚠ **So what on this line is not a derivation?** The pre-registered candidate was the **interaction**, and it comes with an elegant null of its own: **a trivariate Gaussian world matched on the same correlation matrix** — in a Gaussian the conditional correlation **does not vary with the conditioning variable**, so any stratified difference in the real data that exceeds it **is not a function of second moments**. The result: **within religiosity terciles, ρ(obey, extramarital sex) falls from +0.2078 to +0.0655**, and all four items' spreads **exceed the Gaussian null (1.08×–2.60×)**. ⇒ **The interaction is not a function of second moments — the first non-derivation quantity this project has found on this line.** ⇒ **In one sentence about people: among the least devout, wanting children to obey is most tightly tied to condemning other people's sex; the more devout a person is, the weaker that tie becomes — because among the devout the condemnation is already supplied by religion, so obedience no longer has to carry it.** ⚠⚠ **But the magnitude is unreadable, and that was written before the run**: the Gaussian null removes what stratification itself creates, and cannot remove the real change in variance across strata — `xmarsex`'s attitude sd falls from 0.856 to **0.542 (−37%)**, because **the most devout agree with one another and there is less variance left to correlate**. ⇒ **What is supported is that the interaction exists, not how large it is.** ⚠ And `premarsx` at **1.08× sits on its null and does not count**; it is also the only non-monotone item (+0.1759→+0.1267→+0.1970), **running against the other three — and it is not to be explained yet.**`[#771「第一个不是推导的量:交互;而它的方向被第二具仪器推翻了」]`. ⚠⚠ **And the next round dissolved that disagreement entirely: the answer is not in the people, it is in the scale.** Censoring first: **in the most devout tercile, 87.8% say extramarital sex is "always wrong"** (`teensex` 82.2%, `homosex` 80.5%), against a pre-registered censoring threshold of 60% — **that four-point item is already at its ceiling for them, and no strength-of-relation reading is legible in that stratum.** ⇒ **In one sentence about people: among the most devout third of Americans this question cannot distinguish anyone, because they agree with each other; what "the tie weakens with devotion" was reading is the scale hitting a wall, not a relation weakening.** ⚠ And that explains why the two instruments differed: **GSS uses a single four-point item that saturates in the devout stratum, while MFQ's authority score is a continuous multi-item mean with flat sd (0.783→0.838) and no ceiling** — **they never disagreed about the world; one instrument simply cannot see in that stratum.** ⚠ The variance-robust statistics (raw-value OLS slope, marginal-matched ρ) are reported but **marked unreadable per the pre-registration** — a slope fixes variance and **cannot fix censoring**, which was written before the run. ⚠ The only item not at its ceiling is `premarsx` (endpoint 46.4%, sd **rising**, slope **not falling**), **three indicators agreeing — and this round does not explain it.**`[#772「那道题在最虔诚的人身上已经到顶了,所以那个方向是撞墙不是关系」]`. ⚠⚠ **And that ceiling has a longitudinal version, which is a question about people: has the consensus always been this tight, or did it tighten?** The arithmetic trap is the whole of it — **on a bounded scale the variance must fall as the mean approaches an endpoint**, so "the devout's variance is falling" could be produced entirely by the mean moving. Removing it (reporting `var_obs / var_max(μ)`, which is immune to the mean's movement), the most devout tercile from 1988→2024 gives: **`homosex` 0.7531→0.9194 (2.20× its own null), `premarsx` 0.6522→0.7875 (1.85×), `teensex` 0.5359→0.7346, while `xmarsex` goes 0.5849→0.6024 at 0.13×, sitting still. Zero of eight cells fall significantly and four of eight rise.** ⇒ **In one sentence about people: among the most devout third of Americans, opinions about sex did not converge between 1988 and 2024 — they spread out; the single exception is extramarital sex, which has not moved in decades — and that is exactly the one item genuinely at its ceiling.** ⚠ **So two ceilings that look identical come from different places**: `xmarsex`'s is real unanimity, and the other three items' is a consequence of the mean moving.`[#776「虔诚者不是越来越一致,是越来越分散;只有婚外性纹丝不动」]`. ⚠⚠ **And when the next round asked whether "only extramarital sex sits still" means genuine stability or an early ceiling, the premise was refuted outright by the data:** in the devout tercile `xmarsex`'s endpoint share **never falls below 85.1%** (it was already 87.2% in 1988), and all six threshold windows **return 0 years** — **"before it saturated" does not exist in GSS.** ⇒ All that can be said is "unanimous across the 36 years we can see", and **the "genuine unanimity" in the row above overstepped that**. ⚠ The positive control passed (a 3×-null slope planted into the 28-year series was recovered exactly, and g=0 recovered zero), **so the empty window is not a power problem but an absence of that history in the data.** ⚠ And the data answered a question I had not asked, with its own null attached: **among the devout, the share saying "always wrong" fell for `homosex` from 85.9% to 67.4% (2.38× its own null) and for `premarsx` from 53.6% to 40.9% (2.12×), while `xmarsex` went 87.2%→89.6% (0.76×, not clearing its null).** ⇒ **In one sentence about people: the most devout third of Americans genuinely changed their minds about homosexuality — by eighteen points — and have not moved on extramarital sex.** ⚠ Endpoint share is a **mean-side** statistic and does not license the variance claim above; and "changed their minds" is a synthetic cohort across waves, not the same people.`[#777「虔诚的人在同性恋上真的改了主意,在婚外性上从我们看得见的第一年起就没动过」]`. ⚠⚠ **And that sentence was missing half of itself, which the next round supplied: eighteen points — relative to whom?** `#747` had already measured that churchgoers' level shift was half of everyone else's, **and I had never read the two side by side.** Over the same 28 years, across two stratifications and two statistics: **the least devout tercile fell from 61.1% to 21.8%** against the most devout's 85.9%→67.4%; **devout/non-devout ratios are 0.409–0.431 on level and 0.328–0.341 on endpoint share relative to baseline, differing by only 1.26×.** ⇒ **The sentence with its comparison attached reads: the most devout third of Americans did change their minds about homosexuality, but by only a third to two-fifths of everyone else's change, and they started higher and finished higher.**⚠ (**this precision is retracted in `#783`** — that range was the span between two stratifications, not an uncertainty; with each one's own sampling interval attached it is **[0.241, 0.568]**, same direction and 3.2× less precise)  ⚠ **A change reported without a denominator gets one supplied by the reader, and that denominator is usually "everyone moved alike"** — which is what the row above owed. ⚠ And this round runs opposite to another: there the choice of statistic **flipped** the answer, while here two statistics differ by 1.26× — **both situations have occurred in this project, so "try another statistic" is sometimes decisive and sometimes redundant, and only running it tells you which.**`[#778「他们确实改了主意,但只有别人的三分之一到五分之二」]`. ⚠⚠ **And three consecutive same-direction rounds make a basin, so the next round deliberately ran at the most ordinary rival explanation: the devout are older, and older people change less.** Recomputed with birth cohort held fixed: **1930–1949 gives a level ratio of 0.321 and an endpoint-relative ratio of 0.222; 1950–1964 gives 0.435 and 0.230** — **the ratio does not drift toward 1, and on the endpoint side it is more extreme than the pooled version (0.226 against 0.328–0.341).** ⇒ **In one sentence about people: with birth cohort held fixed, the devout's change on homosexuality is still only a quarter to a third of everyone else's — "the devout change less" is not a restatement of "old people change less".** ⚠⚠ **And the identification limit belongs ahead of the conclusion, as it was written before the run**: within a cohort `age = year − cohort` is an **identity**, so a within-cohort slope is period and age combined — **this round did not separate age and does not claim to**; all it can say is that the ratio does not move once cohort is fixed. ⚠ The two younger cohorts are **unreadable** (1965–79's devout slope sits inside its own null; 1980–99 is missing a stratum) and are **labelled rather than averaged in** — 2 of 4 readable, exactly the pre-registered minimum.`[#779「钉住出生世代之后,那个比不但还在,而且更极端」]`. ⚠⚠ **And when the next round asked whether a different cut would bring the younger cohorts in, the pre-registered guard fired at me.** Replacing terciles with a median split lifts **1930–1949's level ratio from 0.321 to 0.501, a move of 0.181 against a pre-registered 0.10** ⇒ **the cut is deciding the answer.** ⚠⚠ **And 1965–1979 did become readable under the two-way split (devout 1.49×, non-devout 1.74×) — exactly the outcome I wanted; but the pre-registration puts the guard first, and the guard did not pass, so it is not read.** **That is the one thing that round genuinely tested: a rule I wrote to constrain myself was obeyed at the moment it actually constrained me.** ⚠ The direction of the move was written before the run: a two-way split folds the middle third into both sides, lowering contrast and **pushing the ratio toward 1 — arithmetic, not a finding.** ⇒ **The consequence is that these ratios are re-reported as an interval over cuts: level [0.32, 0.50], endpoint relative to baseline [0.22, 0.37]** — **the direction is unchanged and the devout still change markedly less, but the precision I published was too tight.**`[#780「切法在决定答案,而护栏是对着我开的火」]`. ⚠⚠ **And once the whole family of degrees of freedom was swept together, the pre-registered retraction condition fired.** 2 cuts × 2 per-year thresholds × 2 cohort segmentations × 2 statistics = 50 cells, **37 readable and 13 not, all listed**; the ratio percentiles are **5% 0.185, median 0.375, 95% 0.692, max 1.128**. **Only one cell reaches 1.0: Seg-B · terciles · n≥60 · cohort 1975–1994 · level statistic = 1.128** (that cohort's other three cells are 0.438, 0.623, 0.821). ⚠⚠ **This is the easiest place to find myself an excuse, so it is stated flatly: that cell passed the readability criterion I wrote myself, so it counts; "36 out of 37 is good enough" is not what the pre-registration said — I wrote "any", and one fired.** ⇒ **The unconditional sentence "the devout change less" is retracted. What replaces it is the distribution: of 37 readable specifications, 36 show the devout changing less (median 0.375) and one does not.** ⚠ **That cell is located, not explained** — its year span and n are the weakest in the grid, and **it must not be read as "the gap has vanished among the young", which would need its own round.** ⚠ The grid also answered which degree of freedom moves the answer most: **cut 0.147, statistic 0.146, cohort segmentation 0.075, and the per-year threshold only 0.022.**`[#781「三十七个规格里有一个反号,而预注册说那就撤回」]`. ⚠⚠ **And when the next round asked whether that cell was a genuine reversal or my criterion being too loose, the answer was the latter.** Giving the ratio its own year-level bootstrap interval: **1.128 sits in [0.561, 2.851], width 2.29, containing 1.0, 0.623 and 0.821 all at once** — **the cell cannot even establish which side of 1 the ratio is on.** ⇒ **The retraction stands, but its reason changes: not "the world contains a reversed specification" but "my readability criterion was too loose"** — `#779`–`#781` required each stratum's slope to clear its own null, which **never tested the ratio**; with a small denominator, two individually significant quantities can still divide into something wildly unstable. ⚠⚠ **And I nearly published on a verdict branch that asked the wrong question**: the first version tested whether the interval contained the whole segment [0.44, 1.13], and since 0.561 > 0.44 it returned "a genuine reversal", **while the pre-registration asked whether the cell could distinguish them** — another instance of the wrong-question-branch family, **and this time it changed the conclusion.** ⚠ Applied back across all 44 cells: **10 (23%) have intervals containing 1.0**, and the split by cohort is remarkably clean — **0/30 for 1935–54, 1950–64, 1955–74 and 1965–79; 4/4 for 1975–94; 2/2 for 1980–99 (median width 7.73); 4/8 for 1930–49.** ⚠ **those two denominators are corrected in `#787`**: they count **the cells that could be computed**, and of the design's 8 cells only **4** and **2** respectively could be — writing 4/4 and 2/2 makes the unreadability look more total than it is. ⇒ **In one sentence about people, harder than the row above: across the four cohorts the data can actually see — thirty specifications — not one interval contains 1.0, so the devout really do change less; and in the youngest and oldest cohorts the ratio simply cannot be pinned. The cell that triggered the retraction fell exactly where the data cannot see.**`[#782「撤回维持,但理由换了人:不是世界有反号,是我的判据没检验比值」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The first thing `#782`① asked for was a count, and the count overturned the way I was counting.** The anchored windows publish **17 devout/non-devout ratios**, and **the problem only becomes visible once they are split by unit**: 9 cohort cells, 4 pooled, 3 grid percentiles, 1 derived. **The four pooled numbers — the two most-quoted sentences on this page — never had an interval**; computed here: level **0.431 [0.319, 0.568]** and **0.409 [0.301, 0.526]**, endpoint-relative **0.341 [0.246, 0.457]** and **0.328 [0.241, 0.437]**, **all four excluding 1.0.** ⚠⚠ **What was actually overturned is not "missing intervals" but "the intervals already on the page are a different object"**: `0.409–0.431` and `0.328–0.341` are **spans between two stratifications** (0.022 and 0.013) while the bootstrap widths of the same numbers are 0.237 and 0.203 — **10.8× and 16.1×**; median over four places **6.3×** against a pre-registered threshold of 2×. ⇒ **the page has been publishing the small kind of uncertainty and leaving the large kind off it.** ⚠⚠ **And that sentence was narrowed within the same round by a second arm, which is exactly why the arm was run.** As written above it claims "wherever I publish a span" — a general claim about my reporting habit, **measured on GSS alone** (`readme_gate`'s `single_instrument` blocked the commit on precisely this). Moving to **MSSCQ** (sexual self-concept, **n = 12,789 complete respondents**, resampling **people** rather than year-points): the 2×2 specification grid spans **0.0519**, the four bootstrap widths run 0.0110–0.0171, and **the median 0.0129 ÷ 0.0519 = 0.25× — the opposite sign to GSS's 6.3×.** ⇒ **the qualifier is not about how I write but about how many units get resampled**: not "the page has been publishing the small kind of uncertainty" but **"on quantities whose effective n is 28 year-points, the specification span is far smaller than the sampling width"**; across 12,789 people the span is the more conservative of the two and using it as an error bar does not distort. ⚠⚠ **And the second arm's first version was killed by its own positive control, which is worth more than its conclusion**: I hand-rolled "per-facet median then median" and got 0.5468, failing to reproduce the page's 0.5645 ⇒ UNVERIFIED. Two causes: ① I rewrote a function the project already has (`lib/blocks.py::median_of_group_medians`, whose docstring already says "`#721` measured: 0.5645 vs 0.5856"); ② **and calling it does not give 0.5645 either** — only enumerating the 2×2 matched: **`0.5645` = normalised ρ + per-facet median, `0.5856` = raw |ρ| + 200-pair pooled. ⇒ the two numbers the page prints side by side differ on two degrees of freedom at once; that was never a specification span.** ⚠⚠ **The heaviest self-catch is my own matcher**: the first version matched published numbers to `#782`'s 44 cells **by value**, so the pooled `0.328` landed on a **cohort cell** by coincidence, `0.375` — the **median over the whole grid** — was treated as a single cell, and `0.226` matched `0.222` (0.004 apart). **The instrument's unit and the claim's unit have to be written as two strings and required to be equal**; adding that drops the matchable count from 14 to 9 and **blocks 5 false pairings on the spot**. ⇒ **One sentence about people, and this time it is the precision that changes, not the direction: the most devout third of Americans did change less on homosexuality than everyone else — all four pooled specifications exclude 1.0, and that holds; but the page's "only a third to two-fifths of everyone else" (0.33–0.40) treated the gap between two stratifications as the error bar. Folding in each one's own sampling uncertainty gives [0.241, 0.568], 3.2× wider. The honest phrasing is "a quarter to nearly three-fifths" — the direction is unchanged and my published precision was three times too tight.**`[#783「页面公布的是小的那种不确定性,大的那种一直在幕后」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The next round was built to kill the sentence above: what if "the devout changed less" is not psychology at all, but the shape of a bounded ruler?** The devout stratum's 1974 endpoint share is **0.859** against **0.611** for the non-devout — **the higher the start, the fewer categories a given attitude change can move through.** Turning that rival into a null that can actually fire: shared thresholds, each stratum's own starting point, and **the same latent shift Δ applied to both** (Δ is not a free parameter — it is solved so the non-devout arm reproduces its own observed 50-year change), then read off what ratio the starting points alone would produce. **The answer is 0.813 (8 cells, 0.715–0.887); what they actually produced is 0.456.** **All 8 specifications** place the forced ratio outside and above the observed interval — **none indistinguishable, none reversed.** ⇒ **One sentence about people: the ceiling explains roughly a third and the other two thirds are theirs — with the shape of the ruler removed, the most devout third of Americans still changed only 0.54 [0.38, 0.78] as much as everyone else, and none of the eight intervals contains 1.0.** ⚠⚠ **Both numbers are retracted with a sign change in `#785`**: this round's `r_obs` is a slope ratio while Δ was calibrated on a first-to-last difference — **two estimands in one ratio**; unified onto slopes they become **0.813 → 1.369** and **0.536 → 0.331**, so **the ceiling explains none of it and removing it makes the gap larger, not smaller.** ⚠ The round's largest assumption is stated ahead of its conclusion: **shared thresholds** — if the two strata read the same question differently (DIF), `r_forced` is not what it claims to be, and GSS cannot test that. ⚠⚠ **And the first version was killed on the spot by its own two controls, which is worth more than the conclusion**: Δ was bisected over `[0, 6]`, searching only upward shifts, while attitudes were becoming more permissive (high = strict ⇒ dB<0), so Δ collapsed to 0, every `r_forced` came out −0.000, the positive control did not return 1.0 and the negative control was not monotone ⇒ UNVERIFIED. **The direction is worth recording: the common case is a control failing for its own reasons; here the reverse happened — the control failed and what failed was the thing under test. The two are told apart by one thing, whether the failure localises to a mechanism.**`[#784「天花板解释了三分之一,剩下的三分之二是他们自己」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The next round turned the item into an axis, and it retracted the number the line above had just put here — with the sign of the correction reversed.** The previous round ran the ceiling control on `homosex` alone — **one item is not a family.** The first run caught it: that round's `r_obs` came from a **slope ratio** while Δ was calibrated on a **first-to-last difference**, **two estimands written into one ratio**. Unified onto slopes, `r_forced` moves from **0.813 to 1.369** and the residual from **0.536 to 0.331** ⇒ **"the ceiling explains about a third" is retracted: `r_forced > 1` means the ceiling shielded the devout not at all — the same latent shift should have moved them *more* (1.37×) and they moved 0.43, so removing it makes the gap larger, not smaller.** The direction is unchanged; what reversed is that term's sign, **and it was the previous round's headline.** ⚠⚠ **And with all four items in view the sentence's scope has to shrink too:** `homosex` 8/8 residuals entirely below 1.0 (0.331), `premarsx` 2/2 (0.390) ⚠ **denominator corrected in `#786`/`#787`: only 1 of 4 specifications is readable**, and those 2 cells are that one specification under two link functions, `teensex` **0/4, all containing 1.0** — and that is precisely the item where the devout stratum's endpoint share is highest (0.823) and the ceiling really is shielding (forced ratio 0.572) — `xmarsex` **not one readable cell**, because neither stratum moved, so the denominator approaches zero and the ratio turns Cauchy-like. ⇒ **One sentence about people: "the devout changed less" is not a law about sexual morality but a law about the items everyone else actually moved on — on homosexuality and premarital sex they moved about a third as much, and the ceiling excuses none of it; on teen sex it cannot be separated; on extramarital sex the question cannot be asked at all.**`[#785「不是一条关于性道德的规律,是一条关于「其余人真的动了的那些题」的规律」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The line above wrote down what it had not separated, and the next round is exactly that.** It said part of `teensex`'s unreadability might be nothing but a shortage of years — **the shape of "a result attributed to a mechanism that may only be power".** Checked as a paired design: same item, same people, same machine, **only the year set moves**. `teensex` actually has **21 year-points (1986–2024)**. Restricting `homosex` to that same window: **still 4/4 readable**; taking instead the earliest 21 points, which separates *how many points* from *which era*: **still 4/4**. Ratios 0.31–0.48 across all three windows. ⇒ **`teensex`'s unreadability is not caused by having 21 points, and the line above holds.** ⚠⚠ **What this round caught along the way is a denominator inside that same table**: it reported "`premarsx` 2 readable cells, 2/2 entirely below 1.0", which reads as unanimous — **in fact only one of four specifications is readable**, and those two cells are that one specification under two link functions. **The denominator of "2/2" is the cells that survived, not the cells attempted.** And under a different year window `premarsx`'s only readable cell is **1.902 [1.004, 3.261]**, above 1.0, **pointing the other way**. ⇒ **One sentence about people, one notch narrower than the line above: "the devout moved about a third as much" now rests on homosexuality alone — and rests there more firmly, across three year-windows, twelve of twelve cells readable, ratios 0.31–0.48 — while the premarital-sex half is downgraded to "one of four specifications readable, and it reverses under a different window".**`[#786「同一道题只动年份集合,而同性恋那条在三个窗口下纹丝不动」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The next question is purely psychological — why homosexuality and not the others? — and it was blocked, and what blocked it is worth writing down.** Two ontologically different worlds: **one master brake** (devoutness as general resistance to any moral change) against **individual switches** (a doctrinal brake, applied only where the tradition names something). Separating them requires measuring **non-sexual** moral items. ⇒ **Of 18 candidates, `label_pole` refused to judge the direction of 14.** The refusals split in two, and **only one kind is my fault**: `homosex` has five categories with `other` last and I passed all of them rather than the first four; **the other kind is not an error but a boundary** — **for binary policy items the direction lives in the question stem, not the labels**: `yes` in `abany` is permissive, `yes` in `racmar` (should there be laws against racial intermarriage) is restrictive, **the same word with opposite moral polarity.** Adding `yes/no` to the vocabulary would make it run, **and that is exactly what this instrument exists to prevent** — its proxy ledger says: no match ⇒ I genuinely do not know the direction ⇒ raise; **a match never proves the direction is right.** ⇒ **One sentence about this work itself: this is the first time the project has been blocked by an instrument boundary rather than a data boundary, and the two call for opposite responses — a data boundary means find other data, an instrument boundary means find another reading, and the new reading must arrive with its own control.** ⚠ The positive control did its job here: its first words were that this round's instrument is not the one the previous round used — **without it I would have answered an 18-item question with 4 items.**` ⚠ **and since `#806` this must carry one more sentence: the "common latent shift" control is **out of this design's reach** for the top pair — both calibration arms exceed the 30% reachable-range limit (`sexeduc` 42%/31%, `racmar` 72%/71%) ⇒ zero usable cells, **structurally impossible, not a debt**.** [#788「挡住这个问题的不是数据,是我那具自动定向仪器的边界」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The next round first killed the boundary in the line above, then ran into something much larger.** That boundary said the automatic polarity instrument cannot read the direction of yes/no policy items, so the question cannot be asked — **but the ratio I need is invariant to polarity**: flip an item and both strata's slopes flip, leaving the ratio untouched. Three lines of algebra, and a gauge control turned them into a measurement: **all 24 items flipped, max |difference| 1.33e−15.** ⇒ **A capability boundary written wider than it really is, is as dangerous as a conclusion written too wide — and more insidious, because nobody ever goes back to verify a "cannot", and I had built this particular wall a day earlier.** With the boundary removed the item set went from 4 to 24 candidates, 8 readable. ⇒ **The two group medians are almost identical (sexual 0.409, non-sexual 0.410) and I came close to publishing "one master brake" exactly as pre-registered.** The control I added overturned it: **that difference's own 95% interval is [−2.067, +1.432], width 3.499, where resolving the question needs ≤0.330** — the 0.165 threshold was **within-item** jitter while the comparison is a **between-item** median. ⇒ **One sentence about people, and what it retracts is my own question rather than my answer: "the devout brake specifically on sexual morality" is cut along the wrong dimension.** The axis that is actually moving puts "sex education in schools" (2.19) and "laws against racial intermarriage" (1.84) at the same end — the devout changed *more* than everyone else on both — and "school prayer" (0.13) and "should the government aid Black people" (0.11) at the other, barely moving at all. **That is not "sexual versus non-sexual." And the cut this page has used since `E03` is, from this round on, a hypothesis under test rather than a premise.**`[#789「变异在组内,不在组间:「性 / 非性」这个切法解释不了任何东西」]`。 ⚠⚠ **The axis in the line above rests on eight points, so the next round asked whether the twelve unstable items can be rescued — the answer is no, and the reason is a statement about what can be asked at all.** Regressing `log(interval width)` on two pre-specified quantities at once: **the denominator's signal-to-noise has a standardised coefficient of −0.681 [−0.903, −0.466], excluding 0, while the number of year-points gives +0.101 [−0.239, +0.410], containing 0** (and the two are separable, `corr = −0.324`). The positive control did its job: shrinking the denominator to 0.25× widened the interval from 0.22 to 0.90. ⇒ **Those twelve items are unstable not for want of years but because society moved too little on them** — **year-window surgery cannot rescue them, because this is not a power problem: the estimand has no definition where society did not move.** ⇒ **One sentence about people: resistance can only be measured against change that actually happened.** On extramarital sex, gun permits, letting incurable patients die, and abortion for wanting no more children, society itself did not move; on twelve others it moved but too little for the ratio to settle. **So the "measurable" in "sexual morality is a measurable social operation" is decided by whether society moved, not by my instrument — and this also pins the scope of the axis above: it holds only on the eight items where society moved a lot.**⚠⚠ **the mechanism in this paragraph was refined in `#799` — not retracted, distinguished**: `#799`'s σ=0 control shows that **with no noise a small denominator produces no inflation at all** (all eight return exactly 0.450000) ⇒ **the denominator is noise's amplifier, not a push** — this sentence must be read as "small denominator ⇒ noise amplified ⇒ **the interval widens**", **not** "small denominator ⇒ **the point estimate is pushed up**". **Two different mechanisms this paragraph had been treating as one.**`[#790「不稳来自分母,所以 n=8 是硬天花板:抗拒只能在真的发生过的变化上被测量」]`。 ⚠⚠ **Before looking for an explanation of that axis, the next round asked something more basic: is it an object at all? — and the answer is "yes, but it is not an axis, and explaining it is arithmetically out of reach".** First, what could not be done: the previous step required candidates to be written down before seeing the ordering, **and I had already seen it** — so any candidate would be written to the answer, and marking an impossible criterion "planned" is forbidden. ⇒ Only the two things needing no candidate were run. **The first is the MDE, which depends only on the design: at n=8 a candidate would need |ρ| ≥ 0.90 to be detected (the permutation null's 95th percentile is 0.714) — almost nothing in social science reaches that across items. And the row above already proved this instrument cannot supply more items.
