⚠ **`#812`③'s new rule is executed here for the first time**: every `UNRESOLVED` must print what it is compatible with — the 1980s cells all print "compatible with {0, 0.5, 1}", **which is precisely the evidence that they say nothing**; ⚠ each decade uses only its first and last year ⇒ the intervals are **lower bounds**; shared thresholds remain an assumption and **DIF is structurally untestable**; `matters = 0.25` **is my choice**⟩ ⚠⚠ **appended by `#816`**: the phrase "two groups moving in different directions" on this row **presupposes that the same people moved, and that premise had never been tested**. `#816` decomposes the 1990→1998 `Δgap` by birth cohort: **attitude term −0.501, composition term −0.086, the composition share being 14.7% [1.2%, 36.5%], an interval excluding 0** ⇒ **most of it really was people changing their minds (and both strata did: the devout's attitude term is +0.166 against +0.667 for everyone else), but roughly one seventh is not anyone "moving" at all — it is the two groups' generational composition shifting** ⇒ **"all of it is movement" is wrong.**|
| **The divide did not grow — it split open in the nineties and the two-thousands** | **decade-by-decade `Δgap`, referenced not against 0 but against "the uniform rate scaled to that decade's actual span"** (the −1.103 total spread over five decades = −0.2206, times `actual span/10`; `matters` = 0.11, supplied explicitly as `#811` requires): 1970s −0.075 [−0.268,+0.106]; **1980s +0.052 [−0.168,+0.271], EXCLUDES the uniform rate**; **1990s −0.629 [−0.860,−0.402], EXCLUDES both the uniform rate and zero**; **2000s −0.476 [−0.705,−0.238], EXCLUDES both**; 2010s +0.023 [−0.207,+0.252]; the 2020s have only 2 years and **do not enter the count, by a rule fixed before the run** ⇒ **3 of the 5 counted decades depart from uniformity ⇒ "uniform" is dead** | ⚠⚠ **a sentence about people, far more concrete than "it tripled over fifty years"**: **in the eighties it did not measurably move; in the nineties it opened by −0.63 and in the two-thousands by a further −0.48; by the twenty-tens it had stopped again — this divide has its own decades**; ⚠ **and the two decades' point estimates summing to −1.10 against a total of −1.103 is a coincidence that must not be read as precision: the decade slices do not tile the timeline** (1977→1980, 1989→1990, 1998→2000 and 2008→2010 are covered by no cell) ⇒ **by construction they were never meant to sum to the total**; ⚠⚠ **and my own verdict string contained a P6 error, corrected in the same round**: it said GSS's 2010s was "inconsistent" with NSFG's tight null — **wrong. GSS [−0.207,+0.252] entirely contains NSFG [−0.089,+0.096]**, **so they are not inconsistent; that GSS cell simply lacks resolution. `UNRESOLVED` is neither an acquittal nor a conviction, and I read it as a conviction** ⇒ **this is the same default move in its third form** (twice reading "a narrow zero" as "could not measure", now reading "could not measure" as "conflict") — **of the three values I only ever genuinely use two** `[#812「这条鸿沟不是长出来的」]` (Entry 812) 〔GSS〕 ⟨positive control (a synthetic world with the entire shift concentrated in the 1990s) ⇒ **only the 1990s returns EXCLUDES**; negative control (a genuinely uniform world) ⇒ **none of the five decades returns EXCLUDES**; ⚠⚠ **the negative control failed on the first run and what it caught was two errors, one of which was contaminating the real analysis too**: ① **the generator added the structure on top of the observed data instead of replacing it** — the "uniform world" was the real world plus a uniform trend, **non-uniform by construction** (`realstat`'s wording is destroy the structure under test, preserve everything else — **and I had preserved it**); ② **the reference was not scaled to each decade's actual span** — the decades span 3–9 years, so **short-spanning decades were bound to be judged as departures, and that error corrupted every row of the real analysis** ⇒ **a failing negative control that turned out to expose a definitional error in the estimand itself**; ⚠ what could not be fixed: the 2020s have only 2 years ⇒ **whether it has moved again since the twenty-tens is unanswerable here**; `matters = 0.11` **is my choice, a judgement about what magnitude counts, not something the data supplied**⟩|

**And the two decades `#812` found had no explanation at all — so the common-shift control was run on them, with the unmoving eighties included as a control decade.** **The nineties: `explained` = +0.27 and +0.37, both excluding 1. The two-thousands: +0.008 and +0.010, both excluding 1. And the control decade, the eighties, returns four intervals spanning ±7 to ±14 — saying nothing at all.** ⇒ **A sentence about people: both openings were two groups genuinely moving in different directions — not the same change read from two starting lines. In the nineties roughly seven-tenths of it is not explicable by the scale; in the two-thousands almost none of it is.** ⚠⚠ This also narrows what `#805`'s published interval **means** (its number is not wrong): it was computed across the whole fifty years, **blending two decades that split open with three that did not move — so it describes no process that actually occurred.** ⚠⚠⚠ **And the most transferable finding lands on my own two previous rounds**: this round's negative control failed on a single draw, and redrawn 200 times its median is +1.0057 with a 95% range of [+0.77, +1.44] — **the instrument is unbiased and that failure was noise; but `#805` and `#806` drew the same control once each against a tolerance of 0.08, four times narrower than its own sampling spread.** **A control whose tolerance sits inside its own noise floor fires at random — those passes were luck, not validation.** ⚠ This overturns neither round's conclusion; **what is withdrawn is the strength claimed by the phrase "all controls sound".** `[#813「那两次张开是真分歧」]`
| **What "a narrow zero" was missing was not a branch — it was an input I had never said out loud** | **`Gate.interval_verdict(lo, hi, reference, matters)`**, three-valued: `EXCLUDES`, `TIGHT_NULL` (measured, and it is approximately no change), `UNRESOLVED` (could not measure); **backtested on 7 cases whose answers were known in advance ⇒ 7/7** (`#810` NSFG [−0.0887,+0.0959] against a 0.17 threshold ⇒ **TIGHT_NULL**; both GSS arms at the same threshold ⇒ **UNRESOLVED**; `#808` `homosex` fit ⇒ **EXCLUDES**; `suicide2` raw ⇒ **TIGHT_NULL**; `matters` omitted ⇒ **UNRESOLVED**) | ⚠⚠ **writing it revealed that the missing piece was never an `elif` but an input I had never stated**: **the word "narrow" means nothing on its own — an interval is wide or narrow only relative to how large a change would matter** ⇒ `matters` **must be supplied explicitly, and omitting it returns `UNRESOLVED`** — **if you cannot say how large a thing you are looking for, you have not earned the right to call a zero "no change"**; the criterion has the shape of an equivalence test, not a significance test; ⚠⚠ **and the backtest exposed a hole in `#808`, which is this round's real gain**: `#808` grouped three items' `raw` intervals together as "containing 0", and at a 0.17 threshold they split into two classes (`suicide2` = `TIGHT_NULL`, the other two = `UNRESOLVED`) ⇒ **`#808` never once stated how large a change would matter, and without that number every "contains 0" in its table is simultaneously compatible with "genuinely unchanged" and with "could not be measured"**; ⚠ **and I do not supply a threshold on its behalf** — 0.17 was derived for NSFG's six-year window while `#808` spans 19–28 years, so **reusing the number would be a substitution** ⇒ **registered as `#808`'s debt: what it owes is a statement of what magnitude matters, not more computation. That is exactly this rule's value — it did not decide anything for me, it made visible a question I had never answered** `[#811「「窄的零」缺的不是一个分支」]` (Entry 811) 〔this page's own gate library〕 ⟨P6 proxy ledger: **only one direction is sound — an interval not inside the band ⇒ a change worth caring about really has not been excluded; being inside the band does not prove the true value equals the reference, only that a deviation that large has been excluded, and `matters` is my choice** ⇒ **it reports "not excluded" only and never certifies "truly zero"**; ⚠ **what could not be fixed**: like `headroom_control` it can serve as both a control and a screen, and `#805` has just demonstrated that **a screen failing and a control failing are different events** ⇒ **no screen entry point is added here** (the pure function `interval_verdict` is the screening use), **stated plainly: whoever uses it as a screen carries `#805`'s consequence**; **this round reports no effect**⟩|
| **A second instrument exists — and what it returns is a *narrow* zero, not "no resolution"** | **both NSFG female waves (2011-2013, 2017-2019) carry `SAMESEX` ("sexual relations between two same-sex adults is all right", 5 categories, the same topic as GSS `homosex`) together with `ATTNDNOW` (7 categories, no missingness in either wave)**; after three matching steps (single-item stratifier plus a matched GSS "`attend` only" specification; population restricted to women 18–49; polarity flipped to `6−v`): **NSFG Δgap = +0.0015 [−0.0887, +0.0959]**, GSS (`attend`, matched) +0.2218 [−0.0350, +0.4720], GSS (three-item composite) **−0.0306** [−0.3201, +0.2651] ⇒ **no "same sign and both excluding 0" ⇒ the kill fired against expectation** | ⚠⚠ **this round began from an observation about me, not about the world**: `#802`–`#809` ran eight consecutive rounds on GSS, each writing "no second instrument is available" on the authority of `R223` — **but `R223` closed the door for `r`, the ratio of slopes, which needs ≥8 time points because `r_forced` calibrates a fifty-year shift; and since `#802` the object has been `Δgap`, which literally needs two** ⇒ **a "cannot change instrument" was carried from one estimand to another and nobody checked the carry for eight rounds** (`#777`: a limitation is valid no longer than the question it was measured against); ⚠⚠ **and my own verdict string read the NSFG result as "no resolution", which is wrong, and is `#808`②'s bug a second time**: the interval is 0.185 wide on a 4-point span ⇒ **that is a narrow zero, not a wide one** ⇒ **the correct sentence is "among women 15–49 this divide shows no measurable change between 2011-13 and 2017-19, at a resolution of ±0.09"** — **twice in three rounds means it is not a slip but a default move**; ⚠ **the two GSS stratifier specifications return opposite signs** (+0.2218 vs −0.0306, both containing 0) ⇒ **over a 6–8 year window the stratifier definition alone flips the sign**, and `G4` requires publishing it anyway — **withholding it would mean choosing a specification on the reader's behalf**; ⚠ **one post-hoc observation, labelled as post-hoc**: `#808`'s fifty-year Δgap of −1.103, spread over six years and rescaled to a 4-point span, predicts about **−0.17**, which the NSFG interval excludes ⇒ **either the widening was not uniform in time, or it does not hold for women 15–49, or the two instruments genuinely disagree — registered as next round's pre-registered question, not treated as a conclusion here** `[#810「第二具仪器有了」]` (Entry 810) 〔NSFG · GSS — **two instruments**〕 ⟨polarity control: after alignment all three arms show the population becoming more permissive ⇒ the coding assumption holds; positive control planted at −0.400 ⇒ recovered **−0.5213 [−0.6329,−0.4139]**; negative control with a gap constant by construction ⇒ **+0.0221 [−0.0918,+0.1264], containing 0** (⚠ here the reference really is 0); ⚠⚠ **both synthetic controls failed on the first run and what they caught was my own generator** — I built `v1` from wave 0's stratum labels and then returned a freshly randomised `k1` ⇒ Δgap **was identically +0.5 regardless of what I planted**; **a negative and a positive control returning the same wrong value point at the generator, not at the quantity under test**; ⚠ three things this round could not fix: `SAMESEX`'s question number moved `IH-3`→`IH-1` (order effects, **affecting the NSFG side only**), the age floor 15 vs 18 does not match, and **neither synthetic control passes through `terc` ⇒ they do not exercise the stratification step, so the controls do not cover the whole path**⟩|

**And of the three worlds `#810` left standing, one could be separated using GSS alone — so the fifty years were cut into decades.** **The result: in the eighties the divide did not measurably move; in the nineties it opened by −0.629 [−0.860, −0.402]; in the two-thousands by a further −0.476 [−0.705, −0.238]; and by the twenty-tens it had stopped again.** ⇒ **A sentence about people, far more concrete than "it tripled over fifty years": on homosexuality, the distance between devout Americans and everyone else did not grow slowly across fifty years — it has its own decades, and it split open in the nineties and the two-thousands.** ⚠ That the two decades sum to roughly the total is **a coincidence that must not be read as precision: the decade slices do not tile the timeline, and by construction were never meant to sum.** ⚠⚠ **And my own verdict string contained a P6 error, corrected in the same round**: I wrote that GSS's 2010s was "inconsistent" with NSFG's narrow zero — **wrong. GSS's interval entirely contains NSFG's; they are not inconsistent, that GSS cell simply lacks resolution.** `UNRESOLVED` is neither an acquittal nor a conviction, and I read it as a conviction. **That is the same default move in its third form — of the three values I only ever genuinely use two.** `[#812「这条鸿沟不是长出来的」]`
| **The intervals reached the page — and all four hits for "fifty years without moving" are the retraction itself** | **`#808`① landed**: `#802`'s row had carried only a direction for six rounds (3 of 8 narrowed, 5 of 8 widened), and the eight intervals (`fit`, year-cluster resampling, B = 3000, **all eight excluding 0**) are now attached to it — **`#807`'s rule: computed but not on the page means the round did not land**; **`#808`③ checked and owes nothing**: a lexical scan hits 4 places (2 per version), **and classifying each by the structural population puts all 4 inside `#808`'s own units — the retraction quoting the sentence it retracts** | ⚠⚠ **worth recording on its own, because this is `#798`'s trap appearing a second time in the same position and not being stepped into**: **a retraction necessarily writes the retracted wording onto the page** ⇒ **any lexical scan will report the retraction as a residue**; `#798` escaped it only by writing "count 'retracting it' separately from 'using it'" before the run, and this round used the same control (side assigned by owning entry number) ⇒ **an audit rule's default failure direction is to report the correction itself as outstanding, and it can only appear once you have already fixed the thing — that is, it bites precisely in the round where you got it right**; ⚠ **what could not be fixed**: `#808`②'s missing bucket is still missing — the repair is to the **dimensionality** of the verdict (per-specification verdict × whether the specifications agree, two dimensions), not an added `else`, **registered as a design problem rather than pretended solved in passing** `[#809「区间上页了」]` (Entry 809) 〔the page itself〕 ⟨this round is **Production and says so — it produces no new judgement about people**; **it reports no effect**, only whether the correction landed⟩|

**And after eight consecutive rounds, an observation about me had more leverage than any question about the world: every one of those rounds wrote "no second instrument is available", on the authority of `R223` —** **but `R223` closed the door for `r`, the ratio of slopes, which needs eight time points, while the object had long since become `Δgap`, which needs two.** Re-running that search: both NSFG female waves carry "sexual relations between two same-sex adults is all right" (the same topic as GSS) together with a complete religious-attendance variable. **The second instrument had been there all along.** ⇒ **NSFG: among women 15–49, this divide shows no measurable change between 2011-13 and 2017-19 (+0.0015 [−0.0887, +0.0959], a resolution of ±0.09 on a 4-point scale).** ⚠⚠ **And my own verdict string read that as "could not measure it", which is wrong**: the interval is narrow — **"could not measure" and "measured, and it is approximately no change" are entirely different sentences** — and this is `#808`②'s bug for the second time in three rounds, **which makes it a default move rather than a slip.** ⚠ The two GSS stratifier specifications return **opposite signs** over the same window, both containing 0 — **over six to eight years, the definition of who counts as devout is by itself enough to flip the answer.** `[#810「第二具仪器有了」]`
| **The eight divides finally have intervals — and "it did not move" is the wrong sentence; it is "it moved least"** | **`#802`①, owed for six rounds, is paid here** (B = 3000, two **different** resampling schemes, each labelled, never merged or averaged — `#797` found two incompatible bootstraps coexisting on this page): under `fit` (trend over all years + **year-cluster resampling**) **all eight exclude 0** — `homosex` **−1.103 [−1.178,−0.921]**, `helpblk` **+0.628 [+0.375,+0.717]**, `spanking` −0.197 [−0.248,−0.091], `racmar` +0.169 [+0.128,+0.185], `sexeduc` −0.076 [−0.092,−0.048], `teensex` −0.285 [−0.344,−0.172], `prayer` +0.145 [+0.098,+0.172], `suicide2` +0.063 [+0.031,+0.086]; under `raw` (endpoint years + **within-cell person resampling**) `teensex` [−0.318,+0.064], `prayer` [−0.037,+0.183] and `suicide2` [−0.021,+0.095] **contain 0** ⇒ the two specifications agree on **5 of 8** ⇒ **"five widened, three narrowed" stands, and now it carries intervals** | ⚠⚠ **and `#806`③'s conjecture is refuted, in a more informative way than confirmation would have been**: last round I wrote that the three items' gaps "had barely moved at all, and 'fifty years without moving' is itself a sentence about people"; **measured, all three gaps did move (all excluding 0 under `fit`) — they simply moved least, too little for a two-endpoint reading to tell from nothing** ⇒ **the right sentence is not "did not move" but "moved least" — "did not move" says two groups walked side by side for fifty years; "moved least" says they are drifting apart too, only slowly** ⇒ **`#806`③'s trailer is withdrawn here and must not reach the page**; ⚠ **the three items where the specifications disagree are exactly the three whose `explained` blew up to ±7–±31 in `#806`** — two rounds seeing one fact from two directions: a small `Δgap` ⇒ ① amplifies noise into double digits when used as a denominator, ② cannot be resolved by a low-power endpoint specification; ⚠ **the disagreement is about power, not direction** (`raw` uses 2 years, `fit` uses 19–28, and `#802` already measured 8/8 directional agreement) — **do not read a power difference as a contradiction**; ⚠⚠ **two of my own errors, recorded in the same round**: ① **the summary logic was missing a bucket** — the three categories were defined as "all specifications agree that…", so **an item whose specifications disagree falls into none of them**, printing "moved 5, still 0, unresolved 0" against a total of 8; **three categories that look exhaustive are not**; ② **the "wide" threshold had no bite** — set to "width > the largest |Δgap| in the table" = 1.103, while the widest observed interval is 0.621 ⇒ **that category could never have fired in this round**; it is not a vacuous check (other data would fire it) **but it had no resolving power here and is not counted as evidence** `[#808「八条鸿沟终于有了区间」]` (Entry 808) 〔GSS〕 ⟨positive control: a synthetic world with a known `Δgap = +0.500` ⇒ recovered **+0.5487 [+0.4391,+0.5804]**, excluding 0; negative control: a world with a constant gap by construction ⇒ **+0.0268 [−0.0267,+0.0731], containing 0** — ⚠ **this time the reference really is 0**, unlike `#801`/`#805` where it was **1.0**, **which is why the question "should this zero be zero?" has to be asked afresh every round**; ⚠ spans of 19–28 years and 2–5 categories are listed side by side with "÷ years ÷ span" reported alongside, and **the verdict uses only the three-valued classification, never a cross-item ranking**⟩|
| **"never ran on it" and "cannot run on it" mean opposite things to a reader** | **structural enumeration of the population** (334 / 305 units per version: table rows carrying `(Entry N)` plus the narrative spans between adjacent inline anchors — **recall is 1 by construction**): 19 hits per version on the top pair — 6 on the limitation side, 1 already annotated, **12 still bare** ⇒ **24 across both versions**; after the annotation landed, the same instrument re-run gives **0 bare, 13 annotated per version** | ⚠⚠ **what this round is about is not "24 annotations added" but what those 24 annotations say**: `#801` had written on the page that the control **"never ran"** on this pair (**the register of a debt**, which a reader takes as "it will be filled later"), while `#806` measured `sexeduc` 42%/31% and `racmar` 72%/71% — both calibration arms over the 30% reachable-range limit ⇒ zero usable cells ⇒ **it did not fail to run, it cannot run** — **"a debt" and "structurally impossible" mean opposite things to a reader: one says the road will open later, the other says there is no road, and the page used the first register for six straight rounds**; ⚠⚠ **and the methodological gain is the third control added here**: audits like `#798` carried only "catches what should be caught" and "does not catch what should not be", and **with both passing the criterion may still be unable to tell "fixed" from "untouched"** — because nobody had ever checked that **adding the annotation changes the verdict** ⇒ hence **positive control ②: the same place, once annotated, must become `MARKED`** ⇒ **an audit rule needs at least three controls: it catches what it should, it spares what it should, and the verdict actually moves once the thing is fixed; the third is the only one that proves the ruler moves, and it is the easiest to omit because the first two passing feels like enough** `[#807「「从来没跑到」与「跑不了」对读者的意思是相反的」]` (Entry 807) 〔the page itself〕 ⟨this round is **Production and says so — it produces no new judgement about people**; the kill fired **24 / 0** on the first run and passed **0 / 0** after the annotation landed; ⚠ `#805`'s generalised sentence had both its hits **on the limitation side** ⇒ already narrowed to `homosex` in `#806` itself, nothing owed; ⚠ **what this round could not fix**: the annotation is one sentence copied 24 times, which **buys consistency and also buys noise** — the better shape is a "capability boundary" block at the top of the page with 24 pointers into it, **and the page has no such block, registered as next round's structural problem rather than pretended solved**⟩|
| **Changing sides was not made by the scale (0.6%) — and the top pair is out of this design's reach** | **the common-shift control extended to all eight items** (18 cells, all reported; 6 of 8 items have usable cells): `helpblk` (5 categories) has the gap moving **−0.344 → +0.333, crossing zero**, while under a common shift the predicted end gap is **−0.352, still on the original side**; neither cell reproduces the crossing, and the **bootstrap reproduction rate over 1000 draws is 0.6%/0.7%**, `explained` = **−0.011 [−0.033, +0.008]**; `spanking` +0.008 to +0.036 with all four cells excluding 1; `homosex` +0.429 [+0.242,+0.754] and +0.557 [+0.311,+0.949] ⇒ **"the devout went from behind to ahead on this question" stands, and it now has a control** | ⚠⚠ **the same grid delivered three unwelcome things**: ① **the top pair (`sexeduc` 42%/31%, `racmar` 72%/71%) has both calibration arms over the 30% reachable-range limit ⇒ zero usable cells — this design **structurally** cannot reach it, and the page's entire `r` story rests on that pair; `#801` wrote "the ceiling control never ran on this pair", and **only now is it clear that it cannot run**, registered as structurally impossible rather than as a debt**; ② **`teensex` [−13.2,+13.5], `suicide2` [−30.9,+28.1], `prayer` [−7.5,+6.8] — their denominators (`gap1−gap0`) are small to begin with, so the ratio amplifies noise into double digits (`#799`'s denominator amplifier) ⇒ not one of their point estimates may be reported (`#783`'s naked point estimate), registered as "this design has no resolution on these three"**; ③ **`#805`'s "both are present" is a property of `homosex`, not a general one** — on `spanking` and `helpblk` a common shift explains essentially nothing ⇒ **that sentence, "even if they changed by the same amount their answers would drift apart", must be narrowed to `homosex` and may not be written as a general law** `[#806「换边不是刻度造的」]` (Entry 806) 〔GSS〕 ⟨negative control `identity_control` referenced against **1.0, not 0**, +1.0473 (tolerance 0.08); positive control (different Δ) +0.3897 < 0.80; the positive control does not fire at g=0; ⚠ the 7 dropped item×arm pairs and the 2 wholly unanswerable items are **all named explicitly, never silently truncated**; ⚠ the intervals remain **lower bounds** (no year-to-year variation); ⚠ shared thresholds are an assumption and **DIF is structurally untestable**; ⚠ category counts [2,4,5], **no ranking across them**⟩ ⚠⚠ **appended by `#814` (neither round's conclusion changes; what changes is the strength it may claim)**: the phrase "all controls sound" on this row **does not hold** — that round drew its **negative control once**, and `#814` redrew it 200 times to measure its own 95% noise half-width at **0.29–0.57**, against which the tolerance of 0.08 is only **0.14×–0.28×** (same verdict at both synthetic sample sizes) ⇒ **that negative control fired at random: neither passing nor failing carried information** ⇒ **the correct statement is "the negative control had no resolution; the positive and g=0 controls still hold".**|

**And `#802`'s "five widened, three narrowed", owed its intervals for six rounds, is paid here — all eight `Δgap` values exclude 0 under the specification that uses every year, the largest being `homosex` at −1.103 [−1.178, −0.921] and the smallest `suicide2` at +0.063 [+0.031, +0.086].** ⚠⚠ **And the trailer I wrote last round is refuted here by my own measurement, in a more informative way than confirmation would have been**: I had guessed that `teensex`, `suicide2` and `prayer` had gaps that "barely moved in fifty years", and that this would be a sentence about people; **measured, all three did move — they simply moved least, too little for a reading built on two endpoint years to tell from nothing.** ⇒ **"Did not move" says two groups walked side by side for fifty years; "moved least" says they are drifting apart too, only slowly. The two say entirely different things to a reader, and only the second is true.** ⚠ Those three are exactly the three whose `explained` blew up to ±7–±31 in `#806` — **two rounds seeing one fact from two directions**; the disagreement is about **power**, not direction, and **a power difference must not be read as a contradiction**. ⚠ Two of my own errors are recorded here too: the three-way classification **looks exhaustive and is not** (an item whose specifications disagree lands in no bucket), and the "wide" threshold **could never have fired in this round at all**. `[#808「八条鸿沟终于有了区间」]`
| **Both are present: the scale explains part of that divide, and does not explain all of it** | **bootstrap interval on the common latent shift** (`homosex` 1974→2024, four cells n = 491/457/303/286, B = 2000, resampled within stratum-years): probit calibrated on the devout `explained` = **+0.429 [+0.240, +0.747]**; logit **+0.557 [+0.327, +0.956]** ⇒ **both cells exclude 0 and exclude 1, union [+0.240, +0.956]** ⇒ **a common latent shift explains part of the widening and cannot explain all of it** | ⚠⚠ **the criterion was replaced: `#803`'s median was +0.478 and `#804`'s screened median +0.493 — both astride the pre-registered 0.50, which is not a numerical coincidence but a threshold criterion having no resolution on a quantity that sits on the threshold** ⇒ replaced by **two independent exclusions** (excluding 1 ⇒ there is divergence; excluding 0 ⇒ the scale is contributing); ⚠⚠ **and this round's real mechanical finding is that the gate I built one round earlier misfired**: the first version used `headroom_control` to **screen**, the non-devout arm came in at 63.5% over the limit ⇒ control row FAIL ⇒ the whole round's `admissible()` = False, **even though that arm had been correctly excluded and never entered the grid** ⇒ **a screen failing means this cell is unusable; a control failing means the instrument is broken — they are not the same thing** (the same family as `#796`, this time committed against a gate I had just built myself, on its first use) ⇒ screening moved to the pure function `Gate.headroom()`, the control row now asserts "every arm that failed the screen really was excluded", **and names which one was dropped**; ⚠ **`#804` made a rule mechanical, and the mechanisation itself introduced a new failure mode — a correct rule attached to the wrong row type reports a correctly-handled problem as a broken instrument** (`P7`: after fixing a lock, attack it again) `[#805「两者都在:刻度解释了那条鸿沟的一部分,解释不完」]` (Entry 805) 〔GSS〕 ⟨negative control `identity_control` referenced against **1.0, not 0**, +0.9917 (tolerance 0.08); positive control (different Δ, +0.15/+0.60) = +0.4728 < 0.80; the positive control does not fire at g=0; ⚠ **only one calibration arm survived the screen ⇒ the "specification curve" is really 2 cells = 2 link functions, not a curve**; ⚠ **the interval is a lower bound** — resampling within stratum-years excludes year-to-year variation ⇒ the true uncertainty is only larger; ⚠ the logit cell's upper bound is **+0.956, so it excludes 1 only barely** — not to be rounded up to "cleanly excluded"; ⚠ shared thresholds are an assumption, and **DIF is structurally untestable here**⟩ ⚠⚠ **appended by `#814` (neither round's conclusion changes; what changes is the strength it may claim)**: the phrase "all controls sound" on this row **does not hold** — that round drew its **negative control once**, and `#814` redrew it 200 times to measure its own 95% noise half-width at **0.29–0.57**, against which the tolerance of 0.08 is only **0.14×–0.28×** (same verdict at both synthetic sample sizes) ⇒ **that negative control fired at random: neither passing nor failing carried information** ⇒ **the correct statement is "the negative control had no resolution; the positive and g=0 controls still hold".**|

**And `#805` had only done one item, so the next round pushed the same control across all eight — and the sharpest cell returned a clean answer.** **`helpblk` (whether government should help Black Americans) has the gap moving −0.344 → +0.333, crossing zero; under a common shift the predicted end gap is −0.352, still on the original side, and across a thousand bootstrap draws only six reproduce the crossing (0.6%).** ⇒ **A sentence about people: on government aid to Black Americans, devout Americans were not moving slowly — they changed sides.** **In 1975 they were less supportive than everyone else; in 2024 they are more supportive, and that is not an illusion of the ruler.** ⚠⚠ The same grid delivered three things I did not want, and all three are written down: ① **the top pair (`sexeduc`, `racmar`) is out of this design's reach, structurally** — both calibration arms exceed the 30% reachable-range limit, leaving zero usable cells, and the page's entire `r` story rests on that pair. `#801` wrote "the control never ran on this pair"; **only now is it clear it cannot run.** ② **three items have intervals spanning ±7 to ±31** (`teensex`, `suicide2`, `prayer`) — their denominators are small to begin with, so the ratio amplifies noise into double digits, **not one point estimate may be reported**, and they are registered as "this design has no resolution here". ③ **`#805`'s "both are present" is a property of `homosex`, not a general one** — on `spanking` and `helpblk` a common shift explains essentially nothing, **so that generalised sentence has to be narrowed back to `homosex`.** `[#806「换边不是刻度造的」]`
| **The rule was written in the ledger, and a ledger enforces nothing** | **`#801` established "no control may demand a change near the reachable limit" and set the standard at 30% of the reachable range — and one round later `#803` built the violation again** ⇒ it is now `Gate.headroom()` + `Gate.headroom_control()` in `lib/gates.py` (`HEADROOM_MAX = 0.30`, entering `_control_rows` so it directly suppresses `admissible()`); **backtested on 4 cases whose answers were known in advance: 2/2 of the should-catch caught (`#801` pre-fix 181.8%; `#803` `fit` calibrated on non-devout 89.8%), 2/2 of the should-pass passed (30.0%, 21.8%)**; ⚠ **and it rules on two cells I had never judged** (`raw` on non-devout 63.5% caught; `fit` on devout 28.0% passed) ⇒ **only two cells satisfy both "internally consistent (raw)" and the 30% ceiling: +0.429 and +0.557, median +0.493 — still astride 0.50** ⇒ **`#803`'s substantive verdict is independently reproduced by a mechanical rule rather than by my judgement** | ⚠⚠ **`#600`: a rule executed only from memory fails by default** — the same repair as `#795`; ⚠ **`lo`/`hi` must be the ends of the scale itself (e.g. 1 and 4), never the observed extremes** — observed extremes would let the ceiling float with the sample, and **saturation is a property of the scale, not of the sample**; ⚠ **two cells are not a specification curve, they are two numbers** — not to be written up as "robust"; ⚠⚠ **a real boundary, not faked**: this rule **cannot be applied retroactively across the repository** — it requires each script to declare the ends of its own scale, and not one existing script writes `lo`/`hi` in machine-readable form ⇒ **registered as "retroactive application requires the result JSONs to carry the scale ends first", rather than faking a sweep** `[#804「那条规矩写在账本里,而账本不执行任何东西」]` (Entry 804) 〔this page's own gate library〕 ⟨P6 proxy ledger: PROXY `|change| / reachable range ≤ 0.30`; **only one direction is sound — over the limit ⇒ that cell really is in the saturation regime; within the limit proves nothing about the cell being good** (it may still mix baselines, it may still lack power) ⇒ **it reports "unusable" only, and never certifies "good"**⟩|

**And `#803` and `#804` both landed astride the 0.50 threshold (+0.478, +0.493) — not a numerical coincidence but a threshold criterion having no resolution on a quantity that sits on the threshold** ⇒ the criterion was replaced by an interval. Tightened to the only internally consistent specification (raw endpoints) and screening each calibration arm against the 30% reachable-range limit, only the devout-calibrated arm survives: **`explained` = +0.429 [+0.240, +0.747] (probit) and +0.557 [+0.327, +0.956] (logit), both excluding 0 and excluding 1.** ⇒ **Both are present, and that carries more content than either pole:** **on homosexuality, part of the divide that opened between the devout and everyone else is "the same change, read from different starting points" —** **even if the two groups had changed by exactly the same amount inwardly, their survey answers would still have drifted apart; and that does not explain all of it, the rest is the two groups genuinely moving differently.** **Both are true, it is not a choice between them — which is exactly what is wrong with "the divide is splitting" and with "it is a scale artifact" taken separately.** ⚠ And this round's real mechanical finding is that **the gate I had built one round earlier misfired**: using `headroom_control` to screen made **a correctly-handled exclusion look like a broken instrument**, and the whole round was declared inadmissible on the spot. **A screen failing means this cell is unusable; a control failing means the instrument is broken.** `#804` made a rule mechanical, **and the mechanisation itself introduced a new failure mode — a correct rule attached to the wrong row type.** ⚠ Three limits are written into the artifact: only one calibration arm survived, so the "curve" is two cells; the interval is **a lower bound only** (no year-to-year variation); shared thresholds are an assumption and DIF is untestable. `[#805「两者都在」]`
| **Education makes sexual morality one position; age does it to gender roles** | education **+0.1349** / age +0.0318 on the floor | same 11,576 people; the placebo is opposite-signed and on the floor for education but +0.0906 for age — **a crossover, so not response coherence**. ⚠ **does not replicate on the second instrument** (Entry 675) 〔GSS〕 ⟨—⟩|

| **Even the one item that was not saturated cannot be decided, and this is the second time ⇒ change direction** | the three statistics disagree on direction: at terciles raw **rises**, the slope **rises**, the marginal-matched ρ **falls**; at quintiles raw **falls**, the slope **rises**, matched **falls**. And the raw ρ's spread is only **0.8×** the Gaussian null (0.75× at quintiles), while the censoring gate also fails (top stratum endpoint **60.9% ≥ 60%**) | ⚠ **both gates FAIL and both were written before the run ⇒ the verdict is UNVERIFIED, with no threshold changed and no side taken**; ⚠ the only statistic monotone under both cuts is the **marginal-matched ρ, and it falls** (.2648→.1534; .2672→.1218) ⇒ **the "`premarsx` runs the other way" flagged in the row above is not a finding**; ⚠ **the three statistics are immune to different things** — raw ρ eats sd(Y), the slope does not but eats censoring, and marginal matching flattens Y's distribution, **so where they disagree is precisely where "what is this interaction defined on" remains unsettled**; ⚠⚠ **a second consecutive UNVERIFIED on the same question triggers the change-direction rule, and the interaction line is suspended here** (Entry 773) 〔GSS〕 ⟨raw spread **0.8×** its own Gaussian null; endpoint share **60.9%** against a pre-registered 60%⟩|

pinning it to **1974's line** and recomputing all eight: `sexeduc` 2.19→**1.93**, `racmar` 1.84→**1.80**, `spanking` 0.46→0.49, `suicide2` 0.41→0.45, `homosex` 0.41→0.35, `teensex` 0.36→0.29, `prayer` 0.13→−0.05, `helpblk` 0.10→0.08 — **8/8 moves stay inside their own bootstrap width and the three clumps survive intact** ⚠ **likewise: with "three clumps" retracted in `#797`, what this row establishes is that the line's drift does not change the conclusion** | ⚠⚠ **this design choice has been in every round since `#776` and the ledger never once named it**; `#779` pinned birth cohort, which **cannot pin the stratification line**; ⚠ `#778`'s two stratifications look like a control but **it is a confounded one** — (a) and (b) differ on "absolute vs relative" *and* "one item vs three" at once; ⚠ the verdict is the **third outcome** written into the prediction matrix and is stronger than "the line held still": **the line moved and the estimand is insensitive to it**; ⚠⚠ **the first version was killed on the spot by the positive control (|diff| 4.94e−02)**: `pd.qcut` bins right-closed `(a,b]` so the top bin is `> q67` while I wrote `>= q67`, **and `REL` is a mean of three ordinal z-scores, so the boundary is thick with ties** — once fixed, |diff| is **exactly 0.0000**; ⚠ noise is controlled by comparing each move against **its own** bootstrap width, **but under an absolute threshold the devout stratum shrinks over time and that width inflates ⇒ this test leans toward "no movement"**, stated as such (Entry 794) 〔GSS〕 ⟨drift −0.302 SD clears the pre-registered 0.25 threshold; **8/8 inside width**⟩ ⚠ **and since `#806` this must carry one more sentence: the "common latent shift" control is **out of this design's reach** for the top pair — both calibration arms exceed the 30% reachable-range limit (`sexeduc` 42%/31%, `racmar` 72%/71%) ⇒ zero usable cells, **structurally impossible, not a debt**.**|
| **With the population defined structurally, exactly one row is affected — and the criterion was wrong again, the third time in this session** | enumerated **91 claims** in the main table (204 table rows; completeness checked as exact equality, **excluding 2 struck-through retracted rows, both printed in full for audit**); the substitution test gives **USE strict 9, lenient 12** against a threshold of 10 ⇒ **the decision straddles the threshold**; under the corrected criterion **1 row is affected** | ⚠ **no longer defining the population by vocabulary**: `#792`'s lesson was not "the word list is too narrow" but that **defining a semantic population by a word list does not work at all** — I have only 1 known USE, and **a recall estimate with a sample size of 1 is not a recall estimate**; ⇒ define the population **structurally**, and recall is 1 by construction; ⚠⚠ **and "the decision depends on me" is what forced me to check why — at which point the criterion turned out to be wrong**: the substitution test asks "does this row **use** the cut?" while the decision asks "has this row's premise **fallen**?" — `#789` overturned a **specific estimand** (the devout/non-devout rate-of-change ratio on GSS) and **did not** overturn education effects, domain cohesion or facet structure, most of which live on MFQ/MSSCQ/SCCS; ⇒ **the strict/lenient disagreement across the threshold came not from my latitude but from my measuring the wrong thing**; ⚠ third instance this session: `#791` counted pairs where the question was structural, `#792` had a positive control sharing the sieve's blind spot, and this round's criterion asked "used" where the decision asked "fallen" — **none of the three was an arithmetic error; each substituted an adjacent quantity for the one being judged**; ⚠ the completeness check caught two self-inflicted defects: a regex requiring the closing paren to follow the digits (4 rows missed) and a completeness criterion written as ≥80% (**which called it complete with 4 rows missing**) (Entry 793) 〔the README itself〕 ⟨all 91 claims enumerated; `#792` **guessed right** while this round **settled it** — that round used a sieve with zero recall, and **guessing right is not the same as settling**⟩|
| **The positive control passed while the sieve was blind to the very thing I was about to claim** | counting how many lines still organise the page by "sexual vs non-sexual": the sieve hit **40 lines**, hand-classified as **USE 0, DISCUSS 1, unverified 36**; **positive control ① (can it see `#789`/`#791`?) passed, positive control ② (can it find `#785`, a line I already know is a USE?) failed — 0 lines** | ⚠⚠ **the vocabulary says `性道德(?:的|是)?(?:一|特殊|不特殊)` while that line says "关于性道德的规律"** — `的` is followed by 规, not 一/特殊, so it is missed; **this is the second instance of that row in `realstat`** — "a positive control asks *can this instrument see?* and never asks *is what it sees the thing I am about to claim about?*" — **and here the shared blind spot is nameable: the sieve looks for lines that DISCUSS the cut (they write "non-sexual" as a word) while I need lines that USE it (they only say "sexual morality") — the two use opposite language**; ⚠ the confound control written before the run saved the round: **classifying USE/DISCUSS/MENTION, without which the lines that overturn the cut would have been counted as lines resting on it**; ⚠ one self-inflicted defect fixed in passing: the verdict string hard-coded "only 0 lines (the `#785` one)" — **the computation says 0 while the prose says it is in there** (Entry 792) 〔the README itself〕 ⟨40 lines hit, USE 0, 36 unverified ⇒ **neither number may be used to decide a reorganisation**⟩|
| **Not an axis — three clumps; and explaining it is arithmetically out of reach** ⚠⚠ **"three clumps" is retracted in `#797` — the current shape is "one pair plus six"; what stands here is that the top pair separates from everything else, and the MDE** | **MDE**: at n=8 the permutation null's 95th percentile is |ρ|=**0.714** and the 80%-power **MDE is |ρ| ≈ 0.90** ⇒ **no candidate explanatory variable could be detected here**; **distinguishable pairs**: joint year bootstrap with family-wide BH gives **16/28** in the main cell and `[16,18,16,16,16,18,16,17]` across the 8-cell specification curve; **and by clump: 0/8 within, 16/20 between** — top `sexeduc`+`racmar` (~1.8–2.2), middle `homosex`+`teensex`+`suicide2`+`spanking` (~0.36–0.46), bottom `prayer`+`helpblk` (~0.11–0.13) | ⚠⚠ **first, what could not be done**: the row above required candidates to be written down *before* seeing the ordering, **and I had already seen it** ⇒ any candidate would be written to the answer, **and marking an impossible criterion "planned" is forbidden** ⇒ this round did only the two things that need no candidate; ⚠ the bootstrap must be **joint** — the eight items ask the same people in the same years, so **resampling each item independently erases the between-item correlation and overstates distinguishability**; ⚠⚠ **the pre-registration counted pairs and never asked what those pairs looked like** — listed out, the pattern is not a gradient but three clumps, **the "verdict tests the wrong question" family, this time in the criterion's own unit**; ⚠ "should this zero be zero?" was asked: **yes** — under the null that all eight share one true ratio the difference has expectation exactly 0 ⇒ `negative_control`, not `offset_control` (Entry 791) 〔GSS〕 ⟨negative control 0/28, positive control 21/28; **confound control**: corr(distinguishable, |Δr|)=**+0.779** vs corr(distinguishable, summed width)=+0.324 — without it, "16/28" might have been nothing but "narrowest against widest"⟩ ⚠ **and since `#806` this must carry one more sentence: the "common latent shift" control is **out of this design's reach** for the top pair — both calibration arms exceed the 30% reachable-range limit (`sexeduc` 42%/31%, `racmar` 72%/71%) ⇒ zero usable cells, **structurally impossible, not a debt**.**|
| **The instability comes from the denominator, so n=8 is a hard ceiling: resistance can only be measured against change that actually happened** ⚠⚠ **mechanism refined in `#799`**: the denominator is noise's **amplifier**, not a push ⇒ what this row establishes is that **the interval widens**, **not that the point estimate is pushed up** | regressing `log(interval width)` on two pre-specified quantities at once: **denominator signal-to-noise −0.681 [−0.903, −0.466], excluding 0**; number of year-points +0.101 [−0.239, +0.410], **containing 0**; the two are separable (`corr = −0.324`) | ⚠ positive control: **shrinking the denominator to 0.25× must widen the interval** — `homosex` 0.22→0.90, `spanking` 0.44→1.80, **the only thing in this round that shows I am measuring a denominator effect and not something else**; ⇒ **those twelve items are unstable not for want of years but because society moved too little on them ⇒ year-window surgery cannot rescue them** — **not a power problem: the estimand simply has no definition where society did not move**; ⚠ no p-values — the coefficient's **own bootstrap interval** (`#782`'s lesson: two quantities each being significant says nothing about the stability of their relation); ⚠ **the two candidates are not exhaustive** — a third source (e.g. the devout stratum's own slope magnitude) is not in the model, **so this is not a complete decomposition** (Entry 790) 〔GSS〕 ⟨20 items that moved; 4 where society itself did not: `xmarsex`, `gunlaw`, `letdie1`, `abnomore`⟩|

⚠ the refusals split in two and **only one kind is my fault**: ① `homosex` has **five categories with `other` last**, and I passed all of them instead of the first four, so the high pole read as `other` ⇒ **the instrument is fine; I fed it wrong**; ② **for binary policy items the direction lives in the question stem, not the labels** — `yes` in `abany` is **permissive** while `yes` in `racmar` (should there be laws against racial intermarriage) is **restrictive**, **the same word with opposite moral polarity** ⇒ **adding `yes/no` to the vocabulary would make it return a confident direction, which is precisely what it exists to prevent**; ⇒ **the first time this project has been blocked by an instrument boundary rather than a data boundary** — a data boundary calls for different data, **an instrument boundary calls for a different reading** (next round: hand-coded polarity with the question stem quoted, stored separately from the machine reading) (Entry 788) 〔GSS〕 ⟨the positive control failed immediately and said so: this round's instrument is not the same one `#786` used; | **The item is already at its ceiling among the most devout, so that direction is the scale hitting a wall, not a relation** | in the most devout tercile **87.8%** say extramarital sex is "always wrong" (`teensex` 82.2%, `homosex` 80.5%), and the sd falls from 0.856 to **0.542** ⇒ **the pre-registered censoring threshold of 60% is breached and no strength-of-relation reading is legible in that stratum** | ⚠⚠ **this dissolves the two-instrument disagreement in the row above, and the direction is that they never disagreed about the world** — GSS uses a single four-point item that saturates at 88% among the devout, while **MFQ's authority score is a continuous multi-item mean with flat sd (0.783→0.838) and no ceiling** ⇒ MFQ's side is legible and GSS's is not; ⚠ **the variance-robust statistics are still reported but marked unreadable per the pre-registration** (OLS slope for `xmarsex` +0.1170→+0.0176; marginal-matched ρ +0.1909→+0.0801); ⚠ **`premarsx` is the only item not at its ceiling** (endpoint 46.4%, sd **rising** 0.888→1.252, slope **not falling** +0.1495→+0.1863) — **three indicators agreeing, and this round does not explain it**; ⚠⚠ two of my own defects, both in the gate: comparing a constant with itself (the library's `_degenerate` **only catches all-zeros**, now annotated) and putting a must-fail inverted check inside the same Gate (recorded in `#767`, **third occurrence**) (Entry 772) 〔GSS〕〔MFQ〕 ⟨endpoint share **87.8%** against a pre-registered 60%; post-matching marginal gap **0.0001** against 0.1194 before⟩|
| **The first non-derivation quantity is the interaction — and its direction was overturned by a second instrument** | within religiosity terciles, ρ(obey, `xmarsex`) falls from **+0.2078** to **+0.0655**; all four items' spreads exceed a **trivariate Gaussian null matched on the same correlation matrix** (**1.08×–2.60×**), and since a Gaussian's conditional correlation does not vary with the conditioning variable, **this part is not a function of second moments** | ⚠ **the first non-derivation quantity this project has found on this line** (against `#770`, where the share is a derivation); ⚠⚠ **but the magnitude is unreadable, and that was written before the run** — the Gaussian null removes the difference stratification itself creates, not the real change in variance across strata: `xmarsex`'s attitude sd falls from 0.856 to **0.542 (−37%)**, because **the most devout agree with one another and there is less variance left to correlate** ⇒ what is supported is that the interaction exists, not how large it is; ⚠ `premarsx` at **1.08× sits on its null and does not count**, and it is also the only **non-monotone** item (+0.1759→+0.1267→+0.1970), **running against the other three**; ⚠ terciles were my cut and a range-based spread is sensitive to the number of strata (Entry 771) 〔GSS〕 ⟨the observed spread exceeds the Gaussian null by **18.1×** its own spread; per item **1.08×–2.60×**⟩|
| **The explained share is a derivation, not a measurement, and I wrote five rounds on it** | the closed form and the numerical pipeline agree to **1.9e−16** (machine precision), so the share is a deterministic function of `(r_XY, r_XZ, r_YZ)`; and **a bivariate Gaussian matching only those two correlations** reproduces the same share to within **0.89pp** despite a skew differing by **0.38** | ⚠⚠ **this retracts "the largest single explanation is something we have not measured"** — the "residual" is just the partial correlation on another scale, **not a quantity about the world**; what remains sayable is "no measured covariate's correlations are large enough to flatten this one"; ⚠ **the correlations themselves remain measurements**, and "religion explains a third" is the same sentence as "religiosity correlates +0.27 with valuing obedience and +0.42–0.47 with sexual attitudes"; ⚠ the check can fail: a **deliberately denominator-less** formula differs by **0.0305**; ⚠ the single-instrument exemption differs from earlier rounds — **the claim is about the statistic, and changing dataset cannot make an identity truer**; ⚠⚠ two defects of the same shape, both caught by `lib/gates.py` rather than by me: **comparing a difference against zero can never fail** (the right shape is comparing the two values), and feeding a numpy array to an indexed Series correlation returns all-NaN (the third instance of `#750`'s family) (Entry 770) 〔GSS〕 ⟨analytic vs numerical **1.9e−16**; synthetic vs real **0.89pp** at a skew gap of 0.38⟩|
| **The entanglement ordering should not have been mine, and once measured it cannot be resolved** | regressing each item's share on **its own bivariate |ρ| with lifetime partners**: **slope +0.2982** (the share does rise with semantic entanglement), **intercept at zero entanglement 1.9%, 95% interval [−2.2%, 6.2%]** | ⚠ **the pre-registered branch fires honestly: the intercept's interval is 8.4pp wide against the hand-ordered 6.6pp above ⇒ this replaces "what I picked" with "what cannot be resolved", not a tightening**, and the row above keeps [5.8%, 12.4%]; ⚠ **but the positive slope is real** ⇒ ordering by entanglement was itself right, and `premarsx`'s 12.2% is partly semantic overlap; ⚠ **the interval contains 0** ⇒ once the overlap is stripped, own lifetime sexual history becomes indistinguishable from nothing; ⚠ four points and two parameters leave **2 degrees of freedom**, and the intercept is an **extrapolation** to a point never observed, with linearity untestable here; ⚠⚠ my first version of the instrument check was vacuous (a least-squares residual sum is identically 0) and **`lib/gates.py`'s `_degenerate` refused it on the spot** — the first time this gate library has stopped me rather than someone else (Entry 769) 〔GSS〕 ⟨zero-entanglement intercept **1.9%** against its own 95% interval width of **8.4pp**⟩|
| **Policing other people's sex is a little bit about what you yourself did in a lifetime, and almost nothing about last year** | a person's **last-year** sexual conduct (partners · frequency · infidelity) explains only **1.9%**; switching to **lifetime partners** gives **9.1%**, a factor of **4.8**; and on the item least semantically entangled with it (attitudes to homosexuality) it is **5.8%** ⇒ **own lifetime sexual history [5.8%, 12.4%]**. For comparison: politics **[6.8%, 11.6%]**, religion **[33.3%, 46.7%]** | ⚠ per hard rule ③ this is not a fourth candidate from the same matrix of social categories but **a different kind of variable**; ⚠⚠ **the first arm used the wrong instrument, which I found and repaired within the round**: for a married fifty-year-old "partners last year" is identically 1, while the folk theory is about a lifetime; ⚠⚠ **and the second arm carries a confound I did not write before the run and am stating after it**: someone with many partners has almost certainly had premarital sex, so `lifetime`×`premarsx` overlaps semantically, which is why the least-entangled cell is quoted; ⚠ all conduct measures are self-reported and **the condemning may under-report ⇒ both 1.9% and 5.8% are lower bounds** (a direction that works against my own conclusion); ⚠ the methodological claim was re-tested on NSFG: **lifetime is stronger on 3 of 4 items with one reversal (`sxok16`, 0.43×) ⇒ pre-registered verdict "not a universal rule"**, and NSFG **top-codes lifetime partners at 50** on a sample of women 15–44, both of which compress the contrast (Entry 768) 〔GSS〕〔NSFG〕 ⟨lifetime vs last year **4.8×**; partialling real sexual history is **407.3×** its own spread⟩|
| **The interval barely moved, and I found I had copied a number from a different run into the ledger** | McDonald's ω = **0.7352** > α = 0.7287, tightening the interval to religion **[33.3%, 46.7%]**, residual **[49.4%, 63.3%]**, residual/religion **[1.06×, 1.90×]** — **still containing 1, so the retraction stands** | ⚠⚠ **what this round actually found is about the ledger**: the α = 0.4804 in `#765` was copied from the **pre-fix** run, while the persisted artifact says **0.7287** — **the shares are unaffected; only the number I quoted was wrong**, the second instance of `#726`'s family; ⚠⚠ **and that mis-quote measured something more valuable**: the same scale gives α = 0.4804 on the truncated sample and 0.7287 on the full one ⇒ **the filter defect also cut the scale's internal consistency**, so "small consequence" holds only for the shares; ⚠ on MFQ's six-item purity scale, truncating each item in turn drops α in **6/6 cells with ω moving the same way ⇒ the direction replicates**, but by only −0.015…−0.051, **making GSS's −0.248 five times any cell ⇒ direction transfers, magnitude does not**; ⚠ politics has only two items ⇒ **ω is structurally unidentifiable** (N/A: it would require a third political indicator); ⚠ I put a **deliberately inverted** check inside the same `Gate`, making its summary unreadable — an inverted check must be run separately (Entry 767) 〔GSS〕〔MFQ〕 ⟨truncation Δα: GSS **−0.248** vs MFQ **−0.015…−0.051**⟩|
| **My filter deleted the reference group, and what actually bit was the controls' reliability** | the filter: `attend` code 0 = never (**n=14,883, the largest single category**) was deleted wholesale by my `.where(x>0)` ⇒ fixing it raises n from 9,580 to **12,151** and moves the residual 62.4%→**61.0%**; reliability: religion α **0.7287** (⚠ this page first said 0.4804, which was measured on the **pre-fix** sample; corrected in `#767`), politics SB **0.5965** ⇒ correcting moves religion 33.3%→**47.1%**, the residual 63.3%→**49.0%**, and **residual/religion 1.90×→1.04×** | ⚠⚠ **this retracts `#764`'s "the residual is 1.89× religion"** — corrected, the two are the same size; ⚠ **the honest form is an interval, not another point**: α under-estimates reliability for heterogeneous scales, so the correction overshoots, and it overshoots in the direction that shrinks the residual ⇒ religion **[33.3%, 47.1%]**, residual **[49.0%, 63.3%]**, residual/religion **[1.04×, 1.90×]**, **which contains 1, so "much larger" is unsupported while "the residual is still the largest single component" holds at both ends**; ⚠ the exposure was measured and confined: 13 scripts repo-wide use `x>0`, only `#762` touched a variable with a valid zero, and `R112`/`R113` and the MFQ arm were checked clean; ⚠ an internal consistency check I had not designed came out right: the political composite explains only 6.8% raw — less than the single indicator's 11.5% — and **the reliability correction returns it to 11.6%** (Entry 765) 〔GSS〕 ⟨the correction's shrinkage is **22.6×** its own spread, and the corrected matrix is positive-definite in all four cells⟩|
| **Landing twice inside a band I picked myself, where the problem was not the threshold's height but the question's form** (⚠ **its "the residual is 1.89× religion" is retracted by the next row** — corrected for the controls' reliability it is **1.04×**, the two being the same size; "the residual is 6.6× politics" becomes 4.24×, and **that half stands**) | rewritten, both historical rounds become decidable: in `#754` authority is **14.0×** its own null and ingroup **4.9×**, the former 2.9× the latter; in `#762` religion is **3.50×** politics, while **the residual is 1.89× religion and 6.61× politics** | ⚠⚠ **this is not relaxing a threshold after the fact** — both old verdicts remain correct as answers to "does retention clear 75%"; what is retired is **the form of the question**, marked RETIRED-AS-BADLY-POSED and **not OVERTURNED**; ⚠ the new form decides not by being more permissive but by comparing only **measured quantities** against **their own nulls**, which **requires me to pick no number at all**; ⚠ P6: a share that is small and close to its own null does establish that it does not dominate (reliable); a large share does **not** establish causation — explained share is correlational, not a causal decomposition (Entry 764) 〔GSS〕〔MFQ〕 ⟨the residual is **6.61×** politics and **1.89×** religion⟩|

| **This data cannot support the question of direction, and only computing the MDE first revealed it** | after detrending, the contemporaneous cross-correlation is **+0.6426** (the highest), while the asymmetry (lag+1 − lag−1) is **−0.1295**, only **0.20×** its own null's 95th percentile of **0.6462** | ⚠⚠ **−0.1295 must not be read as "which one leads"** — it sits below its own noise floor; ⚠ the person level is **structurally** unidentifiable: GSS is a repeated cross-section, not a panel, and each person is asked once, so **it would require panel data** (recorded as N/A plus what it would require, per realstat §2, **never as "planned"**); ⚠ the decade level has only **22 time points**, and injecting lead strengths of 0.1→0.8 yields detection rates of only 0.044→0.551, so **80% power is unreachable anywhere on the grid**; ⚠ and the residual sd / sampling se ratio is 2.53–3.11×, so **there is real signal in the residuals — the problem is not that only noise remains, it is that there are too few time points** (Entry 757) 〔GSS〕 ⟨**0.20×** — the observed asymmetry against its own null's 95th percentile⟩|

| ⚠ **And the row above now carries a live alternative** | the per-stratum **standard-deviation trajectory** of `homosex`: rarely/never 1.321 → 1.131 (**slope −0.00424**) · occasional +0.00109 · **frequent 0.862 → 1.373 (+0.01191)** — **the stratum whose variance rose most is exactly the stratum that coupled most** | ⇒ **under the criterion written before the run, `#745`'s coupling gradient is downgraded**; ⚠ and the next round's attempt to rescue it came back **undecidable**: a simulation holding ρ constant while only each stratum's real marginals move gives **−0.00284 / −0.00225 / −0.00151 — all negative**, meaning the marginal trajectories on their own would make normalised coupling *fall*, while *observed minus simulated* is **+0.00240 / +0.00403 / +0.00438**, so **the gradient is larger, not smaller**; **but the gate returns UNVERIFIED because it compares |null| against |effect| (0.00151 is 53% of 0.00287), and that comparison is blind to sign.** ⇒ **the downgrade stands: neither restored nor retracted** `[#748「模拟指向反方向,而闸只会比大小」]` (Entry 748): it may be a variance difference; ⚠ **and my pre-registered directional prediction was backwards** (I expected the *rarely/never* group to be the one passing through the middle of the scale) — **the measurement corrected the reasoning** (Entry 745 · 747) 〔GSS〕 ⟨**downgraded, no ratio reported**〕|
| **The ones who merged them are the churchgoers** | ceiling-normalised: **frequent +0.00287 · occasional +0.00178 · rarely/never −0.00044**; the gap between the ends is **+0.00331/yr = +0.1191 over 36 years** | each stratum judged against **its own** null: frequent and occasional sit **outside** it, **rarely/never sits inside** ([−0.00196, +0.00190]); the placebo (random three-way split) gives +0.00563 = 84%, so **stratification alone costs 16% while religion costs 36%**; ⚠ **a difference, not a ratio** — a ratio is meaningless when the denominator is near zero (`#691`) (Entry 740 · 745) 〔GSS〕 ⟨**gap +0.1191/36yr**〕|
| **Nor is it age, nor education** | pooled within fixed age bands **+0.00640 = 95% of the within-cohort slope** (the three bands give +0.0069 / +0.0054 / +0.0069); within education terciles **+0.00521 = 77%** | ⚠ **inside a birth cohort `age = year − cohort` is an identity**, so `#740`'s within-cohort slope is by construction period *and* age combined; the placebo (stratifying by sex) gives +0.00610 = 91%, **so stratification itself costs only ~9%**; the null (years shuffled within age band) is [−0.00082, +0.00079]; ⚠ **(age × education) clears 0 of 9 cells and (cohort × education) 1 of 18 ⇒ undecidable, kept out of the conclusion** (Entry 740 · 743) 〔GSS〕 ⟨**8.1×**〕|

| And the family seven are not a domain | largest sub-block **4/7** | 4 at all four thresholds; positive control 3; **placebo (rows shuffled) gives 1** ⇒ the null is 1 (Entry 650 · 734) 〔NSFG〕 ⟨**4.00×**〕|

**°⃠** = **supplying a null would show nothing**: at that n the null is already about as wide as the recorded value ⇒ **structurally uncheckable, which does not mean the claim is wrong** (Entry 710 / `#710`).

**°** = **null not paired**: that claim's null is not in its round's script output (no `results/*.json`, or a JSON without a null) ⇒ **whether the null belongs to that effect cannot be checked**. Across the page, **7 of 24 = 29%** are pairable (Entry 695).

## What this cannot do

| Cannot | Why |
|---|---|
| Decide whether rarity drives condemnation at the **decade** unit | after differencing, condemnation of extramarital sex and support for the death penalty still move together at **+0.57**; dropping 2021/2022 does not remove it (Entry 659 · 660) |
| Replicate "learning by watching adults" across instruments | **not one of five instruments ever asked it** (Entry 645) |
| Carry the society-side contrast to the person side | 11-point ordinal against binary — **different scales** (Entry 646) |
| Say for which societies "+0.845 across targets" holds | SCCS has 186 societies — it cannot yield 10 strata of ≥30, and the gradient ruler has no resolution below ~10 strata (Entry 674) |
| Promote "education makes sexual morality one position" into a claim about *sex* or *purity* | on the second instrument purity's education gradient is **−0.0535**, on the floor and opposite in sign — **it does not replicate**; and only purity blocks there, so that instrument has no legal placebo (Entry 675) |
| Split "compression" into less-extremity and more-consistency on GSS | 27 of its 33 items are binary and extremity is undefined on a binary item; only six four-point items remain (Entry 689) |
| See, at the society level, whether this axis relates to anything outside itself | n tops out at 97 (the five-block intersection), and **after shuffling the between-block similarity still sits at about 0.15 and does not shrink with n** ⇒ a **halo floor**; at n=61 the axis-versus-external MDE is **0.2581** and the observations straddle it ⇒ **any relation below about 0.26 is structurally invisible here** (Entry 702) |
| Re-ask E01's rarity-versus-condemnation on another instrument | measured library by library: **outside BKS the maximum is 3** matched base-rate×condemnation pairs (GSS); YRBS has **0** condemnation items and MFQ/RWAS have **0** behaviour items ⇒ **no second instrument has this structure**, while BKS has **68** (Entry 703) |
| Any causal claim | all cross-sectional · no intervention · self-report |

## Before reading any number here

| | |
|---|---|
| **The gate now catches "no null reported", not "a null that does not belong to this effect"** | the new `effect_without_null` rule is merged (verified on the real gate: one planted entry blocked, one passed); **201** historical entries are named but not blocked | ⚠ it cannot judge whether the reported null belongs to that effect — precisely why the audit failed, and undecidable automatically on a plain-text ledger (Entry 694) |
| **Most numbers on this page have no null behind them** | of the 93 ledger entries after the six-hundredth, only **14–22%** report their own null (both the strict and loose readings are given) ⇒ **for the other 78–86% there is no way to tell whether the number has only just cleared noise**. The fragile-denominator audit was voided for this reason (Entry 693) |
| **A threshold on a ratio must be measured from that ratio's own null** | the 2.4-fold asymmetry belongs to the *more consistent* component at only **p = 0.0423**; and the extremity ratio's null has a 95th percentile of **17.3** — with a near-zero denominator a ratio has almost no resolution. My preregistered [0.5, 2] was chosen by intuition (Entry 691) |
| **An UNVERIFIED does not enter *What stands* — and this one died the next round** | it once cleared its null in all five batteries; on a never-used held-out battery under a directional criterion fixed in advance, partialling out total response variance **flips the sign to −0.0360 (ratio −0.68 < 0.7)** ⇒ **the discriminating-versus-blanket dichotomy does not hold** (Entry 684 · 685) |
| **The asking moves the answer** | same year, same questionnaire, sexual items run higher face-to-face while the death penalty does not ⇒ **part of any sexual-attitude trend crossing 2021 is the asking** |
| **Correlations have ceilings** | skewed marginals pin the maximum: GSS binaries **0.36**, MFQ **0.91–0.95**; an absolute threshold judges a ceiling-limited block "not a domain" (Entry 647 · 649 · 651) |
| **The shared instrument** | one coding team, one questionnaire, manufactures coupling by itself. **A claim whose instrument you cannot name is a claim about the instrument** |
| **A gradient needs ten strata** | "rank the strata, then take ρ" has a permutation null whose 95th percentile sits at **1.0** when k = 3–4 — **nothing short of ρ = ±1 can ever be significant**. Redone at deciles with BH over all nine batteries, only institutional trust (−0.90) and abortion (+0.75) survive; **sexual morality does not** (Entry 674) |

## Structure

**E · A · R** — an epoch closes when the ontology shifts, an arc when a decision becomes safe, a round on
**one claim on this page**. **3 epochs · 30 arcs · 149 rounds · 685 scripts** (Entry 657)**. One round is one
folder, with its scripts flat inside.**
**Closing condition: cross-instrument** — a round does not close until the same question has been asked on
≥2 instruments; enforced by `tools/readme_gate.py`, **which blocked its own author on its first live round.**

[`RETRACTIONS.md`](RETRACTIONS.md) — **697 entries**, every retraction and narrowing with the thing that
killed it.

# The Eroticization Operator

**"Sexual" — is it a content category the brain detects, or a value the brain assigns to ordinary
world-representations?** 531 self-attacking rounds against one public dataset, asking what can be
settled before anyone collects anything new. **486 numbered ledger entries** — [RETRACTIONS.md](RETRACTIONS.md)
carries the history; this page carries only what currently stands. **中文版:[`README_zh.md`](README_zh.md)** — English is canonical; corrections land here first.

Private curiosity project. Not written for publication and not seeking any.

Object: the **Big Kink Survey** public subsample — [Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.18625141](https://zenodo.org/records/18625141),
15,503 respondents × 365 columns, aggressively binned, demographically stripped, noise-injected.
Data is not committed — neither the raw release nor the person-level derived tables; `E01…/A01…/R001_schema/run.py` re-downloads and rebuilds them. **[CORRECTED before publication]** 24 derived `data/derived/*.csv` files, two of them with one row per respondent, were tracked until now, so this sentence was false; they are untracked and gitignored as of this commit. They remain in the commit history.

> ### How big is any of this? (`#264`, read this before anything else)
> Every person-level dimension this project established, **entered together** against the same 29
> outcomes, explains a **median of 1.4%** of the variance — **7.8% at its best** (`animated`), **2.8%**
> for shame. The degrees-of-freedom floor is **0.14%**, and the same pipeline reaches **49%** when a
> deliberately strong predictor is added, so the machinery is not blind and the dimensions are not
> noise: they are real, replicable, cross-instrument, controlled — **and they are small**.
> **Two readings this does not license.** It does *not* mean the findings are empty (the floor is
> 0.14%, and every dimension clears its own family-wise threshold). It does *not* mean sexual
> preference is mostly random — **1.4% is the reach of *these six coordinates*, not a ceiling on
> explainability**. ⚠ And note the ordering: the *least reliable* standing dimension (position, 0.432)
> contributes the *largest* incremental R² (0.715%). **Reliability and explanatory power are different
> things — a quantity can stably measure something that hardly moves anything.**
> ⚠ **`#301` `#302`:**in-sample 1.4% / out-of-sample 0.87%**. Held out — 5-fold person-level cross-validation with the six coordinates **re-estimated inside every fold** — the same pipeline delivers a median of **0.869%**; with the coordinates held fixed it is 1.087%, and ridge-CV 1.056%. On 2 of the 29 outcomes the held-out R² is **negative**. **Over a third of the published number is fit.** The estimator is the largest single knob in [`CALIBER.md`](CALIBER.md) (**0.52pp**, against 0.18pp for every analysis knob). Both numbers are legitimate and they answer different questions — 1.390% is what these coordinates explain **in the people they were built from**, 0.869% is what they deliver **on people they have never seen**. ⚠ **`#356`: and part of that 1.4% is co-linear with how much a person reported.** Adding "how many onset categories you filled in" to the six coordinates and doing a commonality decomposition across all 29 outcomes, the shared component is positive for **22 of 29** outcomes, median **0.345pp** — **18.2% of the median outcome's explained variance** — and it grows with how well the outcome is explained (`r` = **+0.609**). Swapping the breadth proxy for block coverage keeps it (25/29 positive; the two shared vectors correlate **+0.892**). ⚠ Commonality is a bookkeeping term, not a causal one: it says this part is shared, not which side carries it.

## The three models being separated

Ivan's framing, kept verbatim because the whole repository is scoped by it:

| | |
|---|---|
| **A** dedicated sexual-content system | `Y_i(s) = f_i(h_sex(s))` — "sexual" is a content category like *face* in vision |
| **B** erotic valuation of ordinary representation | `v_i(s,c,t) = w_i(c,t)^T h(s)` — ordinary scene, a readout applied to it |
| **C** recursive mix | value re-enters and reshapes the representation it acted on |
> ### Where the three models stand (`#234c`)
> **B is dead** (`#179`: position is not a stance-free coordinate — it comes with shame).
> **C's one distinguishing prediction is unsupported** (`#234a`: stratify by shame, match pick count,
> and the breadth profile's split-half replication is **0.4672 / 0.4611 / 0.4718** — flat, in a design
> whose positive control fires at a gradient **7.4× smaller** than the one C predicts).
> **But the honest reading is not "A wins."** The most reliable person-level structure in this release —
> split-half **0.56–0.72**, against the position score's 0.432 — is *which domains a person opens in and
> in what shape* (`#229`–`#232`). **None of A, B or C predicts it**, because all three ask what makes a
> thing sexual, and this is a fact about the **person**, not the stimulus.
> **The A/B/C decomposition does not cut this data at its joints.** That is a claim about the
> decomposition's completeness, not a refutation of A.


---

## The person-level dimensions this release actually supports

`#234c` found that the A/B/C decomposition does not cut this data at its joints: all three ask what makes a
*thing* sexual, while the most reliable structure here is about the *person*. Those dimensions were scattered
across a dozen ledger entries; this is all of them in one place. **Every number below appears verbatim in
`RETRACTIONS.md` — the round that built this table (`#251`) fails if one does not.** Empty cells say
`UNCOMPUTED`; they are the honest part.

| Dimension | split-half | instrument | cross-instrument | ↔ shame | outcomes cleared | strongest rival |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| **Position S** — how uncommon the options you pick are | **+0.432** | blocks (32 multi-select) | untested | **+0.1185 [cov≥8 · scope stated]** | 9/20 | pick count controlled (+0.1155) · sex · content score |
| **Between-block contrast D** — which domains you open in | **+0.6785** | blocks | **verified** (onset instrument, +0.1384) | +0.0830 | ~8–10/31  ⚠ **`#259`: this row and the breadth-type c3 row overlap heavily** — their outcome profiles correlate **+0.7826** against an attenuation ceiling of 0.9142 ± 0.0171, i.e. **86% of ceiling**, resolvably below it (7.7× the spread) but by only 0.13 in absolute terms. They are kept as two rows because the residual is resolvable, not because they are independent. | pick count near-absent on the onset instrument (+0.0428) |
| **Breadth profile** — the shape you open in | **+0.4290** | blocks | **verified** (≥2 dimensions; onset k=2 replicates at 0.9379) | its per-person **clarity** gives +0.0148 ± 0.0116 (1.3×, one seed) | **8 of 29, read as 3–13** across six option-split seeds (`#255` — ⚠ `#254`'s "predicts nothing" is **withdrawn**: that reading came from one unlucky seed) | pick count controlled · block-level property ruled out (`#228b`) |
| Breadth type c1 | **+0.6429** | blocks | untested | `UNCOMPUTED` | 13 → **9 after control, read as 6–12** (`#257`) | **pick count · sex · position S all controlled** (`#252`) |
| Breadth type c2 | **+0.5778** | blocks | untested | `UNCOMPUTED` | 11 → **6 after control, read as 4–8** (`#257`) | **pick count · sex · position S all controlled** (`#252`) |
| **Breadth type c3 — *where you spend your openness*** | **+0.5631** | blocks | **name verified** on the disjoint onset instrument (`#258`: +0.1815 with its transgressive half, **−0.1086** with its ordinary half, gap 18.6×) | **+0.1286** | 21 → **19 after control, read as 17–21** (`#257`) | correlates only +0.2036 with position; both survive jointly; total category count controlled — the gap **grows** to +0.4905 ⚠ **`#366`: and this route is not as clean as the other two.** Against a panel of **20** non-coordinate person-level variables (age, sex, all five Big Five traits, powerlessness, mental-illness and childhood-adversity flags, upbringing, relationship style, gender tolerance, politics, straightness, BMI, partner count, pick count, porn habit, self-reported honesty), a commonality decomposition on shame leaves c3⁻ with **59.6%** of its own R² (unique 0.996pp of 1.671pp; shared **+0.674pp** against a row-permutation null of +0.007 ± 0.017). **13 of the 20 exceed their own permutation threshold individually**, though no single one takes more than **18.9%** (partner count), then extraversion 14.9%, age 8.8%, neuroticism 8.3%, powerlessness 7.3%. **All five biggest bites point the same way**: younger, fewer partners, less extraverted, more neurotic, less agency, together carrying about 40% of this route's shame. The other 60% none of the twenty can see. ⚠ **`#367`: that 40% is ONE dimension, not twenty — but half of the sentence above was rhetoric.** Sign-aligned, all 10 off-diagonal correlations among the five are positive (median +0.092) and PC1 takes **28.9%** against an offset null of **20.88% ± 0.23%** (+35 sd) — the null is not zero because sign-aligning on a common target *induces* a floor, so it is built by synthesising five variables with the observed \|r\| to c3⁻ and nothing else in common. **A real single factor gives 59.6%, so the common direction is genuine but thin.** And that single PC1 takes **37.1%** of c3⁻'s shame where all twenty take 40.4% — **92% of the work of twenty variables done by one dimension** — against random 5-subsets taking **8.3% ± 7.7%** (beyond all 300 draws,
