All three worlds were written first, **including "the lead is amplified", because a confound can run either way and preparing for only one is leaving yourself a back door**. The result: the five domains' median ceilings are **0.913–0.950**, and normalised the order is **PURITY 0.5392 > AUTHORITY 0.3371 > HARM 0.3311 > FAIRNESS 0.3243 > INGROUP 0.2522** — **unchanged** — while the lead goes from **+0.1748 to +0.2021**, 95% interval **[+0.1802, +0.2226]**: **the raw figure falls below the interval's lower bound, so "understated" is resolved rather than merely apparent.** Controls: the five raw values reproduce to **0.0008**; shuffling the domain assignment leaves a normalised within-minus-cross gap of **+0.0191**, 9% of the effect (and that null **keeps every marginal and therefore every ceiling**, so the ceiling does not manufacture the gap); across a thousand resamples purity ranks first **1.000** of the time. ⚠ **And one specification disagrees, published with the rest**: under Kendall the normalised order becomes PURITY > **FAIRNESS** > AUTHORITY > HARM > INGROUP — **first place agrees across both estimators and ranks two through four reshuffle, so first place is all that may be said.** ⚠ **The round's general result is outside the numbers**: MFQ's ceilings all sit at 0.91–0.95 and normalisation changes almost nothing, whereas the four GSS binary items in the twenty-third had ceilings of **0.095–0.769** and normalisation turned everything over. **Ceiling correction matters only where the marginals are skewed, so the rest of this page's numbers want triage by ceiling rather than a blanket recomputation** `[#648 "我下注纯洁的领先是天花板造的,而它比生的数还要更领先"]`.

- **A twenty-fifth, and it turns the ninth from a direction into a magnitude: the same women, the same five-point answer scale, and their three judgements about sex correlate at 0.47 while their seven judgements about family correlate at 0.10 — a factor of 4.7.** The twenty-fourth left the line "triage by ceiling rather than recompute everything", **and a triage that cannot fail is only a rewording** — so this round built the triage a positive control first: two blocks whose answer is already known, MFQ's five domains required to land in *no recomputation needed* (measured **0.913–0.950**, passed) and GSS's four binary police items required to land in *must recompute* (measured **0.3552**, passed). **Only an instrument that separates those two earns the right to sort the ones never measured.** Of nine blocks, **five need no recomputation, two are undecided, one must be recomputed** — and that one already died in the twenty-third. ⚠ **Then the ninth was triaged for the first time, and its ordering does not flip**: the sexual block's ceiling is **0.9074** and the family block's **0.8464**, normalised the two are **+0.4680** and **+0.0988**, and sex is tighter either way. **So the ninth is not rewritten, and it now carries a magnitude: not "tighter" but "barely the same order of quantity". ⚠ **And the very next round overturned that sentence; it stays here rather than being deleted: the 0.10 was computed over seven items that are not one block — see the twenty-sixth.**** Those seven family items correlate at 0.10 against a ceiling of 0.85 — **her seven judgements about family are close to not being one thing at all** `[#649 "性 0.47 对家庭 0.10,而两块的天花板都高 —— 不是天花板造的"]`.

- **A twenty-sixth, and it overturns the 4.7× I wrote one round ago: her three judgements about sex and her four about whether the unmarried count as a family are equally tight.** The twenty-fifth quantified *sexual morality is tighter than family morality* as 0.47 against 0.10. **But the family side is not a domain.** Reading the item texts as the rule requires (**and a variable name misled me once**: `chsuppor` is not child support but *Okay for unmarried woman to have and raise a child*), **four of the seven are one thing** — whether a young couple may cohabit (`okcohab`), whether a cohabiting couple may raise children (`chcohab`), whether an unmarried woman may (`chsuppor`), and whether cohabiting first prevents divorce (`prvntdiv`) — correlating at **0.32–0.40**; the other three go their own ways: whether divorce is the solution, whether one can be happy without children, and `marrfail`, *marriage has not worked out for most people I know* — **which is not a moral judgement at all but a description of the people around her.** The largest connected block is **4 items**, and it is **4 at thresholds 0.20, 0.25, 0.30 and 0.35** alike; the positive control (the same algorithm on the three sexual items) returns **3**, and the placebo (row-shuffled) returns **1**; the alignment step flipped exactly one item, `okcohab`, which is reverse-worded. ⚠ **So the fair comparison is the three sexual items against the four cohabitation items**: normalised, **+0.4679 against +0.4257 — a factor of 1.10.** ⚠ And because those four were **selected for being the most connected**, the block was re-selected on half the sample and scored on the other half: across 51 splits the same four were chosen every time (size range [4,4]), the held-out within-block median is **+0.3750**, and the selection bias is **−0.0019** — **1.10 is reportable.** **So *sexual morality is tighter than family morality* does not hold: it looked true only because the family side was a bag of seven items, three of which go their own way** `[#650 "七题不是一个领域,所以 4.7 倍是拿一个领域去比一个袋子"]`.

- **A twenty-seventh, and it is an UNVERIFIED whose only reportable content is a mechanism: an absolute threshold systematically judges a ceiling-limited block "not a domain".** The twenty-sixth named a recurring error class — taking the instrument's organisation for the world's — and this round tried to make it measurable, running a connected-sub-block search over fourteen "domains" this page has cited and reporting **largest sub-block ÷ nominal size**. ⚠ **The instrument broke in two places and both were mine.** ① The positive control asserted something never measured: what had been established was the five moral domains' **median** coherence and purity's lead, **never that every item connects to its block at 0.30** — measured, FAIRNESS and INGROUP come in at **5/6**, still 5/6 after normalisation, **so my "known answer" was wrong, not the algorithm**. ② The floor was written at **150** while the ethnographic dataset holds **at most 186 societies** — the corporal-punishment and lecturing quartets returned **not a single computable pair**; **and this is the best-disguised version yet: it did not error, it reported `1/1/1/1`, which looks like a conclusion.** That is the fourth instance of one theme (the previous three wrote a stopping rule's floor on sample size, and cut a threshold ladder finer than its interval) — **the threshold was written without asking what this site can supply.** ⚠ **And the confound control written before the run fired, which is the one thing here that stands**: the four binary GSS police items give a largest sub-block of **2/4** under a raw threshold and **4/4** under a normalised one — their ceiling is only 0.36, so **an absolute 0.30 can never be reached**, and a perfectly connected block is judged to be loose items. **That cell was preregistered and may be used; nothing else here may, and UNVERIFIED is not an acquittal** `[#651 "它没有报错,它报了 1/1/1/1,看起来像一个结论"]`.

- **A twenty-eighth, and it is the second bet I placed the wrong way: the domain boundaries drawn by questionnaire authors and coding teams mostly hold in the data — eleven of fourteen blocks — and all three that fail are nominally LARGER than the real block, never once the other way.** The twenty-seventh came back UNVERIFIED because the instrument broke in two places; with those fixed (floor set per site, any block with an uncomputable pair recorded as undecidable rather than given a number, and the positive controls replaced by three blocks with independently known answers) all four controls pass: the two ethnographic target-quartets had to give 1.00 — **they did**; the family seven had to give 4/7 — **it gave 0.571**; the row-shuffled placebo returns 1. ⚠ **And I bet on "isolated case" and lost**: besides the family seven, the moral questionnaire's **fairness and ingroup domains also come in at 5/6** — one item in each connects to **none** of the other five. ⚠ Hard rule 1 bites here: those two items' texts **are not in the release** (four files in the whole directory, at most 26 labelled variables, and not one of the targets), **so all that may be said is the variable names `rich` and `family`, not what they ask.** ⚠ Only **one** block disagrees between raw and normalised thresholds, and it is exactly the one the previous entry predicted: the four binary GSS police items, judged 2/4 raw and 4/4 normalised, because their ceiling is only 0.36. ⚠ **And this narrows my own wording from one round ago**: I said handwriting, halo and bag were one error class seen three times — **that lumped three mechanisms into one**. Handwriting is a coder's style contaminating correlations across variables; halo is a coder giving one society similar marks down every column; the bag is a nominal block larger than the real one; **only the third is what was measured here, and it now has three instances. Three sightings of one theme, three different mechanisms** `[#652 "十四块里十一块站得住,而站不住的三块全是名义大于真实,一次反向都没有"]`.

- **A twenty-ninth, and it restates the purity result in a different key: purity is not merely the tightest of the five moral domains, it is the only one that is a single thing throughout.** The twenty-eighth's connected-sub-block test asks only that **every item connect to at least one other** — **a chain passes**. So this round switched to the **weakest link**: a block's smallest pairwise correlation (which is exactly the largest threshold at which every pair still connects). Positive control: the ethnographic target-quartet's weakest link is **0.872** against a requirement of ≥ 0.85; placebo: row-shuffled edge density **0.000**. ⚠ **Eight of fourteen blocks are cliques and six are merely connected** — and among the five moral domains, **only purity has all six items correlated pairwise** (weakest link **0.335**, at `disgusting×chastity`), while each of the other four contains a pair that barely touches: authority 0.170, ingroup 0.149, harm 0.139, fairness **0.062**. ⚠ **And purity is exactly the block I named in advance as the one I least wanted to be a chain** — five claims on this page rest on purity being a domain, **it is not a chain, and those five survive.** ⚠ **The second thing that stands is cross-instrument**: sex is a clique in two entirely different instruments — the four sexual-morality items in a national probability sample have a weakest link of **0.416**, and three items asked of women in another national survey **0.346**, with different wordings, populations and years. ⚠ Conversely, **two blocks are openly not one thing**: the four binary police items have a weakest link of **−0.149** and the family seven **−0.220** — **not weak, but reversed**. ⚠ And since "clique" uses a chosen threshold of 0.30, what this page reports is the **weakest link itself**, not that dichotomy `[#653 "五个道德域里只有纯洁是团,而性在两具仪器上都是"]`. ⚠ **The null this claim owed has now been supplied, and what it produced is not a confirmation but a leg going soft.** The null asks: **draw the same number of items at random from the same questionnaire's pool — how high does the weakest link get?** Instrument preserved, domain membership destroyed. Over GSS's eight items (four police + four sexual), all 69 mixed blocks enumerated, the null's 95th percentile is **0.0551** against the sexual four at **0.416** — **7.55×, solid**. Six items drawn at random from MFQ's thirty: null **0.0162**, purity **0.3354** — **20.67×, harder still**. ⚠ **The NSFG leg: all 119 three-item blocks from its pool, null 95th percentile 0.3124, observed 0.3456 — 1.11×.** Under the criterion written before the run (null ≥ observed ⇒ withdraw) it is **not withdrawn**; but 1.11× means **a triple of items drawn at random from that questionnaire is not rarely this tight.** So *sex is one block on two instruments* becomes: **solid on GSS, sitting on its null on NSFG.** ⚠ **And why it sits there is what this round actually found — NSFG is not the problem, the statistic moves with block size.** On the same ten-item pool the null's 95th percentile runs **k=3 → 0.3148 · k=4 → 0.3015 · k=5 → 0.1969 · k=6 → 0.1029**, monotonically down. **The weakest link is a min over C(k,2) pairs; the smaller k is, the fewer draws the min takes, and the higher the null.** And `#653` applied **one fixed threshold of 0.30** to blocks of k = 3, 4, 6 and 7 — **at k=3 that threshold is below its own null.** This is `#713`'s *the same PASS means three different strengths on this page* growing back in a new place: **this time it is one threshold meaning four different strengths across four block sizes.** ⇒ **A weakest link must be compared against a size-matched null, never against a fixed number.** ⚠ The confound was written before the run: mixed blocks pair reverse-scored items and would depress the null artificially; the control is that real and random blocks both pass through `align()` (which hands the random block a free choice of flips — **the direction that hurts me**), and aligned and unaligned nulls came out nearly identical (GSS 0.0551/0.0551 · MFQ 0.0162/0.0149) — **the confound is empty here.** ④ All four positive controls reproduce: SCCS 0.8955 / GSS 0.4160 / NSFG 0.3456 / MFQ 0.3354, largest deviation from the ledger **0.0005** `[#715「一个固定的阈,在四种块大小上意味着四种强度」]`. ⚠ **Pointing the same ruler at the five ethnographic quartets turns up a seam in child-rearing itself.** That pool is **5 techniques × 4 targets** (teaching by example · lecturing · corporal punishment · permissiveness · affection, each applied to early/late boys and girls), so it carries a free control: the five blocks `#653` called clumps are **one technique across four targets**, while **one target across four techniques** is equally four variables from the same pen. Against a null of **all 4,840 enumerated blocks** (q95 0.2504) the two groupings do not look like the same dataset: **same technique × four targets = +0.645 … +0.896 (2.6–3.6×); same target × four techniques has a median of −0.0138 — on the null.** ⚠ **So the five clumps are not the coder's handwriting.** If they were, any four child-rearing variables from the same pen would be equally tight, and they are not. **Whether a society uses a given technique is uniform across all four kinds of child; which techniques it uses on one child is not one thing at all. The seam runs along the technique, not the child.** ⚠ **And I nearly wrote this backwards.** Greedy alignment put the same-target blocks at a median of **−0.4164**, which reads as *techniques exclude one another*; with k=4 there are only 8 sign assignments, and **brute-forcing the optimum gives −0.0138** — **the minus sign was the greedy step's, not the data's.** *Mutually exclusive* and *unrelated* are entirely different sentences, and separating them cost eight enumerations `[#717「接缝在手段上,不在孩子身上」]`. ⚠ **Pushing that optimal-sign step across the whole page then exposed its price: every ratio halves, and two verdicts turn over.** The logic is symmetric — if the real block may choose its own flips, so may **every random block in the null**, and the random blocks gain more: MFQ's null rises from **0.0299 to 0.0592**, GSS's from 0.0574 to **0.0911**, NSFG's from 0.3124 to **0.3587**. So purity goes 11.23× → **5.66×**, the GSS sexual four 7.25× → **4.56×**, and **two cross their own line**: **MFQ·fairness 2.08× → 1.05× (on the null) · NSFG·sexual-three 1.11× → 0.96× (inside its own null).** ⚠ **So this page's first cross-instrument replication has one leg, not two.** *Sex is one block* is solid on GSS under either convention (4.56–7.25×, clearing the null's 99th percentile both ways), **while NSFG's three items are, under the stricter convention, indistinguishable from three items drawn at random.** ⚠ **And *why* they disagree was later taken apart, and the answer is not about people.** Aligning both to **k=3**: **the observed weakest link is GSS 0.4154 against NSFG 0.3452 — the numerator differs by only 1.20× — while the two nulls are 0.1369 against 0.3587, a denominator ratio of 2.62×.** 1.20 × 2.62 = 3.15, exactly the 3.03× against 0.96× gap. **⇒ 84% of the gap comes from the denominator and 16% from the numerator.** And the denominator is set by **whatever else that questionnaire asks**: GSS's pool is the sexual four plus the **police four** (binary, mutually negative), NSFG's is the sexual three plus the **family seven** (which hang together far better) — **three items drawn at random from NSFG's pool cohere more easily than three from GSS's, before anyone is asked anything about sex.** ⇒ **"GSS says yes, NSFG says no" is not a disagreement about people; it is two questionnaires whose *other* items happen to look different.** ⚠⚠ **The next round pushed this to its end, and the result is heavier than the sentence above.** Same people, same GSS sexual four, same statistic, **only the companion battery changes**: against the police four it is **4.56×**, against spending 2.51×, against gender roles 1.51×, against suicide 0.99×, **against abortion 0.93×** — **max/min = 4.90, while the observed weakest link is identical to the digit all five times (0.4154). The entire spread sits in the denominator.** ⇒ **"A person has one sexual morality" stands (0.4154 is real coherence); "and it coheres more than other moral attitudes" does not — that was manufactured by my choosing the police battery as the background.** ⚠ One column fell out on the way: **the abortion four's own weakest link is +0.6358 and the suicide four's is +0.8927, both tighter than the sexual four's +0.4154.** ⚠ **But that can only be an observation, not a finding, and the reason is this: the abortion and suicide batteries vary *four conditions of one act*, while the sexual four vary *four different acts*** — `#542`/`#653`⑤ recorded long ago that **items of the same shape, or near-synonyms, manufacture a block.** ⚠ And the sexual four is not clean either: `teensex` reads literally *Sex before marriage: teens 14-16*, so **it and `premarsx` are the same act at two ages**, the same twin structure as NSFG's. ⇒ **Inside the NSFG questionnaire, "which block coheres more" cannot be asked at all — its ten items already correlate with one another.** Taking `#650`'s *equally tight* apart and redoing it: **the observed 0.468 (sexual three) and 0.425 (cohabitation four) are real coherence; the same-pool same-k nulls are 0.376 and 0.411** ⇒ **1.24× and 1.03×**, and under the stricter exclusion **1.13× and 0.98×** — **both blocks sit essentially on their own nulls** (verdict UNVERIFIED, the null being 88% of the effect; the placebo returns +0.0015 / −0.0007 once rows are shuffled). ⚠ The original **1.10× divided one observed quantity by another** — `realstat` and `#716` both record it: **compare a quantity to its own null, never to another observed quantity.** ⚠ And more fundamentally the blocks are **k=3 against k=4**, and the null moves with k (measured here as 0.376 against 0.411) ⇒ **the two ratios are not on one scale, so "equally tight" is not false but ill-posed.** ⚠ On the first re-run the positive control fired immediately (the cohabitation four came out at **−0.0332** against the ledger's +0.4257) because **`okcohab` is reverse-worded** (*should not live together unless married*): `#650` aligned signs and I had not. **That is the third time since `#718`/`#720` that I have dropped the same step**, and `aligned_pooled_median` now lives in `lib/` `[#734「两块都贴着自己的零,而那句「一样紧」是把两个观测量相除得来的」]`. ⇒ **Turning "which kind of morality coheres more" into a finding needs a comparison matched on item structure, and this round did not build one** `[#731「同一组题换一个陪衬域,比值从 0.93 倍走到 4.56 倍,而分子一动不动」]`. ⚠ **That comparison was then looked for, and GSS does not contain it.** Searching the whole release for the four-point *always wrong … not wrong at all* scale (the search first passed its positive control: it had to recover the sexual four itself, and all four are there) returns **exactly ten variables** — four are the sexual four, four are their alternate wording (`premars1` and siblings), **and the remaining two are abortion *condition* items** (`abdefctw` / `abpoorw`). ⇒ **GSS holds no non-sexual battery of four different acts on this scale. That is not "not yet done"; it is what this instrument cannot do — and a condition-type battery may not be substituted for it.** ⚠ But the search handed over something more useful: **those four `*1` items are a second administration of the same battery.** Within 1994, same population, same convention, **the original four have a weakest link of +0.3339 (n = 894) and the alternate wording +0.4273 (n = 1,104)**; and of the **368 people who answered both versions, the per-item retest correlations are 0.837 / 0.843 / 0.911 / 0.734**. ⇒ **Ask it again in different words and the same Americans give back the same block. So "a person has one sexual morality" is about people, not about the four sentences I happened to pick.** ⚠⚠ **And using that retest as a ruler gives an answer that is not good news.** `#731` showed a ratio is relative to its pool and `#734` showed it collapses entirely in a questionnaire whose items all correlate ⇒ **"a person has one sexual morality" can no longer rest on a ratio.** The retest supplies a **pool-free** ruler instead: a person answering the same construct in different words agrees with herself only **0.734–0.911**, so **the ceiling on a cross-item correlation is not 1.0 but √(r_xx·r_yy)**. Correcting 1994's six pairs accordingly: **0.300 · 0.360 · 0.415 · 0.425 · 0.513 · 0.553**. ⇒ **Even with perfect measurement, the weakest pair of the four sexual-moral judgements correlates only 0.30 and the strongest only 0.55. They share something real, but they are not one thing.** ⚠⚠ **And the next round fixed the direction of that 0.30, which turns out to be upward.** In a synthetic world with the true correlation known (four categories, 27 cells, 200 replicates each), the four estimators' median relative bias is **raw −28.5% · ceiling-only −22.2% · disattenuation-only −16.6% · composed −9.5%** — **all four under-estimate**, and the two improvements are close to additive, so **ceiling normalisation and disattenuation are not correcting the same thing and may be composed**; **even composed it still under-estimates by 9.5%, because four-category discretisation attenuates by 12–13% and no estimator repairs it** (Entry 736). ⇒ **So the 0.3002 above is a lower bound and the latent value is somewhat higher — while still far from 0.50, so the verdict *not one thing* does not move.** ⚠⚠ **And asked for the first time at the level of the decade, the answer is far more specific than "it became a block".** From 1988 to 2024 the level moved from **1.758 to 2.370** (a full step, with `homosex` alone moving +1.209). Over the same years coherence — the mean of the six ceiling-normalised pair correlations — went from **0.483 to 0.635**, a slope of **+0.00313/yr**, against a year-label-shuffled null whose 36-year half-width is only **±0.0359** ⇒ **about 3.1×, and the pre-registered W1 holds.** ⚠ **But once decomposed, "the four became one block" has to narrow into something more specific: of the six pairs, `premarsx × homosex` alone went from +0.288 to +0.632 (+0.2703 over 36 years) while the other five moved −0.039 to +0.051 — essentially not at all.** ⇒ **It is not that sexual morality became a position; it is that the judgement about homosexuality stopped being a separate thing and became part of sexual morality.** ⚠ And this is the same thing `#676` measured at the level of **education** (that round found the tightening concentrated on the homosexuality item) — **two units, one mechanism, measured independently.** ⚠ Three controls: ④ the level's slope is +0.0199/yr against a year-shuffled null of [−0.0012, +0.0012], so **the instrument does recover the known level trend**; the placebo — **the police four's raw correlation moves the other way, −0.0765 over 36 years** — rules out a generic sample or mode drift; and a post-hoc decomposition (**labelled as such: it was not pre-registered**) shows **the raw correlation rises +0.0542 by itself**, with the ceiling's −0.0858 contributing the other half ⇒ **the ceiling is not the driver.** ⚠⚠ **The word *fusion* was then attacked by its strongest confound, and it held.** The confound was recorded in the same round that stated the conclusion: **`homosex`'s level moved +1.209, two to six times the others, and an item moving off the floor toward the middle will mechanically correlate more with everything else (range restriction released).** The test converts **GSS's real four-category proportions in each year** into cutpoints on a latent normal (`homosex`'s 1988 cutpoints are [0.724, 0.877, 1.083] and its 2024 ones [−0.345, −0.26, −0.09]), then **holds the latent correlation constant and lets only the cutpoints travel along the real marginals**. The result: **`premarsx × homosex` gains just +0.0056, 2% of the observed +0.2703**; substituting the 2024 value of ρ — the most generous setting — still gives only 17%. ④ Positive control: **with the cutpoints frozen the simulation moves −0.0020 over 36 years**, so the machinery does not manufacture trends. ⚠ **And what makes the control credible is that it fired elsewhere**: the same simulation accounts for **86%** of `xmarsex×teensex`, 64% of `xmarsex×homosex` and 60% of `homosex×teensex` — **the three small movers are almost entirely range restriction, and the one large mover is almost entirely not.** ⇒ **That fusion is about people, not about the scale.** `[#739「限幅解释了三个小的,唯一大的那个它解释不了」]`
> ⚠⚠ **And the last obvious rival was cut away too: it is not older cohorts leaving and younger ones arriving.** GSS carries year of birth, so the cut can be made: splitting people by birth cohort and recomputing the `premarsx × homosex` slope on year **inside each cohort**, **all four cohorts that clear the floor rise** — 1929–45 **+0.142 over 36 years** · Boomers **+0.273** · X **+0.212** · Millennials **+0.314**; the n-weighted **within-cohort pooled slope is +0.2418 over 36 years, 113% of the total** ⇒ **compositional change contributes −13%: not merely not the driver, but very slightly against it.** ④ Positive control: the within-cohort **level** also rises (+0.0180/yr) and returns to [−0.0026, +0.0028] once years are shuffled within cohort, **so the design can see a known within-cohort change**; the null for the correlation slope is [−0.0018, +0.0018] and the observed +0.0067 sits **3.7×** outside it. ⚠ The pre-1929 cohort has only 4 usable years and Z has none ⇒ **both recorded as undecidable and kept out of the pooling**. ⚠ **And the sentence that must be held: GSS is a repeated cross-section, so a cohort is not the same people — the strict claim is that *Americans born in those years* changed, never that *a person* changed. This instrument has no true panel.** > ⚠⚠ **And the words *within cohort* carry a problem I did not state for three rounds: inside a birth cohort `age = year − cohort` is an identity — year and age are perfectly collinear.** So what `#740` measured is by construction period *and* age together, and the sentence could equally have been *they connected these two things only as they aged*. ⇒ A different cut: **hold the age band fixed (25–39 / 40–54 / 55–69) and let cohort move with year.** The result is **+0.00640 = 95% of the within-cohort slope**, consistent across the three bands (+0.0069 / +0.0054 / +0.0069), against a null (years shuffled within age band) of [−0.00082, +0.00079] — **8.1× outside it.** ⚠ The **placebo** guards against this round's own trap — stratifying costs n, and lost n shrinks slopes through noise alone. Stratifying by **sex** at the same granularity gives **+0.00610 = 91%**, so **stratification itself costs about 9% and age a further 5%.** ⇒ **Pure age is excluded.** ⚠ Education was asked too (`#676` showed education itself produces the same fusion): **within education terciles +0.00521 = 77%**, with all three terciles moving ⇒ **education explains at most a quarter.** ⚠ And the two double stratifications clear **0 of 9 and 1 of 18 cells ⇒ undecidable** — **that zero is silence, not an acquittal**, and is recorded as such. ⚠⚠ **One last thing has to be stated, and it bounds this whole line: age, period and cohort are linearly dependent (APC is not identified). This round and `#740` together can exclude *pure age* and *pure cohort*; they cannot estimate *period* as a coefficient. The word that remains is what elimination left, not what was measured.** `[#743「世代内的年份就是年龄,而固定年龄段之后它还剩九成五」]`
> ⇒ **The population did not turn over; the same cohorts moved *homosexuality* into *sexual morality* over thirty-six years —
> and the words *sexual morality* have been tested, not merely written.** Running the same within-cohort slope over **all 253 pairs** of 23 GSS attitude items:
> **the typical pair moves +0.00001, which is to say not at all** — **so there is no general "everything converged" drift in these decades** —
> while `premarsx × homosex`'s +0.00672 **ranks 2nd of 253**.
> `homosex` against the **19 non-sexual items** has a median of **−0.00025** (−7% of the sexual side); by family:
> **abortion −0.00445 · spending −0.00220 · police 0.00000 · suicide +0.00033 · gender roles +0.00175**.
> ⚠ **If this were political sorting, abortion would be the first thing to converge. It went the other way** `[#741「253 对里典型的一对根本没动,而它排第二」]`.
> ⛔ ~~And one detail that has to be named: of the three sexual items `xmarsex` gains only +0.00075 — what joined is premarital and teen, not extramarital.~~
> **That half-sentence lived on this page for exactly one round before I overturned it myself.** Ceiling-normalised, the three pairs are
> **`premarsx×homosex` +0.00581 · `xmarsex×homosex` +0.00574 · `teensex×homosex` +0.00688 — essentially identical (99%).**
> Meanwhile that pair's ceiling fell from **0.947 to 0.558** over the same years — **the +0.00075 was the ceiling talking, not people.**
> ⇒ **All three sexual items joined, at almost the same rate. "Infidelity did not join" is withdrawn.**
> ⚠ And the part worth remembering: **`#739` had already measured that pair's artefact share at 64%** —
> **the number was in my hands, and one round later I wrote a substantive claim off the raw slope. I used my own control and ignored its output** `[#742「「出轨没加入」是天花板说的,而那个数我上一轮就有了」]`. `[#740「不是老一代退出,是同一批世代自己把两件事连了起来」]`.

⚠ Two shrinkages: GSS is a **repeated cross-section, not a panel** ⇒ this is about *Americans in that year*, **never about the same person changing**; and `teensex` begins in 1986, so **1972–1987 is structurally out of reach** `[#738「变紧的不是四题,是一对:同性恋从一件单独的事变成了性道德的一部分」]`. ⚠⚠ **The next round asked the other half of *who*, and half of it stands while the other half downgrades the round before.** What stands is the **level**: from 1988 to 2024 the mean of `homosex` moved from **1.788 to 3.258 (+1.187 over 36 years)** among the rarely-attending, +1.200 among the occasional, and only from **1.431 to 1.973 (+0.640)** among frequent attenders — each **11.8–14.7×** its own null, with the **MDE computed before the run (0.054–0.087 over 36 years)** and the observed effects 7–22× it. ⇒ **Becoming more permissive happened mostly among people who were not condemning much to begin with; the churchgoers moved half a step.** ⚠⚠ **What downgrades is the coupling**: the same round measured each stratum's **variance trajectory**, and the frequent attenders went from sd **0.862** (near-unanimous condemnation) to **1.373**, the largest rise, while the rarely-attending fell from 1.321 to 1.131 — **the stratum whose variance rose most is exactly the one that coupled most in `#745`.** Under the criterion written before the run, **`#745`'s coupling gradient is therefore downgraded: it may be a variance difference.** ⚠ **And my pre-registered directional prediction was backwards** — I reasoned that the stratum moving from 1.79 to 3.26 passes through the middle of the scale and should gain variance, **when the stratum actually passing through the middle was the churchgoers, starting from the floor. The measurement corrected the reasoning, which is exactly why the docstring said it had to be measured and not argued.** `[#747「方差升得最多的那一层耦合最强,而我预测的方向是反的」]`

⚠⚠ **And the last question was *who*, and the answer is the counter-intuitive one.** Splitting people by how often they attend religious services and recomputing the slope **inside each stratum**, ceiling-normalised: **frequent +0.00287 · occasional +0.00178 · rarely/never −0.00044** — monotone, and **the two ends get different verdicts**: frequent and occasional lie outside **their own** nulls, while **rarely/never sits inside its own** ([−0.00196, +0.00190]). ⇒ **The Americans who merged homosexuality into sexual morality over those thirty-six years are the churchgoers; among those who rarely or never attend, the two judgements never became more tightly linked at all.** ⚠ And this is about **coupling**, not permissiveness — both groups grew more permissive about homosexuality; what differs is whether that judgement became attached to their judgement about premarital sex. ⚠ The placebo guards this round's own trap: a random three-way split gives +0.00563 (84% of the total), so **stratification alone costs 16% while religion costs 36% — the difference is real.** ⛔ ~~One reading: among the rarely-attending, `premarsx` was already near the ceiling in 1988, so sexual morality is no longer a system there for anything to be filed into.~~ **The next round turned it into something testable and it was not supported.** Across the **15 strata that clear the floor** (4 cohorts · 3 attendance · 3 education · 4 regions · 2 sexes), pairing each stratum's early `premarsx` mean with its fusion slope gives **ρ = +0.0393** against a pairing-shuffled null of **[−0.5214, +0.5107]** — **inside the null**; dropping the three attendance strata gives **ρ = +0.6783, still the wrong sign**; shifting the early window to 1988–2000 **flips it to −0.0571**. ⇒ **That reading should no longer be repeated as if established.** ⚠ **And the design's resolution belongs in the same breath: the null's half-width is ±0.52**, so it could only ever have seen a very strong association — **"not supported" is weak evidence here, not a refutation.** ⚠ One further trap worth recording: `#745`① literally said *bin by `premarsx`*, but **`premarsx` is one of the two variables whose correlation is the outcome** — **stratifying on a person's own answer is conditioning on the outcome (Oldham 1962); only a group-level mean can serve.** `[#746「我给的那条解读没被支持,而这个设计只看得见极强的关联」]`. ⚠ **And my first verdict line called this gradient *collapsed*, because I divided one normalised slope by the other and the denominator was −0.00044** — **`#691` recorded it: a ratio of noisy near-zero denominators is heavy-tailed and has no resolving power. Changed to a difference, with each stratum judged against its own null.** `[#745「并进去的是常去教堂的人,而几乎不去的那一群从头到尾没连起来」]`

⚠ Three things must be said together: ① **this is still an upper bound** — the retest runs across *wordings*, mixing reliability with wording differences, so it underestimates reliability and over-corrects; ② the raw correlations here are **1994 raw Spearman (n = 894) with no ceiling normalisation** — the page's 0.4154 is a **per-year-median, ceiling-normalised** quantity, and **the two corrections are not composed in this round, so they must not be quoted against each other**; ③ the placebo passes: shuffling the **person-level pairing** between versions drops the retest coefficient from 0.83 to **0.07**, so that 0.83 really is one person answering twice `[#735「把测量误差校正掉之后,最弱的一对只有 0.30」]`. ⚠ Three shrinkages: ① **numerator only, no ratio** — `#731` has just established that a ratio is relative to its pool, and the two versions sit in different pools; ② `teensex1` **was asked in 1994 only**, so this is a one-off retest that **cannot be extrapolated to other years**; ③ ⚠ and one convention difference, confirmed in passing: these figures **pool the years**, while the page's **+0.4154 is a per-year median** — **two conventions on the same data (pooling all 21 years gives +0.4740), and they must never be quoted against each other** `[#732「换一套问法再问一遍,同一批人给出的还是同一块」]`. ⚠ Three candidate axes were each tested, and one came out backwards: **item count** costs a third (4.56× → median 3.10× at k=3, nowhere near 0.96×); **content** does not matter (the four drop-one ratios are 3.03/3.03/3.17/3.20, max/min = 1.06); and **population is reversed** — **restricted to NSFG's own women aged 15–44, GSS rises to 9.45×, while a random half-sample gives only 3.98×**, so this is not "a smaller slice": **the claim is strongest in exactly the population that should have weakened it.** ⚠ **And this round's first answer was wrong, manufactured by my own null**: the exclusion rule named two **k=4** sets, and a k=3 block can never equal a k=4 set, so **nothing was excluded** — the pool's four all-sexual triples stayed in the null and **its 95th percentile landed on one of them**. The tell was a digit-for-digit *observed == null 95th percentile*, giving 1.00× in all four cells. Only after excluding **any block lying entirely within one true domain** do the numbers above exist `[#730「两具仪器的分歧,84% 在分母,而分母是问卷的性质」]`. ⚠ **And the thing that did not move is exactly the thing I deliberately made load-bearing in `#716`: the ordering.** Purity > authority > ingroup > harm > fairness, position for position, under both conventions. **A binary turns over with the convention; an ordering does not** — which is why the ordering has to be what carries weight. ⚠ A third quantity fell out on the way: pairwise deletion and within-block listwise deletion differ by at most **0.0044** across these nine blocks, so the page's numbers are robust to that choice — and this was forced out by a positive control that failed on my first pass, which caught not the data but **me comparing four-decimal literals against full-precision values** `[#718「比值随符号约定腰斩,而两条判决因此翻面」]`.

- **A thirtieth, and it is a *cannot* plus a mechanism: in the questionnaire that divides sexual self-concept into twenty facets, sixteen of the twenty measure how the questionnaire was built rather than how people are organised.** The twenty-ninth put "sex is one block" on two instruments (0.416 and 0.346); this round went looking for a third. ⚠ **Its codebook does not state the facets at all** — a hundred item texts and three background variables, **with nothing anywhere saying which items belong together** — so *who did the grouping* is, in this release, **nobody**, and by the rule this page has already set, guessing is not allowed. The route that needs no facet key: reading the texts, item 1 and item 21 are both about anxiety, 3 and 23 both begin *I am very aware of my sexual…*, **so "period 20" becomes a falsifiable structural hypothesis**, and **whether it reproduces the previously measured within-facet and between-facet medians is the control that licenses the grouping**. ⚠ **The control failed the first time, because I dropped a symbol when re-quoting my own ledger**: the original says *median **|ρ|** = 0.5789 / 0.1726*, I re-quoted it as *0.579 / 0.173*, **losing the two bars**, and the code ran the signed version, putting between-facet at 0.066 instead of 0.173. On the original's absolute reading, within is **0.5856** and between **0.1734**, **a deviation of 0.0067, well inside tolerance** — **the ledger was complete; my re-quotation was not.** ⚠ Once it passed: **in sixteen of twenty facets the strongest pair is both correlated above 0.60 and built from the same words**, so they are recorded as undecidable; only three are genuine cliques, and the cleanest has a weakest link of **+0.699** and a strongest of **+0.915** with **zero word overlap** — that one's high correlation was not manufactured by paraphrase. ⚠ **And the third replication point is structurally unavailable**: this questionnaire contains **no moral judgement about sexual behaviour at all** (the two lexical hits read *if the sexual aspects of my life were to go wrong, I would be the person to blame* — attribution, not morality). **So "sex is one block" still has two points, and both are moral-judgement scales** `[#654 "我重引自己的账本时丢掉了定义那个量的符号,两条条目之后它弄坏了一道控制"]`.

- **A thirty-first, and it is an UNVERIFIED with no sentence about people in it — because it did not measure people but my own record, and it did not pass its own instrument.** The thirtieth found a new failure mode (dropping, when re-quoting one's own ledger, the symbol that defines the quantity); this round tried to measure it across 638 ledger entries. The preregistration required validating the vocabulary first: the known loss was caught ✅. ⚠ **Then the offset control settled the whole round**: the median loss rate for real re-quotations is **1.000**, and for **a randomly paired unrelated source sentence** it is also **1.000** — **identical**. *Should this zero be zero?* — no: a random pairing ought to lose almost everything, **it is a systematic baseline offset**, which is why an offset control was used rather than a placebo. **And with both sides equal, the instrument cannot separate a real re-quotation from two unrelated sentences; what it fails to measure is not the loss but the gap between loss and irrelevance.** ⚠ The other confound written before the run also fired: **355 of 463 pairs have a source sentence carrying no qualifier at all — those are not lossless, they are unmeasurable**, leaving 108 measurable of which 21 lose nothing. ⚠ **The diagnosis is that the unit is wrong**: qualifiers live in an entry's tables and surrounding context, not in the one sentence carrying the number, so a sentence-to-sentence comparison measures whether that sentence happens to repeat the words — and it almost never does, on either side. **Among the 108 measurable pairs the most-dropped qualifiers are controls, sample size, intervals, pair counts and held-out — but the instrument failed its offset, so that line is not reportable and serves only as a prior for the next round** `[#655 "真重引与随机配对都是 1.000,所以这具仪器测不到丢失与无关的差"]`.

- **A thirty-second, and it is an UNVERIFIED whose only surviving content is a limit: once the trend is removed, two morally unrelated series in this survey still move together at +0.57.** This page's opening sentence — that rarity and shame are both downstream of condemnation — **has never been directly tested**, and this national survey carries both **condemnation** (is extramarital sex wrong) and **prevalence** (has the respondent done it) across **17 shared years and 20,408 people**, so the question can be asked at the **decade** unit for the first time. ⚠ The estimand is the correlation of the two series' **first differences** (differences rather than levels, because the seventh already measured that *what looks like a shared climate is mostly a common trend*): **−0.3413**, 95% interval [−0.6373, −0.1221]. ⚠ **But the placebo failed, and heavily**: taking **support for the death penalty** — a moral series with nothing to do with sex — through the same differencing gives a correlation with condemnation of extramarital sex of **+0.5697, which is 167% of the main effect.** **Two morally unrelated things still move together after the trend is removed.** So −0.3413 cannot be read: the series carries a large **survey-year component**. ⚠ And the series names the suspect itself: the share calling extramarital sex *always wrong* sat at **0.78–0.82** for three decades and **fell to 0.6291 in 2021** — the year this survey moved to the web, and 2018→2021 is the largest single step in the whole series. ⚠ One structural limit belongs here too: **17 years give only 16 differences, and this design's MDE is |r| = 0.651** — **effects smaller than that are invisible to it**, so even had the placebo passed, a null could only have excluded large effects. **This round licenses no new sentence about people, and it narrows the old ones: every decade-unit number on this page must now face a shared component of 0.57 that survives detrending** `[#659 "差分去掉了趋势,而两件不相干的道德序列仍然同步在 0.57"]`.

- **A thirty-third, and it is the first sentence about people in this stretch: in the same year and the same questionnaire, people asked face to face call extramarital sex *always wrong* 11 to 19 percentage points more often than people typing into a screen — and the same contrast applied to the death penalty is essentially zero.** The thirty-second's placebo pointed at a survey-year component with one suspect: the 2021 move to web administration. ⚠ **Testing that as preregistered: dropping the 2021 and 2022 waves leaves the placebo at 1.09 times the main effect** (+0.4190 against −0.3850) — **the shared component is not 2021's doing** — so under the fixed condition, **whether rarity drives condemnation cannot be decided at the decade unit on this instrument; stop, no third attempt.** ⚠ **But this survey carries a column recording how each interview was administered, and in 2022 and 2024 both modes run in the same year** — so the suspect can be **measured directly rather than inferred**: 2022 gives face-to-face **0.7051** against web **0.5951** (a gap of **+0.1099**, 95% interval [+0.069, +0.151]); 2024 gives **0.7917** against **0.6057** (**+0.1860**, [+0.146, +0.226]). **Replicated across two independent years, and growing.** ⚠ The control is what matters: **the same face-to-face-versus-web contrast on the death penalty is +0.018 and −0.012** — **so this is not "people who answer online answer everything differently", it is that this particular thing is hard to say out loud to a person.** ⚠ One shrinkage is required: **mode is not randomly assigned**, it follows the sampling design and the respondent's circumstances, **so this is a difference between two groups of people rather than an intervention on mode** — the placebo rules out "the web group is simply more permissive", it does not rule out "social desirability weighs more on sexual items than on the death penalty". **What may be said is that this is said differently to a person than to a screen, and the death penalty is not; not that changing the mode caused it** `[#660 "被真人当面问的时候,说婚外性总是错的人多十一到十九个百分点,而死刑不动"]`.

- **A thirty-fourth, which demotes the thirty-third, and what killed it was my own check inside the same round: non-sexual moral items also move with the mode of asking — they simply move in different directions, and a signed median cancelled them out.** The thirty-third measured face-to-face running 11–19 points above web, but on one item only; if the mechanism is *hard to say out loud*, it should be large on sexual items and small on non-sexual ones. Across 19 items, two waves and two modes, the first pass looked clean: sexual median **+0.0735**, non-sexual **−0.0074**, a gap of **+0.0809** (95% interval [+0.033, +0.155]), ceiling-normalised **+0.1574** ([+0.062, +0.226]), both versions same sign and excluding zero, the positive control reproducing to four decimals and the placebo at 18% of the effect. ⚠ **Then the check: code 1 does not mean the same thing across items** — for *premarital sex is always wrong* it is the **harsh** answer, for *allow the incurably ill to die* it is the **permissive** one. **A signed median lets the non-sexual group cancel itself, and that bias runs in my favour.** Recomputed on absolute values, the non-sexual median is **+0.0378** rather than −0.0074, the gap falls to **+0.0357**, and its **95% interval [−0.020, +0.102] contains zero**; the normalised version likewise. **So "specific to sexual items" was manufactured by sign cancellation, and the thirty-third is downgraded to one item's observation.** ⚠ One **descriptive, untested** thing survives in the grid and may be the real shape: **all twelve sexual cells point the same way (12/12) while the non-sexual ones scatter (8 positive, 14 negative, 0.636 agreement)** — **not magnitude but direction**; and the four abortion cells also agree, in the direction matching the sexual items. **This was noticed after the failure, so it is a description and not a conclusion**, and the criterion is left to the next round `[#661 "非性道德题也有方式效应,只是方向各不相同,而我拿有符号的中位把自己骗了"]`.

- **A thirty-fifth, and what it corrects is a number I left behind myself one round ago: "all twelve sexual cells point the same way" is wrong — correctly oriented it is ten, and at this cell count the agreement rate cannot tell a real grouping from a random one.** The thirty-fourth diagnosed that *code 1 does not mean the same thing across items* and left "direction consistency" as the next round's preregistration — **but that 12/12 was computed on the unoriented raw differences, that is, using the very defect it had just diagnosed.** This round wrote the orientation table out item by item (sex education's code 1 is *favour*, i.e. permissive; pornography law's code 1 is *illegal to all*, i.e. harsh), and oriented the sexual items give **10/12, an agreement rate of 0.833** against the non-sexual 0.727. ⚠ Under the confound control fixed last round, resampling the non-sexual group down to the same 12 cells gives a median agreement rate of **0.750**, 95% interval [0.583, 0.917], and **the sexual group's 0.833 sits at the 76.6th percentile, empirical p = 0.2340.** ⚠ **And the placebo settled the whole round**: shuffling the sexual/non-sexual labels across the 34 cells gives a median absolute difference in agreement rate of **0.1061** — **127% of the observed 0.0833; random relabelling produces bigger gaps than the real split.** The positive control (oriented `xmarsex` still +0.110 / +0.186) and the meta check (flipping the whole orientation table leaves the magnitude unchanged) both passed, **so what is broken is not the orientation but the resolution at this cell count**: the placebo's 0.106 is the floor, and the effect of 0.083 lies beneath it. **⇒ The thirty-third stays at "one item's observation": those 11–19 points are real (two years, with a death-penalty placebo), but whether they are specific to sexual items is not answerable from this data.** And this is the second consecutive undecidable on one question, so the line changes direction here `[#662 "我诊断出了缺陷,然后把一个用这个缺陷算出来的数留给了下一轮"]`.

- **A thirty-sixth, and it closes this line: people say something different to a person than to a screen — and this data supports that sentence and nothing more.** After the thirty-third measured those 11–19 points, one genuinely psychological question had never been asked: **who is it that cannot say it?** Stratifying by age, sex, religious attendance and political view and computing the mode effect inside each stratum, the largest between-stratum gradient is **political**: liberals **+0.2274** against conservatives **+0.0517**, a gradient of **+0.1756**, 95% interval [+0.097, +0.256], excluding zero. ⚠ **But it died twice, and both deaths were fixed before the run.** ① **The placebo**: the same stratification applied to the **death penalty** gives a largest gradient of **+0.1211 — 69% of the effect**. **The death penalty has no mode effect of its own, yet it has a gradient of one**, so the gradient is not specific to sexual items. ② **And the trap fired too**: the highest-gradient stratum (liberals) **is exactly the stratum where the two modes differ most in composition** (median age +3.0, median education −2.0) — **mode is not randomly assigned, so this may only be which kind of person was given which mode, and it may not be read psychologically.** ⚠ One column in the grid is strikingly clean: **religious attendance is monotone** — almost never +0.1886 → occasionally +0.1121 → often +0.0914 → weekly or more +0.0498, **and it does not trip the trap** (the largest composition difference sits on the lowest-gradient stratum). **But its own death-penalty gradient is +0.0781, still 56% of it, and it fails the same placebo.** **A pretty monotone shape earns no exemption for being pretty** — recorded as description, not conclusion. ⇒ **The mode effect on that one item is real, and it can be shown neither to be specific to sexual items nor to belong to particular people. This line closes here** `[#663 "死刑没有方式效应,却有方式效应的梯度"]`.

- **A thirty-seventh, and it prices everything else on this page: 73% of what is said here stands on a single way of asking — and the first time I actually checked one of my own "no second instrument exists" judgements, it was wrong.** From round 101 onward a round does not close until its question has been asked on two instruments; **but the older claims here were never put to that test.** Counting the 11 standing claims one by one: **only one is already cross-instrument** ("sex is one block", GSS + NSFG), two are cross-coding-team within a single archive, and **the remaining eight each rest on one instrument.** ⚠ **The crux is that I am both the one who writes the claims and the one who rules that no second instrument exists** — so the rule was fixed first: any "cannot" must **print the candidate instrument's variable names and n before it counts**, and every "cannot" must carry a falsifiable form. **The first check fired**: I had been using only the 186-society ethnographic archive, while five others sit in the same directory — and the **Ethnographic Atlas carries *Norms of premarital sexual behaviour of girls* across 1,291 societies, 598 of them coded, on a scale ordered by severity of condemnation, from a different coder's project, and it overlaps those 186 exactly** (they are its subsample; roughly 500 more have never been used). **So the "no second instrument" ruling on "rarer means more condemned" was wrong, and it is reclassified as "to do".** ⚠ The other seven remain "cannot", **each carrying its falsifiable form** — e.g. *if any cross-cultural archive carries child corporal punishment coded separately by target with n ≥ 60, the "cannot" on the +0.845 result is overturned*. The two positive controls (no instrument asked about example-setting; only one instrument has both sides at the decade unit) still come out "cannot" — **the audit is not broken; my earlier searching was** `[#664 "这一页说的话里七成只靠一种问法站着,而我判它们换不了仪器时第一次真去查就查错了一条"]`.

- **A thirty-eighth, the first positive result in this stretch and the fourth bet I placed the wrong way: the less common premarital sex is in a society, the more it is condemned — and that is not one coder's handwriting.** This page's oldest +0.80 had condemnation and frequency **coded by the same project** (Broude 1976), in an archive where same-team variable pairs already correlate **2.14×** as strongly as cross-team ones — so the question worth asking is **whether it survives changing the condemnation side.** ⚠ Hard rule 1 reshaped the round first: the Ethnographic Atlas variable treated as a "second instrument" one entry ago **is already inside this archive** (same column, the title says so outright) — so this is not a change of archive but a **change of coding project**; and the same construct has been coded here by four projects. ⚠ Reading the codes saved the round twice: Murdock's column is **reversed in polarity** (higher means more permissive), and Frayser's top code reads *strongly disapproved **and rare*** — **the code itself binds condemnation to rarity**, so correlating it with rarity is forced by definition; **excluded and reported separately.** ⚠ The result: same project **+0.7966** (n=97), and with Murdock's coding **+0.6725** (n=86, 95% interval [+0.530, +0.801]) — **a drop of 0.124.** The positive control reproduces the original to 0.0034, the placebo is 0.0778 (12% of the effect), and the two projects' condemnation codings correlate only **+0.7064 < 0.90 ⇒ they are two genuinely different codings, not copies.** **The ratio is the point**: this archive's typical handwriting effect is 2.14×, **and this pair is only 1.18×.** ⚠ The remaining half is a real gap: **the rarity side still rests on one project**, the archive holding no second coding of premarital-sex frequency — **so this is a cross-project replication of the condemnation side, not of the whole relation** `[#665 "换一个编码项目重新打分,那个关系从 0.797 只掉到 0.673"]`.

- **A thirty-ninth, and it rescues the previous one from being a possible tautology: how many people in a society actually do a thing, and how that thing is spoken of, are two different things.** The previous entry established that *rarer means more condemned* survives a change of coding project (+0.797 → +0.673), **but the rarity ruler and the original condemnation ruler come from the same person's project** — if his frequency codes were merely his own attitude codes in other words, the whole relation would be a definition. ⚠ First the enumeration: of **45 frequency-titled variables in this archive, seven concern sexual practices and all seven come from one project** — **so a cross-project replication of the whole relation cannot be done**, and the falsifiable form is written as a column-name condition. ⚠ Then the codebook: none of the seven contains a single normative word in its code text (*universal / moderate / occasional / uncommon*), unlike the one excluded last entry, whose top code read *strongly disapproved **and rare*** in a single category. ⚠ **But clean code text does not prove the coder did not infer from norms, and that layer is directly testable**: with his own attitude coding partialled out entirely, does his frequency coding still predict **a different project's** norm coding? **It does — partial correlation +0.3094, 95% interval [+0.093, +0.520], excluding zero**; and the positive control (the reverse: attitudes still predicting norms with frequency partialled out) is +0.3180, also excluding zero — **each carries information the other does not.** Swapping the control for a variable unrelated to sex moves the estimate by **0.0145, 4.2% of the real control's 0.3417.** ⚠ One correction: my preregistration said *swapping in an irrelevant control should barely move it*, which is an **invariance check**, while the tool I called tests that *an effect must exceed a baseline* — **I used it backwards**; with the right one, both gates pass. **⇒ Rarity is not condemnation's shadow, and the previous entry's +0.6725 is therefore not a tautology** `[#666 "把他自己的态度码整个控制掉,他的频率码仍然预测得了另一套规范码"]`.

- **A fortieth, which replicates this page's headline shape on a second coding and simultaneously caught a hole in the very safeguard I had just installed: what a society does to boys and to girls, to small children and to older ones, is almost the same thing.** This page says *changing who is hit barely changes the number* (+0.839), **but both sides of that came from one coding publication** — so the question is whether it survives a change of coding. ⚠ The enumeration first: the other project **coded only one target**, so the cross-project contrast on corporal punishment **cannot be constructed** (recorded and stopped as preregistered, rather than passing one target off as four). ⚠ **And the enumeration itself was wrong**: my first pass printed n = 186 for every variable **because it counted rows in the data table rather than non-missing values** — **21 of 75 hits had ≥150 rows but under 60 real values**, among them a sex-split caretaker-warmth set whose true n is **5 and 6**, **which I nearly ran a whole round on as the second project.** **The ledger records this exact defect once already, and this round's enumeration existed precisely to make me check before speaking.** ⚠ Recomputed on true coverage, a real route appeared: a second publication carries a complete four-target coding of **obedience** (true n = 160–162). **The result: the corporal-punishment quartet's median is +0.8392 (reproducing the original exactly) and obedience's is +0.7905, while the two codings correlate only 0.2951 with each other — they are not measuring the same thing.** **⇒ Within one coding the target barely matters, across constructs they are nearly unrelated**, which is this page's eighteenth shape reappearing on a fresh pair. ⚠ Two boundaries: the two publications **share a first author**, so this is **cross-publication, not cross-independent-team**; and the placebo (obedience × latitude) is **0.2897, 37% of the effect** — passing but high `[#667 "一个为「先查再说话」而设的步骤,自己带着一个假覆盖的 bug"]`.

- **A forty-first, a tool plus a bounded conclusion: not one claim on this page is a victim of false coverage, and two rounds do contain cells below the floor.** The previous entry found me printing a variable's n as 186 — **because I counted rows in the data table rather than non-missing values** — and that is not peculiar to that round, **it is a property of this archive** (every variable fills all 186 rows, with missingness stored as NA). This round turned it into a tool: **compute true non-missing n by two independent paths and only begin auditing once they agree exactly**, then sweep every one of the **17 rounds** that cite this archive. **Three have a minimum true n below 30, and eight below 60.** ⚠ One of the three is this round itself — **the positive control was planted here deliberately** (a variable pair whose true n is 5 and 6, which the tool must flag); the negative control is the corporal-punishment quartet (true n 145/140/147/142, which **must not** be flagged) — **the two separate cleanly, so the tool works.** ⚠ **But the flag is a proxy, not a verdict**: it marks that a round **mentions** a low-n variable, **not that the variable carried the claim**. So I read the sources line by line: the low-n variable in both rounds is *violation of the incest taboo* (true n = 29), **appearing in a candidate pool, while those rounds' headline numbers route through two other columns with true n of 97 and 91** — **no victims on the page; but any cell involving that column has true n ≤ 29, below this project's own floor of 30, and is recorded as undecidable.** ⚠ The tool is reliable in one direction only: **not flagged ⇒ the round has no low-n variable; flagged does **not** prove the claim is broken** `[#668 "工具标的是「提到过」,不是「承担了」——所以它是必读清单,不是判决"]`.

- **A forty-second, closing an arc and pricing the page: four rounds of cross-instrument work moved the claims resting on more than one instrument from three to four — and only one is genuinely cross-instrument.** This stretch opened by finding that eight of eleven standing claims rested on a single instrument; four rounds then advanced them one notch each. The close-out first fixed a distinction the earlier table had hidden: **cross-publication with a shared first author is not cross-independent-team** — graded into four tiers, that gives **1 cross-instrument, 3 cross-independent-team, 1 cross-publication, 6 single-project**. ⚠ The whole constraint on this round was a sentence about myself: **I am the one judging "advanced"** ⇒ every "advanced" **must point to a specific round number and a specific number**, and the round number must be findable in the ledger. **Mechanical check: all five pass, zero unsupported**; the six "cannot" entries **all carry falsifiable forms**, none missing; and the positive controls (two already-known "cannot"s) **did not loosen under my re-reading**. **⇒ Every claim on this page now carries its instrument tier and its falsifiable form, so anyone can price it without trusting my wording** `[#669 "四轮跨仪器的工作,把站在一具以上仪器上的声明从三条推到四条,而真正跨仪器的仍然只有一条"]`。

- **A forty-third, an UNVERIFIED whose only reportable content is a direction: the older a person is, the more sexual morality behaves as one block — and both instruments point the same way.** The previous entry, closing its arc, noted that *sex is one block* is this page's **only genuinely cross-instrument** claim, and that it had never been asked the psychological question: **is it one block for everyone?** Each instrument was stratified and the **weakest link** measured inside each stratum (the least-connected pair, with its ceiling divided out). **The positive control passes on both**: the overall weakest links reproduce to 0.0099 and 0.0008. ⚠ **And the round died on two criteria I set myself, both of the same family.** ① I required that **the two instruments' highest stratum be the same stratum** — **but one instrument's top age band is 65-and-over while the other's sample stops at 45**: **that criterion can never pass on age**, another control that cannot pass. ② The placebo compared the wrong quantity: the non-sexual moral items' weakest links are **all negative** (−0.07 to −0.37) — **they do not form a block at all**, so that quantity is *the most anti-correlated pair*, not *the weakest positive coherence*; **the two are not comparable**. ⚠ Read by direction, only a description survives: **age agrees across both instruments** (the oldest band is tightest at +0.4781 in one and +0.3635 in the other), **while education disagrees** (monotone up to college in one; peaking at *some college* and falling back at *college+* in the other, where that cell holds only 389 people). **⇒ Until the criteria are fixed, "sex is one block" stands as an unconditional claim** `[#670 "一个判据要求两具仪器的最高层是同一层,而其中一具的样本上限就是 45 岁"]`.

- **A forty-fourth, which turns a finding that looked like it was about sex into one about how people answer questionnaires: the older and more educated a person is, the more coherently they answer **any** attitude battery — and sexual morality is not special in this.** The previous entry came back UNVERIFIED because two criteria were set badly; with them fixed (**no longer requiring the two instruments' highest stratum to be the same stratum, but comparing the rank correlation between stratum order and that stratum's weakest link**) the result is clean: the sexual block's tightness **does rise with age and education, and both instruments agree in sign** (age +0.80 / +0.50, education +0.80 / +0.80). ⚠ **But the placebo killed it, in the most informative way possible.** This round's placebo is the **three gender-role items** (same four-point format; the negatively worded one reversed after reading the item text; verified to block at **+0.4167**, marginally tighter than the sexual block) — **and it is simultaneously the rival hypothesis**: if the same gradient shows up there, it is not about sex. **It does, and more strongly on every stratifier (age +1.00, education +1.00, attendance +0.80, against the main effect's +0.80 / +0.80 / +0.60) — 125% of the effect, where the criterion required under half.** ⚠ **And this names an older failure**: an earlier round found that the mode effect's between-stratum gradient also appeared on the death penalty (69% of the effect) and could only record *the gradient is not specific to sexual items*; **the two failures are now one thing — a response-coherence trait, and this time it was measured head-on.** ⚠ One specification disagrees and is published with the rest: NSFG's age gives ρ = 0.0000 under quintiles against +0.50 under three bands, **so the age cell is specification-dependent** `[#671 "对手比主效应更强,所以这不是关于性的,是关于这个人回答任何一组态度题有多一致"]`。

- **A forty-fifth, and it withdraws a name I gave one round ago: the "response-coherence trait" was a name I put on a coincidence.** The previous entry found the sexual block's tightness rising with age and the gender-role block's rising more steeply, and unified two earlier failures into a trait — *how coherently a person answers any attitude battery* — **while that same entry wrote that this was an interpretation named in that round, not an independently measured construct.** This round measured it: enumerating every grouped attitude battery in this survey, **nine of ten genuinely block**, spanning **eight content families**; each was given the rank correlation between age-stratum order and that stratum's weakest link. **They scatter: gender roles and government-should-help at +1.00, sexual morality +0.80, spending +0.40, against library tolerance −0.20, speech tolerance, institutional trust and suicide −0.40, and abortion −0.80. Median −0.20, range 1.80, where the criterion required under 0.5.** ⚠ **And the null is what settles it**: with the age-stratum order **randomly shuffled**, the same-sign rate is **0.556** — **exactly the observed 0.556.** **"Several batteries agree in sign" was never evidence; shuffling produces it just as well.** ⇒ **The name is withdrawn.** ⚠ This is not a false retraction: **what falls is an interpretation that was never tested, not a measured number** — *the sexual block's tightness rises with age at +0.80 and gender roles more steeply at +1.00* still stands. ⚠ One descriptive line remains: **positive for gender roles, redistribution, sexual morality and spending; negative for tolerance, institutional trust, suicide and abortion** — which looks like a content split rather than noise, **but the null says agreement in sign is chance, and I preregistered no content split, so it is description only**. **⛔ And the next round withdrew it: letting a mechanical clustering on item text alone do the carving produces two entirely different clusters whose median ρ differ by only 0.20 at an empirical p of 1.00; while my own eyeballed split separates by 1.30 — exactly the 95th percentile of random splits, because I picked it by looking at the numbers** `[#673 "我用眼睛挑的那个划分,分离度恰好是随机划分的第 95 百分位"]`. ⚠ **And the cross-instrument gate blocked me once here, correctly**: a second instrument did exist (another moral questionnaire's five domains, n = 6,739) and I was about to write *no second instrument*. Run, its five domains give ρ of **+0.60 / −0.40 / −0.60 / −0.80 / −0.80, range 1.40, one of five agreeing** — **both instruments scatter, so this retraction holds across instruments; and the gate's value is that it made me do the work** `[#672 "打乱年龄层序后的同号率等于观测,所以「几个题组同号」从来就不是证据"]`。
- **A forty-sixth, and what dies here is not a psychological conclusion but the ruler I measured it with: "rank the strata, then take the rank correlation" cannot possibly be significant when there are only three or four strata.** The last three rounds all asked *for whom does this hold* about people, while the **society** unit on this page had never been asked at all — so back to the headline society-side claim: **for which societies does "+0.845 across targets" (all four targets of corporal punishment treated alike) hold?** The stratifier was printed first: **community size, eight ordered codes, joint with the corporal-punishment quartet on 139 societies** (three strata at 47/46/46), and this incidentally caught that **"fixity of settlement" is not monotone in its codes** (*impermanent, periodically moved* sits in the middle) ⇒ unusable as an ordinal stratifier, dropped. **The positive control passes**: the overall weakest link is **+0.8219**. **The placebo does not, and fails by an equal magnitude with the opposite sign**: the same stratification on the **affection quartet** gives **+0.5000** against the main effect's **−0.5000**. ⚠ **But the real cause of death is a third thing, and it is structural**: exhausting the permutation null of the rank correlation over k strata (for k ≤ 5 this is all k! permutations, not a sample) gives **a 95th percentile of 1.0000 at k=3 and 0.9700 at k=4** — meaning **nothing short of exactly ±1 can ever be significant**; this round's observed 0.50 has an empirical p of **1.0000**. ⇒ **Not "it holds for all societies", but "this ruler cannot answer".** ⚠ **And it bites back at a round two entries ago**: that round's headline used exactly a four-stratum rank correlation and reported **+0.80 (sexual morality) / +1.00 (gender roles) — whose permutation p values are 0.3333 and 0.0833, neither significant.** Redone at **deciles** for all nine batteries (where the null's 95th percentile falls to 0.6364) and corrected for multiplicity, **only two survive: institutional trust at −0.9000 (p=0.0008) and abortion at +0.7500 (p=0.0148), while sexual morality's +0.6848 does not**; ⚠ and **abortion flips sign between the two specifications** (−0.80 at four strata, +0.75 at deciles), published as found. **⇒ What shrinks is those two four-stratum numbers, not that round's conclusion — the opposite: since "sexual morality tightens with age" cannot itself clear multiplicity, "this is not about sex" is now stronger.** ⚠ And with only 186 societies, SCCS cannot yield ten strata of thirty, **so "for which societies does across-target invariance hold" is structurally unanswerable on this instrument** — recorded under what this cannot do, and not attempted a third time `[#674「四层上的梯度,是量不出来的」]`.
- **A forty-seventh, and the first claim on this page that carries a positive result and a cross-instrument failure at once: the more educated a person is, the more their judgements about sex behave as **one position** rather than four separate ones — and age does not do this; age does it to gender roles.** The previous entry showed that "rank the strata, then take the rank correlation" is a saturating statistic that throws away magnitude and keeps only order, so this round stops stratifying: a **kernel-weighted** pairwise correlation along years of education, take the **weakest link**, and make the main quantity the **end-to-end change**, with the null being **education shuffled across people** — the resolution comes from eleven thousand people, not from four strata. ⚠ **And having just diagnosed that error, the first version committed it again**: it still took the rank correlation over twelve grid points, and a kernel-smoothed curve is monotone by construction, so it sat at ±1, stayed at ±1 under shuffling, and the null's 95th percentile was 1.0 — all three bandwidths "failed to clear". **With the end-to-end change instead, the same null resolves immediately.** ⚠ **The strongest confound was written down first and controlled in the same iteration**: the two batteries come from different samples and different years (15,000 and 29,591 separately, losing 22% and 61% to the intersection) ⇒ rerun on the **same 11,576 people who answered all seven**, with both covariates. **The result is a clean crossover: on education, the sexual quartet gives +0.1349 (p=0.0000) while gender roles give −0.0322, on its own floor (p=0.18) — 4.19× and opposite in sign; on age it reverses exactly, the sexual quartet at +0.0318 on the floor and gender roles at +0.0906 (p=0.0033).** **A response-coherence trait cannot produce a crossover — it predicts both batteries moving on both covariates.** ⚠ And this rewrites the two entries before it precisely: **that round was right about age and wrong about education, and with its instrument it could not have known either**; while the withdrawn name *response-coherence trait* is **strengthened** by this, not overturned. ⚠ **On a second instrument it does not replicate, and that is published as found**: of the five domains of another moral questionnaire **only purity blocks** (+0.3313 against fairness 0.0630, ingroup 0.1429, authority 0.1785, harm 0.1386) ⇒ **that instrument has no legal placebo at all**; and purity's own education gradient is **−0.0535 at p = 0.0967, on the floor and opposite in sign to the +0.1349 here**. **⇒ This sentence holds for the four sexual-permissiveness items only, and may not be promoted into a claim about "sex" or about "purity" as concepts.** ⚠ One specification must be published with it: the placebo looked significant on **its own full sample** (p = 0.0400 / 0.0175 / 0.0050) **and falls back to the floor on the same people** — that significance was the different era brought in by a 61% sample loss, not an effect `[#675「教育让性道德变成一个立场,年龄让性别角色变成一个立场」]`.
- **A forty-eighth, and what kills the "it is just growing more permissive" explanation is not my construction but the placebo itself: education pushes both batteries toward permissiveness — and pushes gender roles **harder** — yet only for sex do the four judgements become bound to one another.** The previous entry established the education gradient without asking what it was made of: do the four items grow permissive together, harsh together, or genuinely more consistent? The marginals were printed first, and they handed over the strongest confound before any correlation was computed: **the homosexuality item's mean rises from 1.633 to 2.698, and the sexual quartet's weakest pair ceiling from 0.4658 to 0.5362 — the ceiling movement alone is 52% of the previous entry's observed value.** So it was split into four curves, reported separately: **level shift +0.2626, raw uncorrected weakest link +0.1030, the ceiling itself +0.0767, and ceiling-normalised +0.1536.** ⚠ **The decisive cut is marginal matching**: within each education window every item is replaced by its within-window empirical-CDF midrank, forcing the marginals level across windows so the ceiling cannot move by construction — **and the change does not fall; it rises, from +0.1536 to +0.1604 (p = 0.0000)**, meaning ceiling normalisation was conservative all along and this gradient cannot be marginal skew. ⚠ **But the clean blow comes from the placebo: gender roles have the larger level shift (+0.3035 against +0.2626) and a coherence change of +0.0054 at p = 0.8367, sitting on the floor.** A mechanism in which education makes people more permissive and thereby makes items more correlated predicts that the battery whose level moves more should also cohere more. **It does not. ⇒ Growing permissive and becoming one position are two different things, and education does only the second, only for sex.** ⚠ One mismatch of name and quantity, recorded against myself: this round's positive control is *named* "reproduces the previous entry's +0.4732" while what was actually planted is the mean of twelve kernel-weighted grid points, **0.4082** — not the same quantity; the gate passed but its name does not describe what it compared, the same family as the earlier case of redefining a quantity while requoting my own ledger `[#676「变宽容和变成一个立场是两件事,教育只对性做了后面那件」]`.
- **A forty-ninth, and it is the confound I preregistered one entry earlier, coming back half right: the battery really does tighten, but not evenly across its six edges — the homosexuality item is being pulled into the core, and nearly five times harder than anything else.** The previous entry used the **weakest link**, and a weakest link is a `min`, so **a min can move because one pair moved**; printing the six pairs caught it before any change was computed: **the weakest pair at both ends is extramarital×homosexuality (+0.3021 → +0.5247) — the whole of that +0.1604 was this one pair.** Computed per pair: **the three pairs containing homosexuality have a median of +0.2033, the three without +0.0423, and the placebo's three +0.0040.** ⚠ **Both preregistered gates pass**: the three pairs without homosexuality have a median of **+0.0423 against a shuffled-education null whose 95th percentile is 0.0313 (p = 0.0167)**, and are **10.6×** the placebo median — **so the previous entry does not shrink, but it now travels with a concentration factor of 4.8×**: the battery is tightening, and nearly four-fifths of the tightening is spent on the homosexuality item. ⚠ **One specification disagreement is published with it**: the crude endpoints (education ≤10 against ≥16) and the kernel weighting disagree materially in two cells — premarital×homosexuality is **+0.1011 against +0.0254**, and the placebo median **+0.0548 against +0.0040**. **One quantity, two estimators, two answers; neither merged nor chosen between — they agree on direction, and the conclusion rests only on direction** `[#677「整块确实收紧,而同性恋那一题被拉进来的力度是其余的近五倍」]`.
