- **A fiftieth, and it raises the sturdiest claim on this page while shrinking one I had raised three hours earlier: between 1988 and 2024 American opinion on homosexuality moved a whole point, and education's effect on how tightly that item binds to the other sexual judgements did not move at all across thirty-six years.** The previous entry settled which item moves without asking when; cut into three eras (n = 4,435 / 4,037 / 3,163) and recomputed, **the extramarital×homosexuality pair gives education changes of +0.2189, +0.1911, +0.2029 — same sign in all three, all far past their own nulls (p = 0.0000 throughout), and barely different in size.** Over the same span that item's era means rise from 1.842 to 2.759. **⇒ The level moves, the structure does not; this is education, not the era.** ⚠ **And the same table shrinks the other half of the previous entry**: *the remaining three pairs also tighten* **holds only in 1988–1998** (+0.0568, p = 0.0120), falling below its own null after 2000 **and below the placebo** (+0.0383 against +0.0435; +0.0245 against +0.0443). **⇒ The battery tightening as a whole is a 1990s fact; after 2000 only the homosexuality item is still being pulled in.** ⚠ This is the mirror of the risk that entry created for itself: it established *the pairs without homosexuality also clear their null* on a sample **pooling twenty-one years**, **and that p = 0.0167 was contributed by the first era — a significance pooled across eras can come entirely from one of them.** ⚠ Printing first was, this time, what removed a confound: I suspected in advance that a missing gradient in the later eras might only be a narrowed education distribution with too few grid points, and the three eras' education standard deviations are 3.030 / 3.019 / 2.915 with **all eleven grid points covered in every era — the suspicion was measured away, not argued away** `[#678「全国对同性恋的看法移了一整格,而教育在这件事上的作用三十六年没动」]`.
- **A fifty-first, and what caught it was not me but a rule I had written down: a conclusion that had passed both its positive and negative controls at p = 0.0000 collapsed at the step where the whole grid gets published.** The previous entry asked *why the homosexuality item*, and this one starts by doing something right: **the shared property is preregistered**, taken from a battery list that existed three rounds earlier — and that list happens to contain the cleanest cut available, since each of the three tolerance batteries carries one item whose target is homosexuals: **same battery, same format, same years, differing only in target.** Pooled, the main quantity is **−0.2484 at p = 0.0000, both gates passing.** ⚠ **Then all thirty-one items were published, and it overturned itself**: the three homosexuality items are **−0.3115, −0.2428, +0.1767** — **not the same sign, and that positive value is the largest in the whole table**; and the library battery's other three items are all positive too. **The split is by battery (may they speak / may they teach / may their book be shelved), not by target.** Recomputed within battery, the contrast is **−0.2111, −0.2632, +0.0671** — two negative, one positive. **⇒ This round's preregistered main quantity is withdrawn: pooling read a battery effect as a target effect, and the mechanism is recorded as unverified, not overturned.** ⚠ This is the third instance of one failure mode — a cross-item pattern that dissolves once you stratify by the preregistered grouping — **and this time the rule "publish the whole grid, do not report only the significant cells" did for me what I would not have done for myself.** ⚠ **And on that same full table two per-item results stand: all seven abortion items are negative at p = 0.0000 (n ≈ 13,000 each, with a total spread of only 0.1425), and all four euthanasia items are negative and clear their nulls — each item passing multiplicity correction over thirty-one, so this is eleven per-item results and not "several batteries agreeing in sign".** With the four entries before it: **education binds the four sexual judgements to each other while pushing them away from abortion and euthanasia. That is differentiation.** ⚠ The scope must be stated: not *decoupled from everything* — only two of five spending items clear, and the three tolerance batteries clear four of five each **while disagreeing in sign inside themselves** `[#679「我自己那条「全网格照登」的规则,推翻了刚刚通过两道门的结论」]`.
- **A fifty-second, and it withdraws the withdrawal: the previous entry declared the mechanism unverified and the split to be by battery, and that entire picture was manufactured by item coding polarity — a defect I had already named in my own ledger, repeated inside one window.** This round set out to measure *differentiation* as a quantity, and the very first step — printing the denominator — ran into it: the denominator was negative, and growing more negative. The codebook then exposed three separate polarity conventions — the sexual items run "1 always wrong … 4 not wrong at all" (permissive high), abortion and euthanasia run "1 yes / 2 no" (permissive low), and inside the tolerance batteries *may they speak* and *may they teach* are permissive-low while **may the book stay on the shelf is 1 remove / 2 not remove, permissive-high** — with one item, `colcom`, reversed against its own battery (4 means *fired*, not *allowed to teach*). **⇒ The previous entry's "split by battery" was this, and not a battery effect.** Aligned so that higher always means more permissive and rerun: **the seven abortion items go from −0.18…−0.32 to +0.18…+0.34, all four euthanasia items turn positive, and the within-battery homosexuality-versus-other-targets contrasts are +0.1836, +0.1823 and +0.0633 — all the same sign.** A new polarity positive control (after alignment, a battery's five targets must cohere in sign) passes on all five, and 23 of 26 items clear multiplicity. **⇒ The previous entry's self-retraction of the target mechanism was itself wrong: the mechanism is real and was disguised as a battery effect by a coding error; and *differentiation* was exactly backwards — it is integration.** **Education knits a person's moral judgements into one fabric — sex, abortion and euthanasia tightening together — and the single tightest thread, in every one of the three tolerance batteries, is the item whose target is homosexuals.** ⚠ What is untouched should be said plainly: the four entries before this all live **inside** the sexual quartet, whose four items share a polarity, so none of them is affected `[#680「上一轮的撤回本身是错的,而错的来源是一件我已经点名过的缺陷」]`.
- **A fifty-third, which demotes its own predecessor and trades it for something cleaner: education knits a person's moral judgements together — and that is a general factor, with sex no exception; the real exception is gender roles, which this fabric leaves outside.** The previous entry said education tightens sex, abortion and euthanasia together, and never asked the obvious question: **what if education tightens any two moral items?** This round computed the 325 pairs among the twenty-six external items as well: **external↔external moves +0.1549 with education, sex↔external +0.2107, a difference of only +0.0558 whose item-level bootstrap 95% interval is [−0.0164, +0.1140] — containing zero.** ⚠ The bootstrap unit must be the **item**, not the **pair**, because the 325 pairs share items and are not independent; bootstrapping over pairs narrows the interval, **and that was the one place this round could have fooled me**. **⇒ The previous sentence shrinks to "education tightens all moral judgements with each other, and sex is no exception" — a much weaker and truer claim, of which the general factor is 74%.** ⚠ **And the placebo delivers what is genuinely new here, in the opposite direction**: gender roles↔external is only +0.0539, a full 0.1009 below the general factor, **with an interval excluding zero** — **gender roles are the battery this fabric leaves out.** Together with the earlier crossover (education tightens the sexual quartet internally and does nothing to gender roles; age does the reverse): **education is doing two things at once — weaving sexual morality into the moral fabric, and leaving gender roles outside it.** ⚠ Scope must be stated: these twenty-six items are not a random sample of moral items — GSS asks what it asks — **so "general factor" holds within these twenty-six and may not be read as "all moral attitudes"** `[#681「大部分是一个一般因子,而例外不是性,是性别角色」]`.
- **A fifty-fourth, and it withdraws the control I had written at the end of the previous entry: entropy is not a confound but a mediator, so regressing on it is over-control — and what told me so was the print-first step.** The previous entry assigned most of the effect to a general factor, and I suspected in advance that the factor might merely be *more dispersed answers at high education, hence less ceiling compression*. **Printing the thirty items' normalised entropy at both ends killed that suspicion on the spot, and killed it backwards: the median entropy change is −0.1156, with only twelve items rising.** ⚠ The distribution is itself a finding: **every tolerance item drops hard (may-they-speak falls by 0.64), so high-education respondents are nearly unanimous; the sexual items rise instead (homosexuality +0.2729), so high-education respondents are more divided; and three of the euthanasia items rise too.** ⇒ The real risk is therefore the opposite one — a nearly unanimous item's ceiling collapses, and dividing by a tiny ceiling amplifies noise. Both specifications were run and both are published, and they give opposite pictures: **regressing out entropy drops the general factor from +0.1549 to +0.0343, absorbing 78%; while restricting to the twenty-one items whose ceilings have not collapsed leaves it at +0.1601, item-bootstrap interval [+0.0156, +0.2750], untouched.** **⇒ The adjudication comes from the print-first step itself: entropy moves with education, and a variable that moves with the treatment is a mediator, not a confound, so regressing on it removes real effect along with artefact. The preregistered control was the wrong one, the restriction is the right one, and +0.0343 is a lower bound rather than an effect.** ⚠ One generalisation deserves its own line: **preregistration does not protect you from choosing the wrong control variable** — I wrote that control before knowing entropy moves with education, and the first step of the same round told me, overturning the same round's preregistration. ⚠ A unit error in my own code is named rather than hidden: one cell of the rank specification printed −2.0960, a residual on the rank scale, **not comparable to the linear numbers and not to be quoted**; this round's verdict does not use that cell `[#682「我预注册的那个控制是过度控制,因为熵是中介不是混淆」]`.
- **A fifty-fifth, the first test on this line that could genuinely have forced the whole line to recant, and it did not: the *schooling* in "more schooling, more tightly knit moral judgements" is really schooling, not "people born later are simply like that".** Everything about education so far had used education alone, and education and birth cohort are famously entangled in American survey data. ⚠ **The print-first step killed my own preregistered confound for the second round running**: I had written that education and cohort must be strongly correlated so that stratifying would collapse the within-cell spread, and the measured correlation is **+0.1340**, with age only −0.0489, and the within-cohort-quintile interquartile ranges of education are 3, 4, 4, 4 and 4 years — **the stop condition written into the previous round passes, the entanglement is far weaker than I expected, and that is measured rather than argued.** Stratified: **the overall change along cohort is only +0.0382, while within the five cohort cells the change along education is +0.2103, +0.0718, +0.0727, +0.0447 and +0.0546 — five of five same sign, median +0.0718, which is 93% of the pooled figure** — against a placebo of +0.0208. **⇒ It is education.** ⚠ One incomparability I flag against myself: this round's ends are *twelve years or fewer* against *sixteen or more*, where the previous round used *ten or fewer* — **a narrower contrast gives a smaller change, so this round's +0.0772 and the previous +0.1601 are not the same quantity and are not in conflict**; comparability holds within the round, which is precisely the inverse of the error I nearly made last time. ⚠ Heterogeneity is published: **the oldest cell is +0.2103, three to four times the others**; this round does not explain it and only records that dropping it leaves the median at +0.0632, or 82% of the pooled figure — **the conclusion is unchanged**. ⚠ And what *it is education* means must be pinned down: **cohort is year minus age and correlates +0.4601 with year, so cohort and period are inseparable in this design; this round separated education from cohort only, and education is not randomly assigned — "it is education" means "within one cohort there is still a gradient along the education axis", not "education caused it"** `[#683「分层掉世代之后,教育效应留下 93%」]`.
- **A fifty-sixth, in which three of my own gates turned out to be broken — the third of them broken in the direction that punishes a good outcome — so this round's direction may be recorded only as the next round's hypothesis, never reported as a result.** The question is the one layer on this line that touches the *operator*: **does a person whose moral judgements are knit together draw finer distinctions between situations, or judge in blanket fashion?** ⚠ The first catch came before running: the main quantity the previous entry wrote down, *a person's within-block coupling*, **is not identified at the person level at all** — coupling is a correlation across people, and one person has no correlation — so *knit together* was redefined as the standard deviation of one person's standardised positions across seven batteries. ⚠ The second also came before running: printing first showed that **of the 36,840 people answering all seven abortion items, 46.3% (17,075) give seven identical answers**, leaving the rank correlation undefined for them — **and the preregistered handling was to exclude them, which would delete the very phenomenon under study** — so, before seeing any result, it became two quantities defined for everyone. ⚠ The third is the ugly one, and it is an interlocking pair: **the situational order was computed wrongly** (the script used a statistic that is ≈0.5 for any column, producing "rape, don't-want-more, health…" where the true order is "health, rape, defect…"), and **the positive control that should have caught it was written as a literal 1.0 — a check structurally incapable of failing, the fifth of its kind in this project.** Rewritten to compare the script's own computed order against the preregistered expectation, it passes 3 of 3. **Results: the Guttman channel dies on its own null — a shuffled situational order produces a larger correlation than the real one; while the dispersion channel clears its null in all five batteries, and after partialling out extremity and then the person's total response variance, all five keep their sign and four grow rather than shrink.** ⚠ **But the third gate was written to measure *how much it changed* rather than *how much was lost***: the abortion cell moves from +0.1043 to +0.2392, which is stronger, and the gate fired merely because it moved. **The criterion needs to be directional, and a criterion may only be changed before seeing results — I found this afterwards, so this round reports nothing. The direction is recorded, the criterion is fixed now for the next round, and it will be tested on batteries this round never looked at** `[#684「三道门是我自己写坏的,而第三道是「惩罚好结果」的那种」]`.
- **A fifty-seventh, in which the criterion was fixed by the previous entry before the run, it fired, and what it killed was my world-decomposition itself: *knit together* and *blanket judgement* are not two kinds of person — they are two ends of one quantity, how much this person's answers vary at all.** The previous entry left the direction *knit-together people answer more dispersedly across situations* unreportable, because its gate had been written to punish a good outcome. This round's criterion was fixed before running: **after partialling out the person's total response variance, the partial must keep its sign and stay at or above seven-tenths of the raw correlation**; and the battery is one this project had never used — **four situations in which a policeman strikes a citizen**. ⚠ Printing first caught another structural fact: **the sexual quartet and the police battery have a joint sample of zero** — GSS ballot design; they were never on the same form, and requiring five or more batteries plus the police items is likewise zero — so the quantity had to be rebuilt from four batteries, **and the four-battery and seven-battery versions also overlap in zero people, so the correlation between them does not exist at this site.** ⚠ The positive control passes 4 of 4, and this time against **the script's own computed order** rather than the previous round's literal: attacking with fists 92.3%, attempting to escape 76.0%, a murder suspect under questioning 9.9%, vulgar words 9.4% — every rank correct. **Result: the raw correlation is +0.0530 and clears its shuffled null; after partialling out total response variance it is −0.0360 — the sign flips, the ratio is −0.68, below the preregistered 0.7.** **⇒ The criterion lands on "the dichotomy does not hold".** ⚠ **This is the first time the meta-separator has genuinely succeeded, and what it killed is not one world but the decomposition**: both worlds presupposed that being knit together and discriminating between situations are two properties of a person, **when they are two readings of one quantity.** The stable positive correlation of the last few rounds came from both quantities being derived from that one — **and only on a held-out instrument sharing no items does partialling it out flip the sign.** ⚠ The degradation is stated too: this kill holds for the four-battery version of the quantity, **and whether it holds for the seven-battery version is structurally unanswerable here**; but the degradation runs in the conclusion's favour — a weaker instrument makes confirmation harder, and this round is a kill `[#685「判据是提前写死的,而它杀掉的是我的世界分解本身」]`.
- **A fifty-eighth, and it folds the eleven before it into a single sentence: the more educated a person is, the less their answers fluctuate across a whole questionnaire — they are not drawing finer distinctions across more issues, they are gathering every issue into one position.** Killing the discriminating-versus-blanket dichotomy, the previous entry had incidentally raised a more basic quantity — **how much a person's answers vary at all** — which this line had never once looked at directly. Neither of the two stop conditions printed first was triggered: its correlation with *how many items you answered* is only +0.2089 (not a completion metric), and the item set swings from eight to thirty-three items by year, so **every computation is confined to the longest run with a fixed item set, 1988 to 2018, eighteen waves, just over thirty-eight thousand people**. The positive control reproduces the item-level result of two entries back (at high education the tolerance items' entropy falls by 0.3637 and the sexual items' rises by 0.1467). **Result: education's correlation with total variation is −0.1680, −0.1783 after partialling out item count, against a shuffled-education null of only 0.0091 — five percent of the effect; recomputed inside five cohort cells it is −0.2730, −0.1490, −0.1135, −0.1020, −0.0923, five of five the same sign, with the median at 68% of the pooled figure.** ⇒ The criterion the previous entry fixed in advance lands on the affirmative branch: **it is education.** ⚠ **And this explains why the previous entry had to kill that dichotomy**: the three apparently different things education does — shrinking within-person fluctuation, tightening batteries with one another, making tolerance items nearly unanimous while the sexual items grow more divided — **are one thing: gathering every issue into a single position. *Finer discrimination* and *blanket judgement* are two descriptions of that one compression, which is why they could not be told apart.** ⚠ Heterogeneity is published and it is monotone: **the oldest cell is −0.2730 and the youngest −0.0923, weakening to about a third across generations**; this round does not explain it and only records that it is a different quantity from *the structure did not move in thirty-six years* and must not be read together with it `[#686「读书越多,整份问卷答下来的起伏越小」]`.
- **A fifty-ninth, and it is the result I wrote down in advance as the one I would not want: education's main work is pushing people toward permissiveness, and *gathering every issue into one position* is 2.4 times stronger on the permissive side.** Five consecutive rounds confirming one story is itself an alarm, so this round's design named the unwelcome positive outcome explicitly: **if the compression is one-sided, then "compression" is merely a restatement of "educated people are more liberal", which is among the oldest findings in survey research.** Printing first: stance correlates −0.2189 with total variation (inside the preregistered 0.3 threshold, so the split is usable), and in the lowest decile of total variation the permissive side holds 79.5% against the harsh side's 20.5%, where the full sample is fifty-fifty. ⚠ Because part of that 79.5% is manufactured by the −0.2189, **a random split is not a sufficient placebo**: a random split gives a two-sided ratio of 0.996 (interval 0.884 to 1.122), while **a purpose-built variable correlating −0.2189 with total variation but carrying no stance content gives 1.164** — **the selection structure is worth only 1.16. The observation is 2.436.** **⇒ The compression is directional.** ⚠ **The previous entry therefore narrows, though it is not withdrawn**: its pooled −0.1680 behind *gathering every issue into one position* comes 2.4-fold from the permissive side, **and should read "mostly gathering into one particular position"**; its core survives because **both sides share a sign** — the harsh side tightens too, at −0.0547, **which "education equals liberalism" does not predict**. ⚠ The prior art must be stated first and by me: this round's incidental **correlation of +0.3555 between education and permissive stance is among the most replicated results in survey research, and it is a verification rather than a finding of this project**; the only possibly new thing here is that 2.436 asymmetry, and it counts as a result only because the correlation-matched sham has already absorbed the 1.164 `[#687「我预注册要它能发生的那个不受欢迎结果,发生了」]`.
- **A sixtieth, the one test on this line capable of demoting its own twelve rounds wholesale, and it demoted half of them: "education tightens all moral judgements with one another" does not survive holding stance fixed — what tightens is the stance, not the structure; while "education flattens a person's answers across the whole questionnaire" retains more than half after stance is held fixed, and is not a shadow of stance.** The hole the previous entry exposed was that education's correlation with permissive stance, +0.3555, is more than twice any compression effect this line has measured, and twelve rounds never put it in a model. ⚠ Computing the preregistered confound first turns out to be a finding in itself: stance is the mean of thirty-three items and total variation the standard deviation of the same thirty-three, **which on a bounded scale must be related** — permuting each item independently across people (marginals preserved, all cross-item structure destroyed) gives a mean–SD correlation of **−0.2086** against an observed −0.2189, **so 95% of it is arithmetically forced**. That makes the half that survives below harder, and the half that collapses less arguable. **The verdict splits: within-person compression goes from −0.1574 to −0.0872, retaining 55% with its sign; the general factor goes from +0.0616 to −0.0088, retaining −14% with its sign flipped, and its five stance-quintile cells are −0.0161, +0.0063, +0.0412, −0.0088, −0.0933 — entirely scattered.** ⚠ The placebo shows this is not a method artefact: swapping stance for cohort in the same operation, the two quantities retain 102% and 96%. **⇒ "Education tightens all moral judgements with each other, and sex is no exception" is demoted; and since "the exception is gender roles" rests on the same general factor, it is demoted with it to "not tested with stance held fixed".** ⚠ One incomparability flagged against myself: this round's general factor of +0.0616 is not the same quantity as the earlier +0.1549 — narrower ends, narrower window; a narrower contrast gives a smaller value, and that is not a contradiction `[#688「绑紧的是立场,不是结构;而人内压缩不是立场的影子」]`.
- **A sixty-first, half a registration of something structurally impossible and half the second genuinely cross-instrument claim on this line: "the more educated, the less a person's answers fluctuate across a whole questionnaire" survives a different questionnaire, different people and a different item set.** The question left open was whether flatter answering means **less extremity** or **more consistency** — identical in total variation, opposite in this project's terms. ⚠ Printing first killed it on the spot: **twenty-seven of GSS's thirty-three items are binary, and extremity has no definition on a binary item** — every answer sits at an endpoint — leaving only six four-point items. **⇒ The decomposition is structurally impossible on GSS, and the rules forbid marking an impossible criterion "planned".** Measured incidentally on those six, extremity correlates +0.2713 with total variation, **so the question is real; this instrument simply cannot answer it.** ⇒ Hence a change of instrument: another moral questionnaire's thirty-four items are all six-point, with 3,820 people answering all of them and reporting education. **The positive control is this round's product: on the second instrument, education correlates −0.0542 with total variation, the same direction as GSS's −0.1574, and clears its own null.** ⚠ The decomposition half is UNVERIFIED, and the reason is a gate I chose wrongly, stated plainly: the gate I used means *the null must be under half the effect*, and at n = 3,820 the null's 95th percentile is 0.0332, **which demands an effect above 0.066** — a very large value for a quantity of this kind. **That gate asks about power, not validity; and a criterion may not be changed afterwards, so the decomposition is recorded UNVERIFIED.** ⚠ By the rules this is the first UNVERIFIED on *what the compression is*, **and if the next round returns another, the direction must change rather than chase a third** `[#689「分解在 GSS 上结构性做不到;换到第二具仪器,复制成了而分解没成」]`.
- **A sixty-second, the last attempt the rules allowed, with the criterion fixed in advance — and it resolved: more educated people give more extreme answers, and at the same level of extremity they are more consistent with themselves. "Answering more flatly" is an aggregate that had two opposite things pressed together — education does not make people milder, it makes them stand more firmly in one place.** The previous entry ruled this decomposition structurally impossible on GSS (twenty-seven of thirty-three items binary, extremity undefined), and printing first located a third instrument in `data/external/`: an authoritarianism scale with **twenty-two same-scale items on nine points**, 9,881 respondents, **and three fake words built in as a validity check, which 24.3% of people claimed to know**. The sample definition was fixed before the main analysis (all items answered, education present, no fake word ticked), giving 7,258; the ten reverse-keyed items were identified empirically from item–total correlations rather than from memory. **Results: education correlates −0.1242 with total variation, +0.0980 with extremity, and −0.0970 with dispersion-given-extremity, all three clearing their own shuffled nulls.** ⚠ **And the positive control is itself the first three-instrument claim on this page: GSS −0.1574 (a probability sample), the second instrument −0.0542, this one −0.1242 — three instruments, one direction.** ⚠ Two defects of my own, caught before publishing and both recorded: **first, the synthetic baseline was structurally incapable of returning anything but zero** — I computed the two quantities from two *different* shuffles, destroying their pairing, **an inverted "check that cannot fail" which makes the observation look maximally non-mechanical**; recomputed from a single shuffle, the baseline is +0.0617 against an observed −0.4316, **so the forced part is only 14% and points the other way, and the correction strengthens the conclusion**. **Second, the third specification filtered nobody** — that variable takes a single value across all 9,881 rows, so the filter removed zero people, **another check incapable of failing, and the specification is void**. ⚠ The scope may not be dropped: this instrument is a self-selected online sample, **so the decomposition holds in a self-selected sample only**; the direction, however, also holds in GSS's probability sample, so only the decomposition is limited `[#690「教育不是让人变温和,是让人更笃定地站在一处」]`.
- **A sixty-third, whose real product is not the psychological conclusion but something about my own criterion: the band I wrote from intuition gave the right answer this time, and would have given the wrong one at 2.3.** The previous entry split "answering flatly" into two opposing things — more extremity, and more consistency at equal extremity — so this one asks which of them carries the earlier finding that compression is 2.4 times stronger on one side. ⚠ Printing first closed one route and handed over a finding on the way: the same *stance-versus-total-variation* correlation is **−0.22, −0.17 and +0.77** across the three instruments — the third reversed and four times larger, because it is a single ideological scale with reverse-keyed items, **and the reverse-keying itself manufactures that coupling**; under the preregistered contamination threshold, **only the second instrument has both a computable extremity and an uncontaminated split**. Three of the four resulting cells fall inside their own nulls, with two-sided ratios of 1.967 for extremity and 2.834 for dispersion. ⚠ **And here is the real event: I ran the nulls for those ratios — the extremity ratio's null has a 95th percentile of 17.3**, because the denominator is near zero and a ratio of two noisy correlations is heavy-tailed, **so the statistic has almost no resolution there**; the dispersion ratio's null sits at 2.686 against an observed 2.834, an empirical p of 0.0423, **barely clearing**. **⇒ My preregistered [0.5, 2] was written from intuition rather than measured from the null; it agrees with the calibrated null this time, but at an observed 2.3 my band would have said "directional" where the null says "nothing".** The generalisation deserves its own line: **a threshold on a ratio must be measured from that ratio's own null and never chosen by intuition.** ⇒ So the conclusion is stated at its weakest: the 2.4-fold asymmetry belongs to the *more consistent* component, at p = 0.042, with three of four cells inside their nulls, a denominator indistinguishable from zero, and a self-selected sample — **and it does not enter *What stands*** `[#691「一个凭直觉写下的区间,这次给对了答案,而在 2.3 那里会给错」]`.
- **A sixty-fourth, a closure round, and its sentence is about how I speak: "the homosexuality item is pulled in nearly five times harder" — the *nearly five times* is indistinguishable from noise, while each group's own value stands.** The previous entry found my preregistered band had been written from intuition, so this one put every ratio this line has used back into its own null. ⚠ Printing first caught my own bad instrument: the ledger and pages contain 192 places with a "times" expression, **and the overwhelming majority are "N times its own spread" — an effect divided by a standard error, whose null is well behaved, and not the target at all**; the target class is defined by a rule — a ratio whose numerator and denominator are both estimates — and by that rule six page claims are affected. ⚠ The preregistered worry landed: **the round behind the earliest 1.10× persisted no results at all, so it cannot be recomputed, recorded as such and not reconstructed by approximation**; two more were not done, one of them because **the statistic I built is not the same quantity as the number on the page, and the recomputed 24.0 may not stand in for the page's 4.19**. **The positive control reproduces to the digit (1.967 / 0.3837 and 2.834 / 0.0423), so the verdicts are admissible: the two multiples come back at empirical p of 0.1000 and 0.0800, against nulls whose 95th percentiles are 9.86 and 28.17 — both indistinguishable.** ⚠ What is withdrawn must be stated precisely: **that round's gate was never the ratio** but "the three pairs without homosexuality have a median of +0.0423 against a shuffled-education null of 0.0313, p = 0.0167", **a z-type test which stands and is untouched here**; what falls is only the standing of "4.8×" and "10.6×" as quantities — **the denominator sits too close to zero, so each group's value stands while their ratio does not.** ⇒ The page keeps both values and drops the assertion of a multiple. **The generalisation: when the denominator is a small effect that has only just cleared its own null, "A is N times B" carries no resolution, and the two values with their own nulls should be reported instead** `[#692「两组各自的值站得住,而它们的比不站得住」]`.
- **A sixty-fifth, another closure round, and it has no sentence about people — I will not invent one: the preregistered audit failed, and the reason it failed is that most numbers on this page have no null behind them.** The previous entry's generalisation was that a multiple carries no resolution when its denominator is a small effect only just past its own null, so this round set out to count how many conclusions on this line stand on such a denominator. ⚠ **And the positive control the previous entry had fixed in advance killed the audit on the spot**: I used a heuristic to **guess** which effect belonged to which null, and guessing a pairing is not a measurement — it matched one entry to two numbers from entirely different tests, and the 1.35 that was specified as the thing to reproduce was not reproduced. **⇒ The audit is void and no fragility list is published.** This is the same family as what the previous entry caught: **a loose regular expression treated as a measuring instrument, for the second time in one day.** ⚠ One number does not depend on the pairing and can therefore be reported, but only after the instrument checks itself: two known entries had to be hit by their respective patterns, and both were. **Result: of the 93 entries after the six-hundredth, those reporting their own null number 13 on the strict reading (14%) and 20 on the loose one (22%); 55 merely contain the word, which is an upper bound and not a measurement.** ⇒ **An interval, not a point: only 14–22% report their own null, leaving 78–86% where fragility is structurally undecidable.** ⚠ **And this nails down the previous entry's generalisation**: since most entries never reported a null, whether a given denominator is one of those barely-past-noise effects is, in most cases, **unknowable** — not an analytic difficulty but **missing data**; so a third attempt at the count would be futile, and the next step is to turn "every conclusion carries its own null" into a rule on the gate `[#693「清点失败了,而它失败的原因是这一页上大多数数字后面没有零」]`.
- **A sixty-sixth, a delivery, and it has no sentence about people — I will not invent one: two consecutive failures pointed not at the analysis but at a rule that was never mechanically enforced, that every conclusion must carry its own null, so this round made it a rule on the gate.** The previous entry measured that only 14–22% of the later ledger reports its own null, so a third audit would be futile — the data does not exist. The new rule's proxy ledger is written into the function: **it is reliable in one direction only** — an entry with an effect and no null expression really has not reported one; **the converse does not hold, since containing a null expression does not prove that null belongs to that effect**, which is exactly where the previous entry died on "guessing a pairing is not a measurement". The merge criterion was verified **on the real gate** rather than in a harness: the planted entries were blocked and passed respectively, and the ledger was restored to the byte; the full backtest produced zero false positives; and 201 historical entries are named without being blocked. ⚠ **Two defects of my own turned up during the merge, and together they would have made the number "0" meaningless**: first, my guard condition was satisfied by a **substring** (a four-space string is a substring of the eight-space line), so the insertion never ran **while the script printed "merged"**; second, **the gate displays 0 for a missing key**, so a rule that was never wired is indistinguishable in the report from one that ran and found nothing. **Together, I came within one step of recording an unwired rule as "merged and clean".** What caught it is the old rule that **an unverified zero is silence, not an acquittal**: I insisted on calling the function directly, it returned 201 while the report showed 0, and only then did the contradiction surface. The fixes are to anchor the insertion after the `except` block with an assertion, and to display −2 rather than 0 for a missing key `[#694「把规矩变成闸,而合入过程里抓到两处让「0」失去意义的缺陷」]`.
- **A sixty-seventh, another closure round, and it turns an ugly fact into a visible mark on the page: for three quarters of the claims under *What stands*, I cannot check whether the null belongs to the effect.** The gate delivered last time catches "no null reported" but not "a null that does not belong here" — which is exactly what killed the round before it. ⚠ Having already failed once by guessing pairings, this round **forbids any heuristic pairing**: only effect–null pairs that the round's own script wrote into the same output are accepted, **paired by that script's code rather than by me**, and everything else is recorded as unmatchable. The positive control ran first: one round's three nulls had to appear verbatim in its own output — they all do, so the count is admissible. **Result: of twenty-four rows, seven are pairable (29%); eleven come from rounds that produced no script output at all, mostly the earliest society-side rounds; six have output containing no null.** The remaining seventeen now carry a "null not paired" mark on the page, with a legend. ⚠ **What the mark is not must be stated plainly**: it does **not** mean the claim is wrong, it means **I cannot check whether that null belongs to that effect** — one of them, for instance, states its gate perfectly clearly in the ledger prose (an effect of +0.0423 against a null of 0.0313, p = 0.0167), **and merely never had it written into the script's output, so automatic checking cannot reach it**. **Unpairable is not wrong, and conflating the two would manufacture a false retraction, which is as permanent as a false confirmation** `[#695「页面上四分之三的声明,我无法核对它的零配不配得上」]`.
- **A sixty-eighth, back to the object after four rounds spent taking my own work apart — which was a basin — and it puts the page's oldest society-side claim back inside the scope it actually has: "changing who is hit barely changes how hard a society hits" reproduces, and it rests entirely on one team's marks for 186 societies.** Printing first delivered the round's most important fact: **all four corporal-punishment items come from a single coding project** (non-missing n of 145, 140, 147 and 142), as do the four affection items; **and an earlier round already measured that a shared coding project inflates correlations by roughly 2.14×.** Rerun through today's pipeline: **the same-project median over six pairs is +0.8679 against the ledger's +0.845, a difference of 0.0229 that is smaller than its own null, so the old value reproduces** — and this time the effect and the null were written into the same output, **so the "null not paired" mark the previous entry gave this row can be removed for this claim**. ⚠ Reported separately as preregistered: **the twenty-eight cross-project pairs have a median of just +0.0617, ranging from −0.191 to +0.685** — **ask the question with another team's variables and the number is gone.** ⚠ **But this is not a retraction, and the reason must be stated: cross-project variables measure different constructs** (marriage form, stratification, warfare frequency) and are **not an equivalent control**, which I registered as structurally impossible before running. What can be said is scope: **+0.845 is the consistency among one team's marks for four targets across one set of societies**, not the proposition that a society's severity toward different targets holds under any independent reading. ⚠ And this round is recorded UNVERIFIED because **my own null was built wrong**: I permuted only one of the four items, so three of the six pairs were untouched and their real correlations stayed in the median, pushing the null to 59% of the observation. **A null that does not destroy the structure it claims to destroy is not a null** `[#696「这一页最老的社会侧声明,六对全在一个编码团队里」]`.
- **A sixty-ninth, and it is the outcome I had written down as the one I did not want, and it won: "changing who is hit barely changes how hard a society hits" and "this society received similar marks overall" are the same sentence.** ⚠ First I had to refute the tight null I myself wrote one entry earlier — **"shuffle only the society labels, keep each row intact" is a check incapable of firing**, since permuting row labels merely renames rows and leaves every column-pair correlation identical; **the correct tight null shuffles each society's own four scores among its four targets**, preserving that society's overall severity and destroying only which target got which mark — **which is exactly a halo test**. Before running, the loose null was shown to shuffle genuinely (zero of six pairs left untouched). **Result: the observation is +0.8679 against a loose-null 95th percentile of just 0.0895 — the instrument fires; while the halo null's median is +0.8558 with a 95th percentile of 0.8670, so the observation clears it by 0.0009, one tenth of its own spread.** ⇒ **The halo world won.** ⚠ A line in my own script saying "clears the tight null" was wrong and is named here: it compared against a quantile with a **bare greater-than**, and 0.8679 > 0.8670 is not clearing anything at a spread of 0.01; **the gate judged correctly and my print line misled** — the same family as "a threshold must be measured from its own null". ⚠ **And it does not consume the finding that changing the technique cuts the number by three quarters**: the halo null shuffles only among **targets** and says nothing about techniques, so the correct restatement is that **the coder's halo operates inside a technique block rather than globally** — which sharpens that claim rather than overturning it. ⇒ The page action: this page's oldest society-side claim leaves *What stands*, because **what it states is not "a society treats different targets alike" but "a society received similar marks overall", and in this design the two are inseparable while the latter is very nearly self-evident** `[#697「「换谁挨打不改变力度」和「这个社会整体被打了相近的分」是同一句话」]`.
- **A seventieth, and it completes the previous entry's bad news into a whole sentence: the same halo null swallows +0.8679 entirely on the *target* dimension yet reaches only 0.1684 on the *technique* dimension — the coder's halo explains who gets hit, and does not explain which technique is used.** The previous entry demoted this page's oldest society-side claim to a halo and pointed out that the only thing possibly left unexplained was the coupling between blocks. Printing first: that coder left 144 variables in all, with the disciplinary techniques organised as **ten blocks by four targets**, the five blocks' non-missing n running from 134 to 169, and **only 97 societies scored on all five** (the intersection is far smaller than any single block, published as found). **Result: the median absolute normalised correlation across the ten block pairs is +0.3241; the loose null's 95th percentile is only 0.1183, so the instrument fires; and the halo null's 95th percentile is 0.1684, which the observation clears by 0.1558, or 15.6 times its own spread.** All three gates pass. ⚠ **The signed median of −0.0720 must be reported alongside the absolute one**: it sits near zero because the signs genuinely differ — an earlier round measured the directions, and **a society that hits more also lectures more, permissiveness travels with affection, and hitting opposes permissiveness**. **Reporting only the absolute value would turn "there is a pattern" into "everything is high"; reporting only the signed one would turn it into "there is nothing".** ⚠ A level mismatch flagged against myself: the earlier +0.2288 was computed per target and then medianed, while this round correlates block means — **they are not the same quantity and neither may be cited as a replication of the other**. ⚠ And the heaviest limitation goes last: **all five blocks come from one coder, so between-block and within-block share the same handwriting; this round's halo null models exactly "the same society received similar marks", but modelling is not removing — real halo and real structure are inseparable at this site** `[#698「halo 解释得了「谁挨打」,解释不了「用哪一种手段」」]`.
- **A seventy-first, and it gives the one surviving society-side structure its shape: how a society raises children is not a single dial of severity — it lies along an imposition-versus-warmth axis, with hitting and lecturing at one end and permissiveness and affection at the other.** ⚠ The main quantity the previous entry had written down was retired on the spot, by that entry's **own** stop condition: a match count over ten signs takes only eleven values, fewer than twenty, **and the entry itself said not to report a p on so coarse a grid**. The replacement was fixed **before the correlation table was printed**, since otherwise the prediction would be post hoc: the semantic loading comes from the codebook's wording rather than the data (hitting +1, lecturing +1, example-setting 0 with its four pairs excluded in advance, permissiveness −1, affection −1), and the statistic is the mean of predicted-sign times observed correlation over the six pairs — a continuous quantity, neither an eleven-level count nor a rank correlation over few points. **Result: all six predicted signs are correct and the statistic is +0.3243; the loose null sits exactly on zero (median +0.0003, 95th percentile +0.0750); the halo null's 95th percentile is +0.1231, which the observation clears by 0.2012, twenty times its own spread. All three gates pass.** ⚠ Three things stated plainly against myself: **the axis is not uniformly strong** — lecturing against affection is only −0.0603, the weakest link of the six; **example-setting was excluded in advance and cannot be spoken about here**, and its +0.3165 with affection is written down only so it is not hidden, **as description rather than a test**; and **the levels are not comparable** — the earlier +0.3438 was computed per target and then medianed while this round's +0.3486 correlates block means, **and near-identical numbers are not a replication**. ⚠ The heaviest limitation is unchanged: all five blocks come from one coder, and **the halo null models that without removing it** `[#699「一个社会怎么管孩子,是一条「施加↔温暖」的轴,而这个形状不是 halo」]`.
- **A seventy-second, and it is the step that joins this project's actual question to the one surviving society-side structure: the more a society raises children by coercion and the less by permissiveness, the more it condemns premarital sex — two things on one axis. And what reaches sex is the coercive end; affection is essentially orthogonal to sexual norms.** ⚠ Printing first caught my own bad instrument again: searching on "sexual" hits 248 variables, **the overwhelming majority being sexual division of labour rather than sexual norms**; narrowed by **source**, a decidable rule, only two coding projects actually code sexual norms, **and neither is the child-rearing coder**. After checking each codebook's polarity, one variable survives: attitudes to premarital sex (1 "expected" through 6 "strongly disapproved", a clean ordinal, joint sample of 75 societies); the extramarital item's codes are **types rather than intensities** ("double standard, husband only" sitting in the middle) and it is dropped for the same reason an earlier round dropped its counterpart; a third is both unorderable and down to 32 societies. **The direction was fixed before running: on one axis, the correlation should be positive. It is +0.3631, against a loose-null 95th percentile of 0.2335 and a halo-null 0.2381, which the observation clears by 0.1250 — 12.5 times its own spread; and since this is a cross-project pair whose baseline is only about 0.105, the observation is 3.5× that floor.** ⚠ **The full grid delivers something more interesting than the headline**: permissiveness −0.4299, corporal punishment +0.3826, lecturing +0.2569, **and affection just −0.0208, essentially nothing**. ⇒ **The previous entry named this axis imposition-versus-warmth, and this one shows that only the imposition half reaches sexual norms. Whether a society hits its children and how far it lets them alone is one thing with how it views premarital sex; whether it shows them affection is not.** ⚠ This agrees in direction with the weakest link of the previous entry — **affection has been the loosest joint on this axis throughout, and at sexual norms it comes apart entirely** `[#700「管孩子的那条轴够得到性,而够到它的是强制那一端,不是温暖那一端」]`.
- **A seventy-third, recorded UNVERIFIED, and the failure itself is the heaviest information in it: across the sixty-one societies that carry both a condemnation and a rarity reading, this design can no longer resolve anything.** The previous entry measured the axis against condemnation of premarital sex at +0.3631 on seventy-five societies, with a null of 0.2381; to obtain rarity as well, this round's subsample falls to sixty-one, **and there the association drops to +0.2549 while the null rises to 0.2555 — it no longer clears its own null.** ⇒ **The previous entry holds on its own seventy-five societies and is not reproducible on this sixty-one-society subsample; that is not a retraction, since a smaller sample widens the null by necessity, but a scope, and the page row must carry it.** ⚠ The most easily misread number here is the third: **condemnation and rarity, as coded by the same person, correlate +0.8320** — closely resembling E01's person-level 0.758, **and that resemblance is not evidence**: the two variables come from one coding project and are **one rater's two questions about one behaviour**, while earlier rounds measured same-project pairs inflated about 2.14× against a cross-team median of only 0.105. **⇒ A society-level correlation of 0.83 between condemnation and rarity is inseparable from "one person asked two related questions gives consistent answers"; E01's 0.758 was once demoted to "both downstream of condemnation", and here even that demotion cannot be performed — this site has no second coder.** ⚠ **Two numbers look alike, and the reason they look alike may be the same one: a single instrument asked twice** `[#701「在同时能看到「谴责」和「罕见」的那 61 个社会里,这个设计已经分辨不出任何东西」]`.
- **A seventy-fourth, measuring how much eyesight this line has left: the society side sees its own internal shape clearly and can barely see whether that shape relates to anything else.** ⚠ First, an error of my own: the first curve I built measured the wrong estimand — the previous entry's nulls belong to *axis versus an external variable*, while I measured the *axis-internal* statistic, which is not the same quantity; **and half the fault lies in the preregistration itself, whose fourth item named the internal statistic while its first named the external nulls, so following it produced a curve that answered a question nobody asked**, and both are reported after the rerun. **Result: the axis-internal minimum detectable effect falls from 0.170 at n=40 to 0.117 at n=97; while axis-versus-external falls only from 0.342 at n=40 to 0.233 at n=75. The pipeline is validated against the previous entry's numbers — axis-versus-external computes to 0.2581 and 0.2330 at n=61 and n=75, where the previous entry measured 0.2555 and 0.2381, two independent routes agreeing.** ⇒ **On sixty-one societies the axis-versus-external minimum detectable effect is 0.258, and the previous entry's two observations straddle it — this is not "there is no relation", it is "this sample size cannot quite see whether there is one".** ⚠ And the preregistered confound has a harder answer than I expected: **the mean between-block correlation is 0.25–0.26 before shuffling and 0.14–0.17 after, and that post-shuffle value barely shrinks with n. The null's width therefore has two sources, one that shrinks with sample size and one that does not — the latter being a halo floor of about 0.15 that no number of societies removes.** ⇒ **"Find more societies" is closed off at this site; and the archive-level enumeration already showed there is no second instrument, so the society side ends here** `[#702「这条线看得清自己内部的形状,却几乎看不清它和别的东西有没有关系」]`.
- **A seventy-fifth, answering a question nobody had asked: why has E01's relation never been replicated on another instrument? Because only that one instrument asked, for every behaviour, both how many people do it and how shameful it is.** ⚠ First, the previous entry's wording is refuted: it wrote "n = number of people", **but a base rate is a property of an item, not of a person** — a behaviour's prevalence in a sample is the same number for everyone, so that typing is wrong; E01 itself was item-level across sixty-eight categories, **so the correct level is the item, and the item count sets the resolution**. Measured library by library, rather than concluded at the title: **GSS has six condemnation items and four behaviour items but only three that pair; YRBS's 270 variables contain not a single hit for "wrong/approve/should/attitude", so it has zero condemnation items; NSFG's dictionary yields one attitude token; and the two pure attitude scales have no behaviour items at all. Against that, E01's instrument has sixty-eight matched pairs.** ⇒ **Outside it the maximum is three, and statistics over so few points were long ago shown to saturate.** ⇒ **The conclusion is a capability boundary: that 0.758 was measurable because its instrument is unusual; it has never been replicated not for want of trying but because no second instrument has this structure. And that strengthens the earlier demotion rather than weakening it — a relation that cannot be tested elsewhere should stay demoted.** ⚠ Two sentences that may not be merged: **person-side rarity is a sample base rate, society-side rarity is an ethnographer's judgement, and they are not the same construct**; so "redo E01 on the person side" was never a replication but a different construct, and now it cannot even be asked. ⚠ One small standard lesson in passing: a background search I ran first on the same question **matched no files at all in half its scope because the path was wrong, so it returned empty — and an empty return is silence, not a zero** `[#703「只有一具仪器,为每个行为同时问了「多少人做」和「有多丢人」」]`.
- **A seventy-sixth, a closure round with no sentence about people — I will not invent one; the sentence for the reader is that half the conclusions on this page never said, in their own row, which instrument they were read off, and now they do.** ⚠ The first audit was voided on the spot by the positive control I had fixed in advance: routing by **page row text** classified the three-instrument replication as "unknown", so the control failed; **and the reason it failed is the product — thirteen of twenty-seven rows (48%) name no instrument in their own text, which is the page's omission, not a miscount**. Rerouted through the ledger entries (where a gate has compelled instrument naming since a certain round), the control passes. **Result: by archive, the largest single-instrument cluster is six or seven entries, 25–29%, under half ⇒ no single instrument dominates; subdivided by coding project, the largest project cluster inside SCCS is four.** ⚠ **And *cross-instrument* has two readings, both of which are reported**: the gate's own documentation says that merely mentioning another instrument will register, and several entries only mention one in their impossibility register **without having measured anything on it**; **the broad reading gives nine to twelve, the strict reading only four** — agreeing with an earlier tiered audit. ⚠ One more thing to say against myself: **I ran the same audit twice and got different counts** (SCCS seven versus six, broad cross-instrument twelve versus nine), **because the two regexes differed, not because the data did**; this is the same lesson this project keeps relearning — **a grep is a measuring instrument** — so both sets of numbers are in the ledger, and the conclusion is the same under either `[#704「这一页有一半的结论,在它自己那一行里没说清它是从哪具仪器读出来的」]`.
- **A seventy-seventh, a closure round whose product is a distinction rather than an effect: cross-instrument replication makes a conclusion more credible without making it more precise — and this page had been treating those as one thing.** ⚠ Two catches before running, the second voiding the preregistered comparison: **the cross-instrument group actually holds two entries, not four** (the other two have no pairable effect and null); and more importantly, **the estimand I myself wrote in the previous entry measures the wrong thing** — I was taking the third instrument's own correlation against its own null, **and "effect over its own null" measures how precise that measurement is on that instrument, not how much cross-instrument replication hardens a claim; cross-instrument hardness is not a ratio at all but a categorical fact**. ⇒ The comparison is void and was not run. ⚠ The stop check is logged anyway: the random-regrouping null has twenty-one levels against twenty-one total combinations, **so this design's empirical p floor sits at 0.048** — it could only ever be barely significant, once. **The table itself is readable: the most precise measurement on this page is a single-instrument one at 18.46 (GSS), while the two replications on the second and third instruments come in at 1.65 and 5.84 — replications are always looser, because those samples are smaller and their nulls wider.** ⇒ **Credible and precise are two different things; cross-instrument buys the first, and I was conflating them when I wrote that estimand.** ⚠ It also explains something counterintuitive: **the replication that turned a claim from "a number on one instrument" into "three instruments agreeing" has a ratio of just 1.65, barely off its null — a measurement that makes a conclusion credible can itself be very loose** `[#705「跨仪器让一条结论更可信,但没有让它更精确」]`.
- **A seventy-eighth, which takes an old conclusion that never had a null, supplies one, and finds it standing without a digit out of place: within one birth cohort, the more educated a person is, the more their moral judgements do hang together.** Sixteen rows still carry the "null not checkable" mark; measuring each one's re-runnable sample size, the largest is 74,592 people — **and its ledger contains no null at all**: the criterion of the day was "five cells agreeing in sign with a median at least half the pooled value", **which is a ratio, not a test against a null**. So "re-run" here means supplying the null it never had. ⚠ **The positive control reproduces to the digit: the largest absolute difference from the ledger's five cell values is 0.0000**, with a pooled +0.0776 against the ledger's +0.0772, a gap of 0.0004. ⚠ **And the strongest confound, written down in advance, measures to nothing**: the old pipeline's threshold was one hundred and fifty and today's standard is thirty, **and the two conventions return identical cell values and pooled value** ⇒ this conclusion does not rest on that pipeline change. **The null supplied is "shuffle education inside each cohort cell" — preserving the cohort structure and that cell's education marginal while destroying the pairing between education and person; its 95th percentile is 0.0207 with a median of 0.0056, and the observed five-cell median of +0.0718 clears it three and a half times over.** ⇒ That row's mark is removed and its precision ratio goes from "—" to 3.47. ⚠ **And this changes my default posture toward old conclusions: "reported no null then" does not mean "was wrong then", only "could not be checked then"; those sixteen marks are checkability marks, not suspicion marks** — and this is the first time a real re-run has demonstrated the difference `[#709「同一个世代里,读书更多的人道德判断确实更连成一片,而这次它有零了」]`.

- **Four things it established cannot be done at all**, listed below
  `[#498 "这条线按它自己写下的规则关闭", #516 "结构上就是不可能的", #521 "零个第二性域", #522 "没有第二个人问过同一个问题"]`.

Every claim above carries the ledger entry it came from. A check verifies that the anchored entry actually contains the number or phrase cited; it is deliberately breakable and reports an error when an anchor is altered. ⚠ How much a passing anchor is worth was then measured, and the two kinds of anchor are not alike. Over the 509 ledger entries, a **number** anchor is weak — `0.20` appears in **13.4% of the ledger**, so a wrong entry passes that check about one time in seven — while each of the four **phrase** anchors above appears in exactly one entry and collides with **none** of the other 508. So the number anchors were replaced by phrase anchors. ⚠ Neither kind verifies: the check confirms that the cited text is there, never that the entry is *about* this claim, and an anchor pointing at an entry that merely happens to contain the sentence would pass. **Semantic correctness is not machine-checkable here, and this round did not fix that — it put a number on it** `[#526]`.

Nothing here rescues the first half; the fourth item is a reason to trust it less, and the last
"cannot do" says why no external instrument can settle it either.

# What was retracted (kept here because I once reported it)

| What I said | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| "Sexual is a value, not a category" (the epoch title) | **False, and the question was two questions** | Line 1 of the loader deleted the rival; and within a block base rate ≡ content, so that contrast can never adjudicate `#67` `#69` `#70` |
| "Domain-general beats domain-specific, 186/276" | **Inverted: 0 general / 201 specific** | The 186 was a −0.135 estimator handicap I had given the specific side `#82` |
| "The three components are the same size (in predictability), 1.05×" | **Withdrawn** | Subtracting a null does not fix an estimator; it charges the estimator's failure to the estimand `#86` `#87` `#88` |
| "A minority concentrates on rare options" | **Wording withdrawn, existence retained** | It is a symmetric widening, not a subgroup `#99` |
| "The earlier something enters the map, the more central it is" | **Withdrawn, 10% remains** | That was not time, it was "people who rate everything highly" `#115` |
| "The trait tracks earlier acquisition age" | **Withdrawn** | −0.030 against a null of −0.028; the null is 91% of the effect `#102` |
| "The √n accumulation across blocks reflects latent structure" | **Inverted** | At fixed budget, more blocks is worse; it is ordinary √N `#64` |

---

# What this data cannot do (each measured, not guessed)

| Cannot | The measured bound |
|---|---|
| See structure carried by ≤30% of people (variance-explained methods) | 5% carriers at **±50 pp** leave skill on the floor; ≈30% is required `#91` |
| Distinguish "a small group unusually intense about a few things" from "one heavy-tailed continuum" | Margin-preserving concentrated/diffuse plants all sit inside seed jitter at 3000 swaps per block; **the way out is an external anchor, not a stronger plant** `#122` |
| Distinguish memory becoming blurry from a story becoming settled | Real curvature is **0.3× its own spread** — it never cleared resolvability at all, so this is a **capability boundary, not a null**; pushing it to 2× needs about **48× the sample** `#120` |
| Measure the interaction magnitude directly, without a model | **Algebraically impossible** — the residual's first moment is margin-determined and identically zero under a margin-preserving null `#105` |
| Count dimensions | An estimator with an honest floor cannot count; the one that can count has a floor that is an artefact `#89` |
| Recover presentation-order primacy | The release exports multiselect answers **alphabetically** (119 pairs, agreement 1.0000); display order is destroyed `#69` |
| Separate breadth from acquiescence | Needs reverse-keyed or forced-choice items; this release has neither |
| Causal direction | Cross-sectional, and a stripped release |
| **Say what makes some interests carry more shame than their rarity predicts** | Across the 31 onset categories, rarity explains **58%** of how ashamed a category's holders are (`r` = +0.758). The remaining **42% is real** — residual sd **0.145** against a per-category sampling floor of **0.029** (**4.9×**), and a person-shuffle null puts the residual at **0.031 ± 0.006**. But four existing quantities (the category's mean onset age, its onset spread, its holders' mean position score, its holders' mean breadth-type score) give **R² = 0.024** against a permutation null of **0.132 ± 0.083** — **less than chance**, while a planted single-driver residual is recovered at **R² = 0.80**. **We can see that something is there and we cannot measure it.** Descriptively (a description, not a name — five naming attempts have died here): more shame than rarity predicts for `humiliation`, `gender`, `bestiality`, `incest`; less for `vore`, `brutal`, `creepy/creature` (`#338` `#339`). **And the thing is stable**: split the respondents in half twenty times and the two halves' 31-dimensional residual vectors correlate **+0.905 ± 0.042** (Spearman–Brown **0.95**), level with a synthetic category-level true signal (**+0.942**) and far above a person-level noise control (**−0.159 ± 0.229**). ⚠ **Stable is reliability, not validity** — a reproducible category-level quantity can still be a shadow of something else (`#343`). |

**And a transferable methodological result — but a much smaller one than I first published.** Block-count matching takes pornhabit's congruence deficit from 0.2285 → 0.0871 → 0.0439 **by direct measurement on that one split**, and **reverses its ordering against sex**; that correction stands and never depended on a correlation — **though it is a single-split direct measurement whose own precision has never been estimated (`#169`, scope stated rather than promised)**. What does **not** stand is the law I built on top of it: ~~`corr(deficit, coverage gap) = +0.815` over nine splits~~ was **killed by `#20`** — median r over K ∈ {3,5,8,12} is **+0.127**, the worst leave-one-out is **−0.294** (removing pornhabit, at 6 of 8 specifications), and the permutation null at this n is enormous: **|r| p95 = 0.820 at n=6 splits**, so +0.815 never cleared its own null. Sex and modality both carry large deficits at near-zero coverage gaps. **The unit was never people** — all 15,503 respondents appear in every split, but the estimand is a correlation over *splits*, and there are six. ⚠ And "nobody has pointed this hazard out" is **false, killed by `Entry 15`** — the explorer's own `analysis/swarm/14-missingness.md`, **five months earlier**, states that group comparisons on gated columns compare different subpopulations. **The hazard is theirs; one correction on one split is mine; the law was nobody's.** (`#11` `#15` `#20`)

---
## What each big round established (R = one iteration, one belief update, one commit)

**[R01 · the object](A01_object_and_structure)** — The item-level data is not the 68 category
ratings; it is 101 multiselect columns exploding to **1,332 options over 15,468 people**. Entry
to every block is gated on a parent rating: **P(enter | parent > 0) = 0.99**. This is
undocumented and it constrains every design downstream — naive cross-block holdout is
conditioned on liking the parent category. Person-profile split-half reliability **0.727**;
item base-rate reliability **0.999**. The noise injection did not destroy individual signal.

**[R02 · is there a shared grammar](A02_is_there_a_shared_grammar)** — Yes, and the first two
instruments could not see it. PC1-vs-PC1 gave 0.064 (retraction #2); held-out CCA gave 0.272 vs a
0.055 floor; leave-one-block-out gave **32/32 blocks positive**, median gain +0.0340 against a −0.0029 floor. **[BOUNDED — RETRACTIONS #25]** with all 32 coverage indicators in the baseline the gain is +0.0170 and 31/32 blocks stay positive; coverage is partly a mediator, so the honest statement is a bound of **[+0.017, +0.037]**. Factors learned from 31 domains predict a domain they never saw.

**[R03 · naming the coordinates](A03_naming_the_coordinates)** — Four coordinates survive a
**block** split-half (not a person split-half — the question is whether a coordinate is
recoverable from either half of the *domains*). Naming failed twice before succeeding. The
surviving three: *light restraint/toys ↔ insertive extremity and confinement* · *abjection and
filth ↔ blood, burning, weapons* (two **opposed** extremities, not one intensity axis) ·
*receiving a substance ↔ giving it*. None is the folk axis. **"Top/bottom" is one word over
three near-independent coordinates**: who submits, who is seen, who receives — disattenuated
**[DOWNGRADED — RETRACTIONS #24, re-affirmed #143]** "three near-independent axes / effective dimensionality 2.95 of 3" was withdrawn long ago. With a validated reliability ladder (positive control: SUBSTANCE against itself r_true = **+1.018**; sham +0.023), **POWER–SUBSTANCE r_true = +0.605** — they share **37% of variance, not 5%**. The pre-registered kill lands in the middle band (0.45 < 0.605 < 0.70): **UNVERIFIED**, neither one construct nor confirmed distinct. ⚠ **`#321`: and that is now a resolved intermediate, not a failure to measure** — the bootstrap 95% interval is **[+0.561, +0.659]**, entirely inside the band, so the two share **31–43% of variance**; the positive control (SUBSTANCE against disjoint halves of itself) recovers **[+0.943, +1.126]**. **RETRACTIONS #141 is withdrawn in full** — it re-priced the claim by re-running A02/R034 without reading the ledger entry that had already superseded it.

**[R04 · acquisition and time](A04_acquisition_and_time)** — Interests arrive on a
population-shared schedule (content-like early: appearance 14.0, body parts 14.4, clothing 14.7;
relational late: power dynamics 16.8, bondage 16.9, mental alteration 17.0). **[SURVIVES FRAMING SWAP — RETRACTIONS #50]** Within-person "acquired together" tracks "liked together" at RSA **+0.599**, and unlike the cross-domain grammar (#49) it carries predictable variance: person-level onset→preference R² = +0.0136 against a −0.0043 null, 31% of the same-domain ceiling after stripping intensity leakage ⚠ **`#358`: the schedule now has its own evidence, and a ceiling.** The 31 category means are resolved — span **3.37 y** against a mean bootstrap interval of **0.34 y**, with only **87 of 465 pairs overlapping (19%)** — and the ordering is **not** a rarity artifact (`corr(mean onset, category rarity)` = **+0.064**, stable at +0.03…+0.09 across three numeric codings of the age bins). Its order reproduces at Spearman **ρ = 0.805** between the sexes and **0.87–0.93** across current-age bands, against a random-split ceiling of **0.966** and a within-person label-shuffle floor of **−0.169**. **Shared, but not identical: the male–female 0.805 sits well below the 0.966 a random split gives.**
(−0.126 — **[VERIFICATION — see RETRACTIONS #16]**, published as `01-age-onset` Finding 1) and recall anchoring, and after near-synonymy is excluded (0.594 for
280 pairs sharing no content word vs 0.646 for 64 that do). **[CONFIRMED — RETRACTIONS #22]** onset carries structure preference does not: observed top residual eigenvalue 0.959 against a purpose-built rival world at 0.441 ± 0.040 (95% upper 0.532), positive control fires at injection 0.3 and stays silent at 0.0. The attached '80% of the sd' is descriptive, not tested — interests a person does *not* like together were acquired at the
same time. But the organising variable is arrival time, not coordinate membership (retraction #9).

**[R05 · group differences and the instrument](A05_group_differences_and_the_instrument)** —
**One split's group comparison on this release was partly measuring survey coverage**: block-count matching took pornhabit from 0.2285 → 0.0871 → 0.0439 and **reversed its ordering against sex** — measured directly on that split, and it stands. **[KILLED — RETRACTIONS #20]** the law I built on it — ~~corr(congruence deficit, coverage gap) = +0.815 across nine splits~~ — does not: median r over K ∈ {3,5,8,12} = +0.127, worst leave-one-out = −0.294 on removing pornhabit, and the permutation null has **|r| p95 = 0.820 at n=6 splits**, which +0.815 never cleared. The estimand was a correlation over *splits*, not over the 15,503 people who appear in every one of them.
**[CORRECTED — RETRACTIONS #15]** the sentence that used to follow — "not documented anywhere I have
read about this dataset" — **is false**: the explorer's own `analysis/swarm/14-missingness.md`, five
months earlier, states that group comparisons on gated columns compare different subpopulations.
**The hazard is theirs; one correction on one split is mine; the law was nobody's (`#20`).** Drawn-vs-live-action consumers — whose content contains no real bodies — differ by **0.0204 ± 0.0265**, bounded below sex-sized (0.093). **[UNVERIFIED — RETRACTIONS #34]** the written-vs-visual deficit is unresolvable at 5 seeds (0 of 9 corpus cuts have effect > 2× seed spread, while sex is resolvable in 9/9); ~44 seeds per cell would be needed. That is a real, bounded constraint on A.

**[R06 · induction](A06_induction)** — the retrospective induction claim, its two absent signatures and the breadth it actually tracks are stated once in the standing-claims table above; this section adds only what the table does not carry. **[CORRECTED — RETRACTIONS #26, caught by #144]** an earlier version of this page reported "rho +0.2515, 85% surviving response-style control" — that pair **appears nowhere in the ledger** and came from a pre-A09 draft of this page. The "85% is response style" step is **UNVERIFIED**: every item is erotic content with no reverse-keying, so general agreeableness and general erotic endorsement are not separable in this release. **Design consequence**: retrospective self-report of induction is unusable as an outcome measure — phase 3 must be prospective.

**[R07 · breadth](A07_breadth)** — Quantity without shape. A person's set is only **0.88%** more
concentrated in coordinate space than a size-matched base-rate set — negligible. ⚠ **That figure was computed at one setting (K=6) and never swept**, and the ledger's own sweep of the same quantity runs from −0.01% at K=2 to −2.56% at K=16, giving −0.29% at the K where 0.88% was reported; the t-value once printed alongside it has no surviving source in this repository. Sets are **24.2%** of the way from chance to perfect nesting. Breadth is moderately one trait (Spearman–Brown 0.557). **[UNVERIFIED — RETRACTIONS #26]** the '9–13% response style' figure comes from an index that is orthogonal to the POWER axis (+0.024) but correlates +0.385 with breadth itself; response style and erotic endorsement cannot be separated without balanced-keyed items, which this release lacks.

**[E01·R05 · is breadth the object](E01_sexual_as_a_value_not_a_category/A05_is_breadth_a_nuisance_or_the_object)** — Nearly domain-encapsulated. All 15 non-sexual
variables jointly: **R² = 0.012**. **[PARTLY RETRACTED — see RETRACTIONS #17]** That held only with survey progression controlled and a *gated* outcome. On the ungated `totalfetishcategory` with acquiescence controlled, childhood adversity → breadth is **r = +0.059, effect/floor 7.5** — real and small, matching the published d=0.151. Adult sexual assault, corporal punishment and upbringing remain near zero.

**[R09 · consumption](A09_consumption)** — Consumption touches both terms: it correlates with θ
(rho 0.17) *and* independently with the coordinates (**0.0439** after matching coverage, breadth
and sex — 3.6× the neuroticism reference). Direction is unavailable; this is exactly what a
prospective design would resolve.

**[R10 · additivity](A10_additivity)** — Ivan's model B requires it. The feature crosses every
substance boundary it meets (+0.24 to +0.58) **except source gender, where it is +0.017**.
Additivity is basis-dependent: in the folk basis (self/other) it fails; in a basis crossing role
with source gender it may hold. Small n on the decisive cells (3–4 pairs) — **PLAUSIBLE, not
CONFIRMED**.

---

## How the shared signal accumulates

**[RETRACTIONS #60]** `increment = 0.00723 × √(source domains)` — CV **6.4%** across n=1…31, against 11.2%
for log and 51.9% for linear (`#60`), with a permuted-label null of exactly 0.0000 at every n. **[MECHANISM REVERSED — #61, #64]** blocks are *not* interchangeable (subset variance 58× seed variance), and at
**fixed total respondent-rows more blocks is worse**: n=8 gives 0.0042, n=16 gives 0.0028, n=31 gives 0.0025.
The accumulation tracks **total sample**, not block count — the √ shape is ordinary √N. Block boundaries are a
tax on the estimate, not a source of it. Price list for new collection:
0.06 needs 69 domains, 0.08 needs 122, 0.10 needs 191 — extrapolated beyond the measured range.

## Within-block vs cross-domain

**[RETRACTIONS #59]** Two largely independent contributions, 87% additive, both against two exactly-null
controls: **cross-block factors +0.0409** (fitted on 31 *other* blocks) and **within-block structure
+0.0290**, combined **+0.0606** (`#59`). The aggregate cross-domain signal is the *larger* — pairwise block→block
is ~0 (#49), and it accumulates across blocks.

## The item margin

**[RETRACTIONS #57]** Fifty-seven rounds measured structure over PEOPLE. The ITEM side, measured once:
item-neighbour structure recovers **+0.0206** held-out R² over a marginals base, against the person
factors' **+0.0289** and a random-neighbour control's **+0.0006** — **71% of the person margin**, on
578,989 held-out cells. **[#58]** Fitted jointly, the two are **67% the same structure**: combined increment +0.0359 against the person margin's +0.0293, so the item side's unique contribution is +0.0066 — ratio 1.65, **below resolution**.

## Scope table for the second half

Every assertion the second half added, with the unit it holds at, the instrument it routes through, the population it was measured on, and the thing it could **not** separate. A claim without all five is a claim without a boundary. Anchors are phrases rather than numbers, because a cited number collides with 13.4% of this project's ledger while a cited phrase collides with none.
