| Assertion | Unit | Instrument | Population | Figure | Not separated |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rarer is more condemned — in one of five cells `[#528「不是关于做法的定律」]` | society | SCCS / Broude & Greene 1976 | 186 societies, 34–97 coded | ρ +0.80 (premarital, n=97); −0.37 to +0.21 elsewhere, all invisible | one coding team; same-source pairs inflated 2.14× |
| No "strict society" `[#529「最有力的那一对,几乎是零」]` | society | same | 26–90 societies per pair | ρ +0.086 at n=90; grid median +0.125 | same team; 6 of 8 cells have MDE ≈ 0.4–0.58 |
| …and that is *indistinguishable from*, not *below*, the instrument's own coupling `[#530「不可分辨,不是低于」]` | society | same | 22 cross-practice pairs | baseline +0.187 ± 0.152 | whether condemnation scales couple like other scales |
| The practice assignment is not load-bearing `[#531「指派不是承重墙」]` | society | same | 3 schemes, one a no-op | baseline range 0.004 | only two schemes actually differed |
| …and that "indistinguishable" cannot be sharpened `[#573「余量不存在」]` | society | same | 18 cross-practice pairs, the maximum available | baseline +0.195, interval [0.112, 0.364] | the instrument holds about eleven usable non-target ordinal variables in total; ⚠ and at this pool size the baseline **does** move with how variables are sorted into practices — range **0.038** across three sortings, where the same test on the larger pool gave 0.004 `[#574「稳健性是池大小的函数」]` |
| Attitudes soften one at a time, not as a tide `[#532「一件一件松开」]` | decade | GSS 1972–2024 | 21–30 shared survey years | cross-topic 41% of within-topic | not separable from noise; disjoint respondents still share a country |
| …and a longer window stops being a test `[#533「长滞后不是分离器」]` | decade | same | 6–18 year windows | ratio 0.44 → 0.52 → 0.76 | trend and synchrony predict the same rise |
| One ruler at the person level `[#534「三个单位,三个答案」]` | person | GSS, within year | 30 survey years | 0.375 vs 0.093, ceiling 0.437 | domain vs question format |
| …of which format explains 13–45% `[#535「格式解释掉一部分」]` | person | same | same | excess +0.15 to +0.27 | domain content; no four-point non-sexual moral battery exists here |
| Sexual morality is tighter than family morality `[#536「性比家庭更紧」]` | person | NSFG 2011–13, interviewer-administered ⚠ **CORRECTED** | 5,600 women | gap **+0.198 to +0.285** across three sortings ⚠ **was a point estimate** | ⚠ **[the "only three pairs" caveat repeated five times was never measured; measured, it is 1.4×]** the three-pair side resolves to **0.034** against the twenty-one-pair side's **0.025**, and the gap is **8× its own resolution** `[#581「引了五次的限制,量出来是 1.4 倍」]`; and which items count as sexual moves the size by a third |
| Already formed at 15; family morality still tightening at 44 `[#539「一个在十五岁就已经成形」]` | person × age | same | six age bands, 600–1,221 each | flat vs 0.124 → 0.160 | age and birth cohort are perfectly collinear |
| Men sit close to women `[#540「波之间做不到」]` | person × sex | NSFG 2017–19 | 5,177 men, 6,115 women | 0.473 vs 0.529, resolution 0.045 | the same pair moved +0.203 in six years while its module shrank from ten items to two |
| "One block" is scale-dependent — and this row is a verification `[#542「面内高于面间」]` | person × facet | MSSCQ, self-selected online | **12,789 respondents | within-facet 0.579 vs between 0.173 | the facets are the scale author's design; items inside one are paraphrases |

**What every row shares, and what therefore cannot be fixed by adding rows:** each instrument is missing a different half. No public instrument asks both what people want and what they are ashamed of; the only source that codes both a practice's acceptability and its frequency is coded by one team; the one battery with a matched non-sexual comparison drops eight of its ten items in the following wave; the largest sexual self-concept battery has no non-sexual items at all; and one authoritarianism dataset ships no item texts, so its questions cannot be classified without inventing what they said.

## What an instrument would have to be

The five things this work established it cannot do are not sample-size problems; each is a property of how
the instrument was built. Written as specifications rather than complaints, they are short:

| What cannot be done | What an instrument would have to do | Closest thing that exists | The gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| No public survey asks both what people want and what they are ashamed of | Ask the same people, about the same list of practices, both questions — presented separately, order randomised | The kink survey this project began with: both, but self-selected, once, one community | A sampling frame, and a second wave |
| The only source coding both a practice's acceptability and its frequency is coded by one team | Two teams who never speak, coding the same ethnographies independently, publishing per-record coder identity and agreement | The Standard Cross-Cultural Sample, 1976 | A second set of coders |
| No four-point non-sexual moral battery exists beside the sexual one | Sexual and non-sexual moral items sharing one scale length and one answer format, where the non-sexual items are themselves a domain | The NSFG self-administered block: five-point, three sexual and seven family | Keeping all ten in the next wave |
| The one battery with a matched comparison lost eight of its ten items in the following wave | Keep the same items across waves, or publish which were cut and why | NSFG 2011–13 → 2017–19 | Item stability across waves |
| Attitudes over decades rest on one source | A second series: same question, fresh sample, fixed interval, twenty years or more, public microdata | The General Social Survey, 1972–2024 | A second series like it |
| Every attitude here was spoken aloud to an interviewer | Ask the same people the same attitude items **twice — once with someone present, once alone** — and report, before any effect, the disagreement rate on a fact that carries no shame | The NSFG asks abortion both ways, but only as behaviour, never as attitude | The same double-asking, applied to the attitude items |
| Nobody was ever asked twice, so "people changed" and "people were replaced" can never be separated | The same people, the same items, **at least two points in time**, with a person identifier released so the waves can be matched | **Nothing here** — the cumulative file was checked by construction, not by keyword: every (year, id) pair is unique across all 75,699 rows, and respondent #1's age runs 23, 54, 21, 38, 56 down the years | The entire panel dimension `[#595「按构造验出来的没有」]` |

One of those five rows was then attacked with the data already here, on the theory that the ethnographies' focal years could stand in for the missing second set of coders: if the coding recorded *when the observer wrote* rather than what the society did, the codes would track that year. They do not, and more importantly **nothing does**: once latitude and longitude are partialled out, the development variables that must depend on era correlate with the focal year at 0.110, and the median variable in the whole dataset correlates at 0.111 `[#561「偏掉地理之后,正对照掉到参照之下」]`. **The era signal is not weak here, it is absent** — in a sample where societies cluster in space and space is entangled with who visited when, geography and history cannot be separated at all. So that row of the specification stands: it needs a second set of coders, and no rearrangement of the existing codes substitutes for them.

⚠ One more thing about this page, which is about the page rather than the subject: **five audit tools already existed in this repository before four more were built beside them**, and one of the five opens by saying it must be run before committing any round that touches this file. Fifteen rounds touched this file without it being run once. Run at last, it **blocks**: eighteen findings, none of them introduced by those fifteen rounds — they have been sitting here across sessions, flagged by a check nobody executed `[#563「又造了四件工具,一件都没先查过」]`. The anchor check has now been folded into that gate as a fifth rule rather than shipped as a tenth tool, and it fails on a tampered anchor and passes on a clean one. ⚠ It has since been split by measured precision: the half that flags an anchor pointing at **text that does not exist** has caught **2 real defects out of 2 firings**; the half that flagged an anchor whose phrase also appears elsewhere fired **3 times and was wrong all 3** — every one was the ledger legitimately quoting the entry the anchor points at, which breaks the anchor while the page is untouched. **Citing something is a kind of writing**, so that half now warns instead of blocking `[#572「引用是一种写入」]`. **The eighteen are recorded as debt, not silently cleared**, because a gate that gets relaxed the first time it says no is not a gate.

⚠ And a sixth row belongs in it, found by scanning for a third way of asking: in this whole collection, **what a person thinks about sexual practices has only ever been measured one way** — spoken aloud to an interviewer. The school-based survey asks 86 questions and **none of them is an attitude**; the online battery's hundred items are about the respondent's own sexuality, not about whether a practice is wrong; the telephone survey ships no item labels at all, so it can only be recorded as undecidable `[#567「态度只被两种模式量过」]`. ⚠ And sharper still, once the dictionary was actually read: **there are not two modes, there is one.** The block of attitude items sits *before* the question that sets the language for the self-interview, and the sections after it are the ones whose variables carry `casi` — so those eleven items were read out loud by an interviewer, exactly as the face-to-face survey's are `[#568「不是两种模式,是一种」]`. Every person-level finding on this page therefore rests on **answers a person gave out loud to someone sitting with them**. What the same person would type alone is *almost* not measured anywhere — with one exception, and it turns out to bound the question rather than answer it. The same survey asks about abortions twice: once through the interviewer's pregnancy history, once in a self-administered block. A woman whose interviewer-reported lifetime count is zero cannot have had one in the last five years, so that contradiction rate is a direct read on what the two ways of asking do differently: **2.5%** (n = 2,702). But the same contradiction computed for **live births** — a fact carrying no shame at all — is **1.6%** (n = 2,427), and the gap of 0.9 points sits inside its own resampling spread of 1.4 `[#569「地板是 1.6%」]`. ⚠ So the honest result is a **resolution, not an effect**: these two instruments already disagree about an unembarrassing fact once in every sixty-two women, and any disclosure difference smaller than that is invisible here. The question of what people say only when nobody is listening stays open, and this design cannot close it.

⚠ Read as seven rows the table looks like seven independent holes. Sorted mechanically — two specs are incompatible only if they need a different unit of observation (a person or a society) or different raw material (new interviews or existing ethnographies) — **it collapses to two**: six of the seven are satisfied by *one* properly designed longitudinal survey of people, and the seventh, a second team coding the same ethnographies, is about societies and shares nothing with it `[#596「七行其实是两件事」]`. **So almost everything this work could not establish about people comes down to a single survey nobody ran.** ⚠ The sorting rule has no time in it, so "one collection" hides that one of those six — a second fifty-year series — takes decades: the answer 2 counts *kinds* of collection, not years. ⚠ Pushed one step further — do those six make **conflicting** demands on the one survey? — the answer is no: of fifteen pairs, **none conflicts**, and **two need an extra part** `[#597「冲突零,而两对要多长一个部件」]`. So the instrument can be written down, and it is short: **one repeated survey of a probability sample, carrying a fixed battery of sexual and non-sexual moral items, with two sampling arms — a fresh cross-section each wave and a panel followed across waves — and every attitude item asked twice, aloud and self-administered, from the first wave onward.** The two parts are not optional: the arms are what separate *people changed* from *people were replaced*, and the double-asking has to be there at the start because introducing it later would itself break the item stability the other rows require. **Everything this work could not learn about people is one survey, and the reason it does not exist is that each of those parts had to be decided before anyone knew it would be needed. ⚠ Broken into six components and checked against every source here — each cell required to name the finding that established it, and marked undecided where none exists — **the best-covered source reaches 3 of 6**, and **two components are verifiably absent from all of them: the panel arm and the double-asking** `[#598「不可选的那两个部件,恰好一份也没有」]`. Those are precisely the two that the structural check had already singled out as **not optional**. ⚠ A third component, a probability sampling frame, also shows zero — but that zero means *never verified inside this project*, not *absent*: the sampling design of these surveys was never checked here against the object, and the table refuses to record an impression as a fact. ⚠ One process rule on this project had been written down twice and broken twice — *edit the page and write the ledger in separate steps, and if the page edit fails, do not write the ledger* — so it is now a check rather than a resolution: **from entry 600 onward, a ledger entry must either appear as an anchor on this page or say in so many words that it was not meant to** `[#600「一条只靠记性执行的规矩,它的失效是默认状态」]`. ⚠ The first version of that check could not catch the failure that prompted it, and was thrown away: it looked for entries *claiming* a page edit, and the entry in question never claimed one — it simply assumed it. The version that runs inverts the default instead, and was kept only after it was shown to catch that entry. ⚠ That check, and the nine others this project has built, all had the same defect: **nothing ever ran them except me remembering to** `[#601「十件工具六条规则,而没有一件东西会自己跑」]`. They are now attached to the point where a commit is made, and what that attachment can see was measured rather than assumed: it catches an uncited number added to the English page, an anchor deleted from it, and an anchor pointing at an entry that does not exist — and **it is blind to an uncited number added to the Chinese page, and to an anchor deleted from it.** This page is published in two languages and the guard covers one and a half of them. That has been fixed, and fixing it found something: two of the four rules had the English page's name written into them as a default, so the Chinese page had never been scanned at all — and when it finally was, **eight numbers on it turned out to carry no source** `[#602「一把只量了半个交付物的尺子,它报的合格是一句关于它自己的话」]`. They had been there the whole time, on a published page, in front of anyone who reads Chinese. Nothing had gone wrong; nothing had ever looked. The count this page's guard reports therefore rose from nine to seventeen the moment the instrument stopped being half-blind, and **that is not the page getting worse — it is the number finally being about the page rather than about the ruler.** ⚠ **And that sentence was itself wrong within the hour** `[#603「同一批债被数了两遍,而我把它写成了另有八笔债」]`: every one of those eight paragraphs turned out to be the *same* claim that was already uncited in English. The debt did not double when the instrument stopped being half-blind — **the counting did.** There are **nine** distinct claims on this page carrying numbers with no source, eight of which are simply counted once in each language. The guard still counts instances, because a claim has to be repaired in both languages and repairing one is not repairing it; but the number quoted about the page is now the number of claims. The first of the nine has been paid, and paying it produced the sharper result `[#604「一个覆盖不是一个出处,而账本里早就写着正确答案」]`: the numbers in these paragraphs do exist in the ledger — **fifty-five of fifty-six of them** — but scattered across different entries, so no single entry is the source and a search that picks the entry covering the most of them picks confidently and wrongly. For the one repaired here it proposed entry 526, which is the entry recording that this page's anchors had *already* been pointed at the wrong entries once; the correct pair was written down in entry 524 the day the question was first asked. **The provenance was not missing. It was recorded, and never read.** ⚠ One round later the guard refused a commit by the person who wrote it `[#605「我给棘轮定基线的那一刻,页面正处在它最差的状态」]`, and it was right to: the baseline it measures itself against had been frozen in the seconds between this page citing an entry and that entry being written, so the worst moment the page ever passes through had been recorded as its normal condition. **A standard is a photograph, and it inherits whatever the shutter happened to catch.** ⚠ The same question asked of this project's own filing produced the sharpest version of it `[#606「我把每一次信念更新都叫成了一个决定,于是我有 263 个决定和 0 个弧」]`: rounds here are grouped into arcs, an arc is supposed to close when a decision becomes safe, and **123 of the 263 arcs contained exactly one round** — which by the rule that defines them means they were never arcs at all. Measured against a sibling project built to the same standard, its arcs hold about thirty rounds each and this one held two. The 635 rounds are unchanged and renumbered nothing; they now sit in **sixteen** arcs, and what changed is not the work but **what this project was willing to call a decision.** ⚠ The first attempt to prove the guard worked proved nothing twice over: the commit it blocked was blocked by an unrelated rule about commit messages, and the violation it was given to detect was one the guard cannot see, so its silence and its absence looked identical.**

**Nothing in that table is expensive to describe and every row is expensive to build**, which is the actual
finding of the second half: the limit on this question is not what has been analysed, it is what was ever
collected, and by whom, and whether anyone kept asking.


### How one sentence got smaller six times

The claim below is the only one in this project that survived every attack made on it, and it is worth
reading as a sequence rather than a result, because **each version was killed by a test, not replaced by a
better phrase**. What is named at each step is the test, not the conclusion.

| # `[#594「六步,每一步都被一个检验杀掉」]` | The sentence at that moment | What killed it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sexual attitudes are a bridge: they reach further into other moral judgements than those judgements reach each other (0.192 vs 0.122) | — |
| 2 | **Swap the comparison group** for three domains spanning loose-to-tight | The cross figure barely moved (0.194 · 0.251 · 0.261) while the comparison group's internal coherence moved four times as far (0.125 · 0.435 · 0.545). **No bridge — the ratio was the denominator.** |
| 3 | Then it is a constant: sexual position predicts any other moral position at ~0.19–0.26, indifferent to which | — |
| 4 | **Partial out the two explanations that would make it dull** — political self-rating, and the habit of picking scale end-points | They account for **12%** and **28%**; the link survives at 0.15–0.21 against a placebo of 0.008. **Not explained.** |
| 5 | **Drop each item in turn; then plot it by year** | No item carries it (max 15.7%), and it is **not a constant**: 0.227 in 1972 → 0.301 in 2024. **It is a process.** |
| 6 | **Split by age band × birth cohort; then re-band three ways** | Both margins rise (+0.0019 per year of age, +0.0016 per year of birth) and **cannot be told apart** — age plus birth year *is* survey year. Re-banding leaves both signs intact; the cohort margin is the steadier of the two (spread 0.0004 against the age margin's 0.0005, and a placebo that collapses to 1% rather than 26%). |

**Where it ends:** how a person judges sex has become a steadily better predictor of how she judges the
death penalty, marijuana and abortion, across fifty-two years; the tightening appears at both the older
and the later-born end; nothing here says which, and nothing here can, because no one in this survey was
ever asked twice `[#594「六步,每一步都被一个检验杀掉」]`.

## Tools: six edges

| tool | what it checks |
|---|---|
| `tools/readme_ledger_audit.py` | README → ledger (has a number here been withdrawn by a later entry; is any number uncited) |
| `tools/round_status.py` | round → ledger (current status of the claims this round supports; has a sibling round superseded it) |
| `tools/guard_lint.py` | round → guards (were the applicable guards called; is a verdict threshold a hard-coded literal) |
| `tools/repro_audit.py` | ledger → round (does the ledger's number still come out; **is a value the ledger condemned still being printed**) |
| `guard_lint.error_bar_scan()` | artifact → precision (**does the real arm have any source of jitter at all**, `#168`) |
| `lib/gates.py` | 10 guards + the `Gate` comparison rules every round attacks itself with |

Each tool carries its own `P6` proxy ledger: **readable in the hit direction only; the output is a
must-read list, not a verdict.**

## Governance

| file | what it holds |
|---|---|
| [`RETRACTIONS.md`](RETRACTIONS.md) | **486 entries.** Every claim killed, scoped or corrected, with what killed it. Twelve are a later round of mine destroying an earlier round of mine |
| [`FROZEN.md`](FROZEN.md) | Lines where further computation cannot identify what it is measuring, each with its unfreeze condition |
| [`PREREGISTRATION.md`](PREREGISTRATION.md) | What the next rounds will test, with thresholds fixed in advance. Rounds r01–r52 were **not** preregistered and are labelled exploratory throughout |
| [`ADVERSARY_FORECAST.md`](ADVERSARY_FORECAST.md) | What I expect an outside challenger to overturn, written before one arrives |
| [`STANDARD_AUDIT.md`](STANDARD_AUDIT.md) | These 52 rounds scored against the campaign standard, including everything they fail |

## Layout

**E · A · R** — an *epoch* closes when the ontology shifts (the object turned out to be a different
object), an *arc* closes when a decision becomes safe, a *round* closes on one belief update.
**3 epochs, 18 arcs, 100 rounds** — one round is one claim on this page, and the scripts that produced it sit flat inside its folder (635 of them across the project). Rounds were regrouped from 685 one-script folders on 2026-08-06; the count is discovered, not chosen.
restarted per arc. Every count is discovered, never chosen; see `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` §P16.

Each round is a directory with `run.py`, `README.md` and `results/`.
`lib/rounds.py` maps a round name to its path, because several rounds reuse an earlier round's
loaders and that dependency is made explicit rather than hidden. Environment is a self-contained
`.venv` (system python 3.14 has no `ensurepip`; pip was bootstrapped).

## Scope, stated once

Cross-sectional · one instrument · one population (18–32, US/Canada/Europe) · aggressively
anonymised, with correlations attenuated roughly 25% by design · every measure self-report.
No causal claim in this repository is identified, and none is made.



</details>
